On the way, Almah said, “Do not use the *sepet-ram* again. You can do no good with it. You must not make it common. Keep it. The time may come when you will need it: you are not fond of death.”

I shuddered.

“Never forget,” she said, “that here death is considered the chief blessing. It is useless for you to interfere in their ways. You cannot change them.”

Some more *joms* passed. The bodies were embalmed, and Almah had more victims to crown with garlands in the horrible *cheder nebilin*.

===Chinese===
归途上，阿尔玛说：「再不要用那塞佩特-拉姆了。你用它成不了什么善事。万不可教它沦为寻常之物。好好收着。说不定会有那么一天，你还用得着它：你并不喜爱死亡。」

我打了个寒噤。

「永远不要忘记，」她说，「在这里，死亡被奉为至高的福分。你去干预他们的行事，是没有用的。你改变不了他们。」

又过了几个约姆。尸身都做了防腐，于是在那可怖的赫德尔·内比林（亡者之厅）里，阿尔玛又有了更多受祭者，等着她为他们戴上花环。

## XII The Baleful Sacrifice / 十二、凶祭

===Original===
I resolved to go on no more sacred hunts. I was sickened at the horrible cruelty, the needless slaughter, the mad self-sacrifice which distinguished them. I was overwhelmed with horror at the merciless destruction of brave comrades, whose wounds, so gallantly received, should have been enough to inspire pity even in a heart of stone. The gentleness, the incessant kindness, the matchless generosity of these people seemed all a mockery. What availed it all when the same hand that heaped favors upon me, the guest, could deal death without compunction upon friends and relatives? It seemed quite possible for the Kohen to kill his own child, or cut the throat of his wife, if the humor seized him. And how long could I hope to be spared among a people who had this insane thirst for blood?

Some more *joms* had passed, and the light season had almost ended. The sun had been sinking lower and lower. The time had at last come when only a portion of his disk would be visible for a little while above the hills, and then he would be seen no more for six months of our time. This was the dark season, and, as I had already learned, its advent was always hailed with joy and celebrated with solemn services, for the dark season freed them from their long confinement, permitted them to go abroad, to travel by sea and land, to carry on their great works, to indulge in all their most important labors and favorite amusements. The Kohen asked me to be present at the great festival, and I gladly consented. There seemed to be nothing in this that could be repellent. As I was anxious to witness some of their purely religious ceremonies, I wished to go. When I told Almah, she looked sad, but said nothing. I wondered at this, and asked her if she was going. She informed me that she would have to go, whereupon I assured her that this was an additional reason why I should go.

I went with Almah. The Kohen attended us with his usual kind and gracious consideration. It seemed almost as though he was our servant. He took us to a place where we could be seated, although all the others were standing. Almah wished to refuse, but I prevailed upon her to sit down, and she did so.

===Chinese===
我打定主意，从此再不去赴什么神圣狩猎了。那里面特有的酷虐、无谓的屠戮和疯狂的自我牺牲，早已叫我深恶痛绝。那些勇敢的伙伴惨遭无情戕害，我惊怖得无以复加——他们的伤，原是何等慷慨豪勇地受来的，纵使铁石心肠，也当为之动容。这些人的温良、无微不至的慈惠、举世无双的慷慨，此刻看来全都成了一场嘲弄。同一只手，方才还把恩宠堆到我这个客人身上，转眼便能毫无愧怍地向亲戚朋友下毒手——这一切又算得了什么呢？这位科恩（Kohen），只要一时兴起，杀掉自己的亲生孩子、割断自己妻子的喉咙，看来都是全然做得出来的。而身在这样一个嗜血成狂的民族中间，我还能指望苟全多久呢？

又过了几个约姆（jom），光明季几乎到头了。太阳一天沉似一天。终于到了这样的时节：只余日轮的一角，还能在山峦之上短暂地露一露脸；此后按我们的历法计算，将有六个月再也见不到它。这便是黑暗季。我早已得知，黑暗季的降临，他们向来欢欣相迎，并以庄严的祭典庆贺，因为黑暗季把他们从漫长的蛰居中解脱出来，容许他们出外走动，跋涉于海上陆地，营办各项大工程，纵情从事种种最紧要的劳作和最心爱的游乐。科恩邀我亲临这场盛大节典，我欣然应允。此事看来并没有什么可嫌可怖的地方。我正渴望见识他们纯乎宗教性的仪节，所以颇愿意去。我把这话告诉阿尔玛时，她面有戚容，却一言不发。我对此颇觉纳罕，便问她去不去。她告诉我，她不得不去；我便向她担保：如此一来，我更该去了。

我同阿尔玛一起去了。科恩照旧以他那一贯殷勤优渥的关照随侍着我们，那光景简直就像是我们的仆人。他把我们领到一处可以落座的地方，尽管其余的人都伫立着。阿尔玛本要推辞，却被我说服，坐了下来。

===Original===
The scene was upon the semicircular terrace in front of the cavern, and we were seated upon a stone platform beside the chief portal. A vast crowd was gathered in front. Before us arose the half-pyramid of which I have already spoken. The light was faint. It came from the disk of the sun, which was partly visible over the icy crest of the distant mountains. Far away the sea was visible, rising high over the tops of the trees, while overhead the brighter stars were plainly discernible.

The Kohen ascended the pyramid, and others followed. At the base there was a crowd of men, with emaciated forms and faces, and coarse, squalid attire, who looked like the most abject paupers, and seemed the lowest in the land. As the Kohen reached the summit there arose a strange sound﻿—a mournful, plaintive chant, which seemed to be sung chiefly by the paupers at the base of the pyramid. The words of this chant I could not make out, but the melancholy strain affected me in spite of myself. There was no particular tune, and nothing like harmony; but the effect of so many voices uniting in this strain was very powerful and altogether indescribable. In the midst of this I saw the crowd parting asunder so as to make way for something; and through the passage thus formed I saw a number of youths in long robes, who advanced to the pyramid, singing as they went. Then they ascended the steps, two by two, still singing, and at length reached the summit, where they arranged themselves in order. There were thirty of them and they arranged themselves in three rows of ten each, and as they stood they never ceased to sing, while the paupers below joined in the strain.

And now the sun was almost hidden, and there was only the faintest line from the upper edge of his disk perceptible over the icy mountaintops. The light was a softened twilight glow. It was to be the last sight of the sun for six months, and this was the spectacle upon which he threw his parting beam. So the sun passed away, and then there came the beginning of the long dark season. At first, however, there was rather twilight than darkness, and this twilight continued long. All this only served to heighten the effect of this striking scene; and as the light faded away, I looked with increasing curiosity upon the group at the top of the pyramid. Almah was silent. I half turned, and said something to her about the beauty of the view. She said nothing, but looked at me with such an expression that I was filled with amazement. I saw in her face something like a dreadful anticipation﻿—something that spoke of coming evil. The feeling was communicated to me, and I turned my eyes back to the group on the pyramid with vague fears in my soul.

===Chinese===
典礼的场地，设在洞窟前那半环形的梯台上；我们的座位在正门旁边的一座石台上。前方聚着黑压压的一大群人。我们面前耸立着我已经说过的那座半金字塔。天光微茫，来自远处群山冰脊之上半隐半现的日轮。极目望去，远处的大海遥遥在望，海面高过树梢；头顶上空，较亮的星辰已清晰可辨。

科恩登上金字塔，另有数人跟随。塔基四周围着一群男子，个个形销骨立，面容枯槁，衣衫粗陋褴褛，望去便是最卑贱的赤贫者，仿佛是全国最底层的人。科恩行至塔顶时，响起了一种奇异的声音——一支哀婉凄切的歌吟，唱的人似乎主要是塔基下的那些赤贫者。歌中的字句我听不真切，但那忧郁的调子不由分说地感染了我。它没有一定的曲调，也谈不上什么和声；然而这么多嗓音汇合在这一个调门里，那效果极是慑人，全然非言语所能形容。正唱之间，我看见人群向两旁分开，让出一条通路；从这条通路里走来一队身披长袍的少年，一路唱着，向金字塔走去。随后他们两人一排，歌声不断，拾级而上，终于抵达塔顶，在那里依次排好。他们共是三十人，排成三行，每行十人；站立之间，歌声一刻不停，塔下的赤贫者也应和着加入这一调门。

这时太阳几乎隐没了，冰封的山巅之上，只余日轮上缘一线微芒，依稀可辨。天色是一片柔和的暮霭般的辉光。这将是六个月里太阳最后一现，而眼前这一幕，正是它洒下临别余晖的所在。太阳就这样逝去，漫长的黑暗季由此发端。不过起初与其说是黑暗，不如说是暮色，而这暮色延续了许久。这一切，只是使这动人心魄的场面愈见森然；随着天光渐渐消隐，我怀着愈来愈盛的好奇，注视着塔顶那一群人。阿尔玛默然无语。我侧过半身，对她说起眼前景致之美。她一言不发，却用那样一种神色望着我，叫我满心诧异。我在她脸上看出一种近乎可怖的预感——一种预示着祸事将临的东西。这情绪传染给了我，我把目光转回塔顶那群人身上，心头笼着莫名的恐惧。

===Original===
Those fears were but too well founded, for now the dread ceremony began. The Kohen drew his knife, and placed himself at the head of the stone table. One of the youths came forward, stepped upon it, and lay down on his back with his head toward the Kohen. The mournful chant still went on. Then the Kohen raised his knife and plunged it into the heart of the youth. I sat for a moment rooted to the spot; then a groan burst from me in spite of myself. Almah caught my hands in hers, which were as cold as ice.

“Be firm,” she said, “or we are both lost. Be firm, Atam-or!”

“I must go,” said I, and I tried to rise.

===Chinese===
那些恐惧，不幸而言中了——那可怖的仪节，此刻开始了。科恩抽出刀来，在石案上首立定。一名少年走上前去，踏上石案，仰面躺下，头朝着科恩。哀婉的歌吟仍在继续。于是科恩举起刀，一刀刺进了那少年的心口。我一时间呆坐原地，如钉在那里一般；随即一声呻吟，不由自主地从我胸中迸出。阿尔玛一把攥住我的双手——她那双手，冷得像冰。

「稳住，」她说，「不然你我两人都要毁了。稳住，阿塔莫尔！」

「我必须走，」我说着，便想站起身来。

===Original===
“Don’t move,” she said, “for your life! We are lost if you move. Keep still﻿—restrain yourself﻿—shut your eyes.”

I tried to do so, but could not. There was a horrible fascination about the scene which forced me to look and see all. The Kohen took the victim, and drawing it from the altar, threw it over the precipice to the ground beneath. Then a loud shout burst forth from the great crowd. “*Sibgu Sibgin! Ranenu! Hodu lecosck!*” which means, “Sacrifice the victims! Rejoice! Give thanks to darkness!”

Then another of the youths went forward amid the singing, and laid himself down to meet the same fate; and again the corpse was flung from the top of the pyramid, and again the shout arose. All the others came forward in the same manner. Oh, horrible, horrible, thrice horrible spectacle! I do not remember how I endured it. I sat there with Almah, trying to restrain myself as she had entreated me, more for her sake than for my own, a prey to every feeling of horror, anguish, and despair. How it all ended I do not know, nor do I know how I got away from the place; for I only remember coming back to my senses in the lighted grotto, with Almah bending anxiously over me.

===Chinese===
「千万别动，」她说，「这可性命交关！你一动，我们就全完了。稳坐着——忍住——闭上眼睛。」

我竭力想照办，却办不到。那场面自有一种骇人的魔力，逼着我非看不可，非把一切看尽不可。科恩提起那祭牲，将它从祭坛上拖了下来，抛下危崖，摔在底下的地上。人群随即迸出一声高呼：「西布古·西布金！拉内努！霍杜·勒科塞克！（Sibgu Sibgin! Ranenu! Hodu lecosck!）」——意思是：「宰杀祭牲！欢庆吧！感谢黑暗！」

接着，歌声之中又一名少年走上前去，躺下身来，迎接同样的命运；那尸身又被从塔顶抛下，那呼喊也再度掀起。其余的人也都一一上前，如法而行。哦，骇人，骇人，骇人至极的场面！我不记得自己是怎样熬过来的。我同阿尔玛坐在那里，遵着她的恳求竭力克制自己——多半是为她的缘故，倒不是为我自己的缘故——任凭惊怖、苦痛与绝望百般啮咬。这一切如何收场，我不知道，也不知道自己是怎样离开那个地方的；我只记得，待神志回转时，我已身在灯烛通明的洞府里，阿尔玛正焦灼地俯身望着我。

===Original===
After this there remained a dark mystery and an ever-present horror. I found myself among a people who were at once the gentlest of the human race and the most bloodthirsty﻿—the kindest and the most cruel. This mild, amiable, and self-sacrificing Kohen, how was it possible that he should transform himself to a fiend incarnate? And for me and for Almah, what possible hope could there be? What fate might they have in reserve for us? Of what avail was all this profound respect, this incessant desire to please, this attention to our slightest wish, this comfort and luxury and splendor, this freedom of speech and action? Was it anything better than a mockery? Might it not be the shallow kindness of the priest to the victim reserved for the sacrifice? Was it, after all, in any degree better than the kindness of the cannibal savages on those drear outer shores who received us with such hospitality, but only that they might destroy us at last? Might they not all belong to the same race, dwelling as they did in caverns, shunning the sunlight, and blending kindness with cruelty? It was an awful thought!

Yet I had one consolation. Almah was with me, and so long as she was spared to me I could endure this life. I tried for her sake to resist the feelings that were coming over me. I saw that she too was a prey to ever-deepening sadness. She felt as I did, and this despair of soul might wreck her young life if there were no alleviation. And so I sought to alleviate her distress and to banish her sadness. The songs of these people had much impressed me; and one day, as I talked about this with Almah, she brought forth a musical instrument of peculiar shape, which was not unlike a guitar, though the shape was square and there were a dozen strings. Upon this she played, singing at the same time some songs of a plaintive character. An idea now occurred to me to have an instrument made according to my own plans, which should be nothing less than a violin. Almah was delighted at the proposal, and at once found a very clever workman, who under my direction succeeded in producing one which served my purpose well. I was a good violinist, and in this I was able to find solace for myself and for Almah for many a long hour.

The first time that I played was memorable. As the tones floated through the air they caught the ears of those outside, and soon great numbers came into the apartment, listening in amazement and in rapt attention. Even the painful light was disregarded in the pleasure of this most novel sensation, and I perceived that if the sense of sight was deficient among them, that of hearing was sufficiently acute. I played many times, and sometimes sang from among the songs of different nations; but those which these people liked best were the Irish and Scottish melodies﻿—those matchless strains created by the genius of the Celtic race, and handed down from immemorial ages through long generations. In these there was nothing artificial, nothing transient. They were the utterance of the human heart, and in them there was that touch of nature which makes all men kin. These were the immortal passions which shall never cease to affect the soul of man, and which had power even here; the strains of love, of sadness, and of pathos were sweet and enticing to this gentle race; for in their mild manners and their outburst of cruelty they seemed to be not unlike the very race which had created this music, since the Celt is at once gentle and bloodthirsty.

===Chinese===
此事之后，留下的是一个黑暗之谜，一种无时不在的恐怖。我发觉自己是身处这样一个民族中间：他们既是人类中最温良的，又是最嗜血的——既是最仁慈的，又是最残酷的。这位温文、和蔼、舍己奉身的科恩，怎么可能摇身一变，化作一个恶魔的化身呢？至于我和阿尔玛，还能有什么指望？他们为我们备下的是怎样的命运？这种种极尽的敬重、无休无止的承欢之意、对我们最细微心愿的体恤、这舒适、豪奢与富丽、这言谈举止的自由，究竟有什么用处？这难道不只是嘲弄么？这会不会只是祭司待那留作祭品的牺牲的一片浅薄恩慈呢？说到底，这比起那些阴凄外海岸边食人的野蛮人，又强得了几分？想当初他们何等殷勤地接纳我们，到头来也不过是要把我们葬送。这些人一样穴居，一样避忌日光，一样把慈和同残酷搀和在一处——他们会不会本就是同一个人种呢？这念头真叫人不寒而栗！

然而我尚有一件安慰。阿尔玛在我身边；只要她还在，这样的日子我便熬得下去。为了她的缘故，我竭力抵御那一阵阵袭上心头的情绪。我看得出，她也正被愈来愈深的哀愁啮咬着。她的感受同我一样；这份灵魂里的绝望，若不得宽解，怕要毁掉她年轻的生命。于是我便设法宽解她的愁苦，驱散她的哀伤。这个民族的歌声曾深深打动过我；一天，我同阿尔玛谈起这个，她取出一件形状古怪的乐器来，同吉他颇为相像，只是琴身是方的，弦有十二根。她在上面弹奏起来，同时唱了几支哀婉情调的歌。这时我忽然起了一个念头，要照我自己的图样叫人制一件乐器——不是别的，正是一把小提琴。阿尔玛听了这主意欢喜不已，立刻寻到一位极灵巧的工匠，在我的指点下，果然制成了一把，很合我的用。我的小提琴拉得不错；倚着它，在许许多多漫长的时辰里，我为自己、也为阿尔玛觅得了一份慰藉。

我头一回拉琴的那个时辰，是难以忘怀的。琴音飘荡在空中，落进了外面诸人的耳中；不多时，大批的人便涌进屋来，既惊且羡，听得全神贯注。在这最新奇的欢愉之中，连那刺目的光亮也被置之度外；我这才看出，这些人视觉虽有欠缺，听觉却足够敏锐。我拉了许多回，有时还从各国的歌谣里拣几支唱起来；但这些人最爱听的，是爱尔兰和苏格兰的曲调——那是凯尔特民族的天才所创造的绝调，自太古以来，经由绵长的世代，一路流传下来。这些曲子里没有一点矫揉造作，没有一点转瞬即逝的浮华。它们是人心的吐诉，其中含有那一点使普天之下人同此心的天性。这是些不朽的激情，永不会停止撼动人的灵魂，而且即使在这里，也有它的伟力；爱情、哀愁与悲悯的曲调，对这个温良的民族自有一番甘美与诱人；因为观其温和的举止与暴起的残酷，他们同创造这音乐的民族，实在相去不远——凯尔特人本就是既温良又嗜血的。

===Original===
I played “Tara,” “Bonnie Doon,” “The Last Rose of Summer,” “The Land of the Leal,” “Auld Lang Syne,” “Lochaber.” They stood entranced, listening with all their souls. They seemed to hunger and thirst after this music, and the strains of the inspired Celtic race seemed to come to them like the revelation of the glory of heaven. Then I played more lively airs. Some I played a second time, singing the words. They seemed eager to have the same one played often. At last a grisly thought came to me: it was that they would learn these sweet strains, and put their own words to them so as to use them at the awful sacrifices. After that I would play no more.

It is a land of tender love and remorseless cruelty. Music is all-powerful to awaken the one, but powerless to abate the other; and the eyes that weep over the pathetic strains of “Lochaber” can gaze without a tear upon the death-agonies of a slaughtered friend.

===Chinese===
我拉了《塔拉》（Tara）、《美丽的杜恩河》（Bonnie Doon）、《夏日最后一朵玫瑰》（The Last Rose of Summer）、《忠信之邦》（The Land of the Leal）、《友谊地久天长》（Auld Lang Syne）、《洛哈伯》（Lochaber）。他们站在那里，心驰神往，全副灵魂地听着。他们对这音乐仿佛如饥似渴；那天纵的凯尔特民族的曲调，落到他们耳中，竟像是天国荣光显圣一般。随后我又拉了几支轻快的调子。有几支我拉了第二遍，还唱出了词句。同一支曲子，他们巴不得叫人多拉几遍。终于，一个阴惨的念头浮上心头：他们是要把这些甜美的曲调学了去，再填上自己的词，好在那可怖的献祭上派用场。自那以后，我便再不肯拉琴了。

这是一个柔情缱绻而残酷无情的国度。音乐于唤醒前者，有无边的伟力；于消减后者，却无半点用处；那双为《洛哈伯》哀婉的曲调而落泪的眼睛，望着惨遭屠戮的朋友临死的挣扎，竟可以一滴泪也没有。

## XIII The Awful Mista Kosek / 十三、骇人的米斯塔·科塞克黑暗盛宴

===Original===
The terrible sacrifice marked the end of the light season. The dark season had now begun, which would last for half the coming year. No more sunlight would now be visible, save at first for a few *joms*, when at certain times the glare would be seen shooting up above the icy crests of the mountains. Now the people all moved out of the caverns into the stone houses on the opposite side of the terraces, and the busy throng transferred themselves and their occupations to the open air. This with them was the season of activity, when all their most important affairs were undertaken and carried out; the season, too, of enjoyment, when all the chief sports and festivals took place. Then the outer world all awoke to life; the streets were thronged, fleets of galleys came forth from their moorings, and the sounds of labor and of pleasure, of toil and revelry, arose into the darkened skies. Then the city was a city of the living, no longer silent, but full of bustle, and the caverns were frequented but little. This cavern life was only tolerable during the light season, when the sun-glare was over the land; but now, when the beneficent and grateful darkness pervaded all things, the outer world was infinitely more agreeable.

To me, however, the arrival of the dark season brought only additional gloom. I could not get rid of the thought that I was reserved for some horrible fate, in which Almah might also be involved. We were both aliens here, in a nation of kindhearted and amiable miscreants﻿—of generous, refined, and most self-denying fiends; of men who were highly civilized, yet utterly wrongheaded and irreclaimable in their bloodthirsty cruelty. The stain of blood-guiltiness was over all the land. What was I, that I could hope to be spared? The hope was madness, and I did not pretend to indulge it.

The only consolation was Almah. The manners of these people were such that we were still left as unconstrained as ever in our movements, and always, wherever we went, we encountered nothing but amiable smiles and courteous offices. Everyone was always eager to do anything for us﻿—to give, to go, to act, to speak, as though we were the most honored of guests, the pride of the city. The Kohen was untiring in his efforts to please. He was in the habit of making presents every time he came to see me, and on each occasion the present was of a different kind; at one time it was a new robe of curiously wrought feathers, at another some beautiful gem, at another some rare fruit. He also made incessant efforts to render my situation pleasant, and was delighted at my rapid progress in acquiring the language.

===Chinese===
那场可怖的献祭，标志着光明季就此终了。黑暗季如今开始了，往后要延续来年的半年。从此再看不见日光，只在起初的几个约姆（jom）里，某些时辰还望得见炫亮的光芒，从群山冰雪的峰巅后迸射上来。这时，人们纷纷迁出洞穴，搬进梯台另一侧的石屋，熙攘的人潮连人带营生，一并挪到了露天里。在这些人，这正是百事兴作的季候，一切最重大的事务都在这时兴办、这时完成；也是行乐的季候，一切主要的竞技与节庆都在这时举行。外面的世界于是一齐苏醒过来：街市上人头攒动，成队的桨帆船解缆出港，劳作与欢乐、辛苦与宴饮的声浪，升入昏暗的天空。这座城于是一座生人之城，不再阒寂无声，而是熙熙攘攘，洞穴里反倒少有人迹了。这种穴居生活，唯有光明季里日光灼照大地时才勉强可耐；而如今，当那造福万物、令人感戴的黑暗弥漫于一切时，外面的世界便有说不出的惬意。

然而在我，黑暗季的到来不过又添一层阴翳。我总摆脱不了一个念头：我是被留下来供某种可怖的命运发落的，而阿尔玛说不定也要牵连在内。我们两个在此地都是异乡之人，寄身于一个善良和蔼的恶徒之邦——一群慷慨、文雅、克己到极处的魔鬼；一群文明程度极高、却在嗜血的残虐上执迷不悟、无可救药的人。血罪的污痕笼盖着整个国土。我算什么人，竟指望得免？这指望无异于痴狂，我也犯不着假意沉溺其中。

唯一的慰藉便是阿尔玛。这些人的风气如此：我们的行动一如既往，丝毫不受拘束，而且无论走到哪里，迎面遇到的尽是和蔼的笑容与殷勤的效劳。人人总是热切地愿为我们效一切劳——馈赠、奔走、出力、进言，仿佛我们是最尊贵的宾客、全城的骄傲。科恩取悦于人的心血从不倦怠。他惯于每次来看我必携馈赠，且每回品类各异：一时是一件羽毛精工织就的新袍，一时是一粒莹洁的宝石，一时是一种珍异的水果。他还不断用心，务使我处境适意，见我学他们的语言学得飞快，更是欢喜不尽。
===Original===
On the *jom* following the sacrifice I accompanied Almah as she went to her daily task, and after it was over I asked when the new victims would be placed here. “How long does it take to embalm them?” I added.

Almah looked at me earnestly. “They will not bring them here; they will not embalm them,” said she.

“Why not?” I asked; “what will they do with them?”

“Do not ask,” said she. “It will pain you to know.”

In spite of repeated solicitation she refused to give me any satisfaction. I felt deeply moved at her words and her looks. What was it, I wondered, that could give me pain? or what could there still be that could excite fear in me, who had learned and seen so much? I could not imagine. It was evidently some disposal of the bodies of the victims﻿—that was plain. Turning this over in my mind, with vague conjectures as to Almah’s meaning, I left her and walked along the terrace until I came to the next cavern. This had never been open before, and I now entered through curiosity to see what it might be. I saw a vast cavern, quite as large as the *cheder nebilin*, full of people, who seemed to be engaged in decorating it. Hundreds were at work, and they had brought immense tree-ferns, which were placed on either side in long rows, with their branches meeting and interlacing at the top. It looked like the interior of some great Gothic cathedral at night, and the few twinkling lights that were scattered here and there made the shadowy outline just visible to me.

===Chinese===
献祭之后的那个约姆，我陪阿尔玛去做她每日的职司；职司既毕，我问她，新的人牲几时才会安放到这里来。「替他们做防腐，得费多少时日？」我又添了一句。

阿尔玛恳切地望着我。「他们不会把他们安放到这里来，也不会替他们做防腐。」她说。

「这是为什么？」我问；「他们打算拿他们怎么办？」

「别问了，」她说。「知道了只会叫你痛心。」

任凭我再三恳求，她也不肯给我半点确切的回答。她的话音与神色，都叫我深深动容。究竟是什么事，我暗自纳闷，还能叫我心痛？我这个见闻已经如此之多的人，还有什么能勾起我的恐惧？我想象不出。这显然牵涉人牲遗体如何发落——那是明摆着的。我心里翻来覆去地盘算着，对阿尔玛的用意作着种种茫然的揣测，随后别过她，沿梯台信步走去，来到旁边那座洞窟跟前。这洞窟以前从不开敞，我一时好奇，便进去看看它究竟是个什么所在。只见一座浩大的洞窟，与赫德尔·内比林（亡者之厅）不相上下，里面满是人群，似乎正忙着装点它。数以百计的人在劳作，运来了高大的树蕨，分列两旁，排成长行，枝柯在顶上相交盘错。那景象宛如一座巨大的哥特式大教堂在夜色中的内景，几点闪烁的灯火散布各处，恰好叫我看清那朦胧的轮廓。
===Original===
I asked one of the bystanders what this might be, and he told me that it was the *Mista Kosek*, which means the “Feast of Darkness,” from which I gathered that they were about to celebrate the advent of the dark season with a feast. From what I knew of their character this seemed quite intelligible, and there was much beauty and taste in the arrangements. All were industrious and orderly, and each one seemed most eager to assist his neighbor. Indeed, there seemed to be a friendly rivalry in this which at times amounted to positive violence; for more than once when a man was seen carrying too large a burden, someone else would insist on taking it from him. At first these altercations seemed exactly like the quarrels of workmen at home, but a closer inspection showed that it was merely the persistent effort of one to help another.

I learned that the feast was to take place as soon as the hall was decorated, and that it would be attended by a great multitude. I felt a great interest in it. There seemed something of poetic beauty in this mode of welcoming the advent of a welcome season, and it served to mitigate the horrible remembrance of that other celebration, upon which I could not think without a shudder. I thought that it would be pleasant to join with them here, and resolved to ask Almah to come with me, so that she might explain the meaning of the ceremonies. Full of this thought, I went to her and told her my wish. She looked at me with a face full of amazement and misery. In great surprise I questioned her eagerly.

“Ask me nothing,” said she. “I will answer nothing; but do not think of it. Do not go near it. Stay in your room till the fearful repast is over.”

===Chinese===
我问旁边一个旁观的人这是做什么，他告诉我，这是「米斯塔·科塞克（Mista Kosek）」——意即「黑暗盛宴」。由此我推想，他们是要设一场盛筵，来庆贺黑暗季的降临。就我对这些人禀性的了解，这似乎很好理解，何况种种布置之中颇见雅致与意趣。人人勤勉而井然，个个都巴不得去帮衬旁人。这中间俨然还有一种友善的争先，有时竟到了当真动粗的地步：因为不止一次，谁扛的东西太重，便另有一个人执意要从他手里夺过去。起初这些争嚷，同家乡工匠们的拌嘴一模一样，细看才知，那不过是一个人执拗地要帮另一个人的忙罢了。

我打听到，大厅一经装点完毕，盛筵便即开张，赴会的将有黑压压的一大群人。我对此大感兴趣。以这样的方式迎接一个受人欢迎的季候的降临，其中似乎颇有几分诗意的美，这也稍稍冲淡了那另一场庆典留下的可怖记忆——想起那一场，我总不免打个寒噤。我想，同他们一起在这里乐一乐倒也不错，便打定主意邀阿尔玛同去，好由她讲解种种仪节的意义。我满怀着这个念头走到她跟前，说出了我的愿望。她望着我，满脸的惊愕与凄楚。我大为诧异，急切地追问她。

「什么也别问我，」她说。「我什么也不会回答；你只别打这个主意。千万别走近它。那可怖的筵席完结之前，你就待在自己屋里。」
===Original===
“Fearful? How is it fearful?” I asked.

“Everything here is fearful,” said Almah, with a sigh. “Every season it grows worse, and I shall grow at length to hate life and love death as these people do. They can never understand us, and we can never understand them. Oh, if I could but once more stand in my own dear native land but for one moment﻿—to see once more the scenes and the faces that I love so well! Oh, how different is this land from mine! Here all is dark, all is terrible. There the people love the light and rejoice in the glorious sun, and when the dark season comes they wait, and have no other desire than long day. There we live under the sky, in the eye of the sun. We build our houses, and when the dark season comes we fill them with lamps that make a blaze like the sun itself.”

“We must try to escape,” I said, in a low voice.

===Chinese===
「可怖？它怎么个可怖法？」我问。

「这里样样都可怖，」阿尔玛叹了口气说。「而且一季甚似一季，我到底也会变得同这些人一样，厌生而尚死。他们永远不能懂得我们，我们也永远不能懂得他们。唉，只要能再在亲爱的故土上站上一时半刻也好——再看一眼我深爱着的那些风物与面容！唉，这片地方同我的家乡多么不同啊！这里一切是黑暗，一切是可怕。在那里，人们热爱光明，在壮丽的太阳底下欢欣；黑暗季来临时，他们便等待，一心只盼长昼，别无他求。在那里，我们生活在青天之下、太阳的眼目之中。我们盖起房屋，黑暗季来临时，便在屋里点满灯火，燃出一片同太阳本身一样的光焰。」

「我们得设法逃走，」我低声说。
===Original===
“Escape!” said she. “That is easy enough. We might go now; but where?”

“Back,” said I, “to your own country. See, the sky is dotted with stars: I can find my way by them.”

“Yes,” said she, “if I could only tell you where to go; but I cannot. My country lies somewhere over the sea, but where, I know not. Over the sea there are many lands, and we might reach one even worse than this.”

“Perhaps,” said I, “the Kohen might allow us to go away to your country, and send us there. He is most generous and most amiable. He seems to spend most of his time in efforts to make us happy. There must be many seamen in this nation who know the way. It would be worth trying.”

===Chinese===
「逃走！」她说。「那再容易不过。我们现在就能走；可是往哪儿去？」

「回去，」我说，「回你自己的国家去。你看，天上缀满了繁星：我能靠它们认路。」

「是啊，」她说，「只要我能告诉你该往哪里去就好；可惜我做不到。我的国家在海外什么地方，究竟在哪儿，我不知道。海那边有许多陆地，说不定我们到的那一块，比这里还要糟。」

「要不然，」我说，「科恩兴许肯放我们到你国家去，还肯送我们前往。他再慷慨、再和气不过了，这些时候，他仿佛把大半光阴都花在讨我们欢心上。这个国里必有许多水手认得航路。倒值得一试。」
===Original===
Almah shook her head. “You do not understand these people,” said she. “Their ruling passion is the hatred of self, and therefore they are eager to confer benefits on others. The only hope of life that I have for you and for myself is in this, that if they kill us they will lose their most agreeable occupation. They value us most highly, because we take everything that is given us. You and I now possess as our own property all this city and all its buildings, and all the people have made themselves our slaves.”

At this I was utterly bewildered.

“I don’t understand,” said I.

“I suppose not,” said Almah; “but you will understand better after you have been here longer. At any rate, you can see for yourself that the ruling passion here is self-denial and the good of others. Everyone is intent upon this, from the Kohen up to the most squalid pauper.”

===Chinese===
阿尔玛摇了摇头。「你不了解这些人，」她说。「他们心中压倒一切的激情是憎恶自我，因此个个热衷于施惠于人。我为你、也为我自己留一条活命，唯一的指望便在这里：他们若杀了我们，就失掉了最称心的营生。他们把我们器重到极处，因为我们把他们给的样样都收下。如今这整座城和城中全部屋宇，都已归你我做自家的产业，全城的百姓也都自己甘愿做了我们的奴隶。」

听了这话，我简直如坠五里雾中。

「我不明白，」我说。

「我本也料到，」阿尔玛说；「不过等你在此时日一久，自会明白得多。无论如何，你自己也看得出：此地压倒一切的激情，便是舍己与利人。人人一心扑在这上头，从科恩起，一路向上，直到最褴褛的赤贫者。」
===Original===
“*Up* to the most squalid pauper?” said I. “I do not understand you. You mean *down* to the most squalid pauper.”

“No,” said Almah; “I mean what I say. In this country the paupers form the most honored and envied class.”

“This is beyond my comprehension,” said I. “But if this is really so, and if these people pretend to be our slaves, why may we not order out a galley and go?”

“Oh, well, with you in your land, if a master were to order his slaves to cut his throat and poison his children and burn his house, would the slaves obey?”

===Chinese===
「『向上』到最褴褛的赤贫者？」我说。「我不明白你的话。你是说『向下』到最褴褛的赤贫者吧。」

「不，」阿尔玛说；「我怎么说的，便是什么意思。在这个国家，赤贫者是最受尊崇、最遭人艳羡的阶级。」

「这真是超出我的悟性了，」我说。「可倘若当真如此，倘若这些人自己甘为我们的奴隶，我们何不吩咐调一艘桨帆船，就此走他一遭？」

「哦，那么，照你们国内的情形，倘使一个主人命令他的奴隶割断他自己的喉咙、毒死他的儿女、烧掉他的房屋，奴隶们会服从么？」
===Original===
“Certainly not.”

“Well, our slaves here would not﻿—in fact could not﻿—obey a command that would be shocking to their natures. They think that we are in the best of all lands, and my request to be sent home would be utterly monstrous.”

“I suppose,” said I, “they would kill us if we asked them to do so?”

“Yes,” said Almah; “for they think death the greatest blessing.”

===Chinese===
「当然不会。」

「同样，我们这里的奴隶也不会——实在也不能——服从一条在他们天性看来骇人听闻的命令。他们认为我们身处万国之中最好的一国，我求他们送我回家，在他们听来便是天大的悖谬。」

「这么说，」我说，「我们若求他们杀了我们，他们倒肯动手？」

「肯，」阿尔玛说；「因为他们把死亡看作至大的福分。」
===Original===
“And if at the point of death we should beg for life, would they spare us?”

“Certainly not,” said Almah. “Would you kill a man who asked for death? No more would these people spare a man who asked for life.”

All this was so utterly incomprehensible that I could pursue the subject no further. I saw, however, that Almah was wretched, dejected, and suffering greatly from homesickness. Gladly would I have taken her and started off on a desperate flight by sea or land﻿—gladly would I have dared every peril, although I well knew what tremendous perils there were; but she would not consent, and believed the attempt to be useless. I could only wait, therefore, and indulge the hope that at last a chance of escape might one day come, of which she would be willing to avail herself.

===Chinese===
「那倘若临死之际我们开口乞命，他们肯饶过我们么？」

「当然不肯，」阿尔玛说。「你可肯杀死一个自己求死的人？同样，这些人也断不肯饶过一个自己乞命的人。」

这一切实在无从索解，我只好把这个话题搁下，不再深谈。然而我看得出，阿尔玛愁苦不堪、郁郁寡欢，怀乡之病折磨得她好苦。我很乐意当即带了她走，从海上或陆路亡命奔逃——我甘愿冒尽一切凶险，尽管我深知那凶险何等骇人；可她不肯应允，认定这尝试徒劳无益。我只得等待，姑且怀着一线指望：逃走的机会终有一日会到来，而到那时，她也会愿意抓住它。
===Original===
Almah utterly refused to go to the feast, and entreated me not to go; but this only served to increase my curiosity, and I determined to see it for myself, whatever it was. She had seen it, and why should not I? Whatever it might be, my nerves could surely stand the shock as well as hers. Besides, I was anxious to know the very worst; and if there was anything that could surpass in atrocity what I had already witnessed, it were better that I should not remain in ignorance of it.

So at length, leaving Almah, I returned to the hall of the feast. I found there a vast multitude, which seemed to comprise the whole city﻿—men, women, children, all were there. Long tables were laid out. The people were all standing and waiting. A choir was singing plaintive strains that sounded like the chant of the sacrifice. Those nearest me regarded me with their usual amiable smiles, and wished to conduct me to some place of honor; but I did not care about taking part in this feast. I wished to be a mere spectator, nothing more. I walked past and came to the next cavern. This seemed to be quite as large as the other. There was a crowd of people here also, and at one end there blazed an enormous fire. It was a furnace that seemed to be used for cooking the food of this banquet, and there was a thick steam rising from an immense cauldron, while the air was filled with an odor like that of a kitchen.

All this I took in at a glance, and at the same instant I saw something else. There were several very long tables, which stood at the sides of the cavern and in the middle, and upon each of these I saw lying certain things covered over with cloths. The shape of these was more than suggestive﻿—it told me all. It was a sight of horror﻿—awful, tremendous, unspeakable! For a moment I stood motionless staring; then all the cavern seemed to swim around me. I reeled, I fell, and sank into nothingness.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛断然不肯赴那场盛筵，还恳求我也别去；可这反而把我的好奇心撩拨得更旺，我打定主意，不管那是什么，总要亲眼看上一看。她看得，我为什么看不得？无论那是什么，我的神经总扛得住这一惊，不见得就输给她。况且，我急欲知道那最坏中的最坏；倘若世上竟有比我已经目睹的更骇人的残虐，那么与其教我懵然无知，不如让我知道的好。

于是我终于撇下阿尔玛，回到盛筵的大厅。只见那里人山人海，仿佛全城的人都到齐了——男人、女人、孩子，无一不在。长长的食案一字排开。众人全都伫立等候。唱诗班唱着哀婉的调子，听去同献祭的诵唱如出一辙。离我最近的人们照例挂着和蔼的笑脸，要引我到什么荣席上去；我却无意参与这场盛筵，只愿做一个看客，仅此而已。我径自走过，来到旁边那座洞窟。这一座似乎同前一座一般大小，里面也有一大群人，一头烧着一大团熊熊的烈火。那是一座炉灶，似乎是用来烹制这场宴席的肴馔的；一只巨釜里腾起浓浓的蒸汽，空气里弥漫着一股厨房似的气味。

这一切我只一眼扫尽，而就在同一瞬间，我看见了别的东西。洞窟两侧和正中，摆着几张很长的食案，每张案上都躺着一些蒙盖着布的东西。那些形状何止是引人疑想——它把一切都道破了。那是恐怖的景象——骇人、可怖、不可言状！有片刻工夫，我木立着直瞪瞪望住；随后整座洞窟仿佛都围着我天旋地转起来。我立足不稳，倒了下去，沉入一片虚无。
===Original===
When I revived I was in the lighted grotto, lying on a couch, with Almah bending over me. Her face was full of tenderest anxiety, yet there was also apparent a certain solemn gloom that well accorded with my own feelings. As I looked at her she drew a long breath, and buried her face in her hands.

After a time my recollection returned, and all came back to me. I rose to a sitting posture.

“Do not rise yet,” said Almah, anxiously; “you are weak.”

“No,” said I; “I am as strong as ever; but I’m afraid that you are weaker.”

===Chinese===
待我苏醒过来，我已在那座灯烛荧荧的洞府里，躺在一张榻上，阿尔玛俯身望着我。她满脸是最温柔的焦灼，然而也透着一层庄重的阴郁，同我心底的滋味正相吻合。我望着她时，她长长吁了一口气，把脸埋进了双手。

过了一晌，我的记性回来了，一切也都想了起来。我撑起身，坐了起来。

「先别起来，」阿尔玛焦灼地说；「你还虚弱。」

「不，」我说；「我硬朗如初；倒是恐怕你，比先前更虚弱了。」
===Original===
Almah shuddered.

“If you had told me exactly what it was, I would not have gone.”

“I could not tell you,” said she. “It is too terrible to name. Even the thought is intolerable. I told you not to go. Why did you go?”

She spoke in accents of tender reproach, and there were tears in her eyes.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛浑身一颤。

「你若早把那究竟是什么，原原本本告诉我，我就不会去了。」

「我说不出口，」她说。「那事情可怕得叫人叫不出名字。连想一想都受不了。我嘱咐过你别去。你为什么偏要去？」

她话音里带着温柔的嗔怪，眼里噙着泪水。
===Original===
“I did not think of anything so hideous as that,” said I. “I thought that there might be a sacrifice, but nothing worse.”

I now learned that when I fainted I had been raised most tenderly, and the Kohen himself came with me as I was carried back, and he thought that Almah would be my most agreeable nurse. The Kohen was most kind and sympathetic, and all the people vied with one another in their efforts to assist me﻿—so much so that there was the greatest confusion. It was only by Almah’s express entreaty that they retired and left me with her.

Here was a new phase in the character of this mysterious people. Could I ever hope to understand them? Where other people are cruel to strangers, or at best indifferent, these are eager in their acts of kindness; they exhibit the most unbounded hospitality, the most lavish generosity, the most self-denying care and attention; where others would be offended at the intrusion of a stranger, and enraged at his unconquerable disgust, these people had no feeling save pity, sympathy, and a desire to alleviate his distress. And yet﻿—oh, and yet!﻿—oh, thought of horror!﻿—what was this that I had seen? The abhorrent savages in the outer wilderness were surely of the same race as these. They too received us kindly, they too lavished upon us their hospitality, and yet there followed the horror of that frightful repast. Here there had been kindness and generosity and affectionate attention, to be succeeded by deeds without a name. Ah me! what an hour that was! And yet it was as nothing compared to what lay before me in the future.

===Chinese===
「我没想到那事竟那般丑恶，」我说。「我只道也许有一场献祭，再没料到更糟的。」

我这时才得知：我晕倒之后，众人把我轻轻扶起，抬送回来时，科恩还亲自随行，一路陪着我，并且想到由阿尔玛看护我最为相宜。科恩极其仁厚体贴，众人也争相服侍我——争先恐后，闹得一片大乱。全凭阿尔玛再三恳求，他们才退了出去，把我留给她照看。

这便是这个神秘民族性情中又一副新面目。我还能指望懂得他们么？在别处，人们待异乡人残忍，至多不过漠然，这些人却争着行善；他们显示出无所不至的款客之道、倾其所有的慷慨、舍己从人的关切与照拂；换了旁人，见异乡人闯到跟前、又见他抑不住的憎恶，早要动怒生嗔，这些人却只有怜悯、体恤，一心只想排解他的苦楚。然而——唉，然而！——唉，一念及此，恐怖袭来！——我方才看见的究竟是什么？外面荒野里那些可憎的野蛮人，想必同这些人是同一个种族。当初他们也是这样善待我们，也是这样倾其殷勤，随后却来了那场骇人的筵席。这里先有善良、慷慨与体贴入微的眷注，随后便是无名可状的行径。天哪！那是何等的一个时辰啊！然而同日后摆在我面前的种种相比，这又算不得什么了。
===Original===
But the subject was one of which I dared not speak﻿—one from which I had to force my thoughts away. I took the violin and played “Lochaber” till Almah wept, and I had to put it away. Then I begged her to play or sing. She brought an instrument like a lute, and upon this she played some melancholy strains. At length the Kohen came in. His mild, benevolent face never exhibited more gentle and affectionate sympathy than now. He seated himself, and with eyes half closed, as usual, talked much; and yet, with a native delicacy which always distinguished this extraordinary man, he made no allusion to the awful Mista Kosek. For my own part, I could not speak. I was absentminded, overwhelmed with gloom and despair, and at the same time full of aversion toward him and all his race. One question, however, I had to put.

“Who were the victims of the Mista Kosek?”

“They?” said he, with an agreeable smile. “Oh, they were the victims of the sacrifice.”

I sank back in my seat, and said no more. The Kohen then took Almah’s lute, played and sang in a very sweet voice, and at length, with his usual consideration, seeing that I looked weary, he retired.

===Chinese===
可是这个题目，我不敢提起——只得强把自己的心思从那上面挪开。我取过小提琴，奏起《洛哈伯》（Lochaber），奏到阿尔玛泫然泣下，只得把琴收起。于是我恳请她弹奏一曲，或唱上一唱。她取来一件形似鲁特琴的乐器，在上面弹了几支哀婉的曲子。末了，科恩走了进来。他那张温良仁厚的脸，从未比此刻更显出温存亲爱、相怜相惜之色。他坐下来，照例半阖着眼，谈说了许久；然而，凭着这位非凡人物身上素来独具的一种天生的细腻，他对那骇人的米斯塔·科塞克只字不提。至于我，则一句话也说不出。我心神恍惚，被阴郁与绝望压得透不过气，同时又满心憎恶他、憎恶他整个的种族。然而有一个问题，我不能不问。

「米斯塔·科塞克的人牲，都是些什么人？」

「他们么？」他带着一团和悦的笑容说。「哦，他们就是献祭的那些人牲呀。」

我颓然靠回座上，再没有话说。科恩随即取过阿尔玛的鲁特琴，弹奏起来，用一副十分甜润的嗓子唱着；到后来，他照例体贴入微，见我面有倦色，便告退了。

## XIV I Learn My Doom / 十四、我得知自己的厄运

===Original===
Horror is a feeling that cannot last long; human nature is incapable of supporting it. Sadness, whether from bereavement, or disappointment, or misfortune of any kind, may linger on through life. In my case, however, the milder and more enduring feeling of sadness had no sufficient cause for existence. The sights which I had seen inspired horror, and horror only. But when the first rush of this feeling had passed there came a reaction. Calmness followed, and then all the circumstances of my life here conspired to perpetuate that calm. For here all on the surface was pleasant and beautiful; all the people were amiable and courteous and most generous. I had light and luxury and amusements. Around me there were thousands of faces, all greeting me with cordial affection, and thousands of hands all ready to perform my slightest wish. Above all, there was Almah. Everything combined to make her most dear to me. My life had been such that I never before had seen anyone whom I loved; and here Almah was the one congenial associate in a whole world of aliens: she was beautiful and gentle and sympathetic, and I loved her dearly, even before I understood what my feelings were. One day I learned all, and found that she was more precious to me than all the world.

It was one *jom* when she did not make her appearance as usual. On asking after her I learned that she was ill. At this intelligence there came over me a feeling of sickening anxiety and fear. Almah ill! What if it should prove serious? Could I endure life here without her sweet companionship? Of what value was life without her? And as I asked myself these questions I learned that Almah had become dearer to me than life itself, and that in her was all the sunshine of my existence. While she was absent, life was nothing; all its value, all its light, its flavor, its beauty, were gone. I felt utterly crushed. I forgot all else save her illness, and all that I had endured seemed as nothing when compared with this.

In the midst of my own anxiety I was surprised to find that the whole community was most profoundly agitated. Among all classes there seemed to be but one thought﻿—her illness. I could overhear them talking. I could see them wait outside to hear about her. It seemed to be the one subject of interest, beside which all others were forgotten. The Kohen was absorbed in her case; all the physicians of the city were more or less engaged in her behalf; and there came forward as volunteers every woman in the place who had any knowledge of sick-duties. I was somewhat perplexed, however, at their manner. They were certainly agitated and intensely interested, yet not exactly sad. Indeed, from what I heard it seemed as though this strange people regarded sickness as rather a blessing than otherwise. This, however, did not interfere in the slightest degree with the most intense interest in her, and the most assiduous attention. The Kohen in particular was devoted to her. He was absentminded, silent, and full of care. On the whole, I felt more than ever puzzled, and less able than ever to understand these people. I loved them, yet loathed them; for the Kohen I had at once affection and horror. He looked like an anxious father, full of tenderest love for a sick child﻿—full also of delicate sympathy with me; and yet I knew all the time that he was quite capable of plunging the sacrificial knife in Almah’s heart and of eating her afterward.

===Chinese===
恐怖这种情感是无法持久的；人性承受不起它。悲哀则不然，无论是出自丧亲、失意，还是任何一种不幸，都能缠绵终身。然而就我而言，这较温婉、较持久的悲哀，却没有足以存在的缘由。我所见的那些景象，激起的只是恐怖，纯然是恐怖。可当这情感的第一次冲击过去之后，继之而来的是一种反弹。平静随之而至，而此后我在此地生活的种种境遇，又仿佛共谋要将这份平静延续下去。因为在这里，表面上处处愉悦而美好；人人皆和蔼、谦恭、极为慷慨。我有光明，有奢华，有种种娱乐。我的周围千百张面孔，无不以真挚的情谊向我致意，千百只手，随时预备去实现我最细微的心愿。而最要紧的，是有阿尔玛。万事汇合，使她于我最亲最珍。我平生的际遇，使我此前从不曾见过一个自己所爱的人；而在这满目异族的天地里，阿尔玛是唯一与我意气相投的伴侣：她美丽、温柔、富于同情，我爱她爱得深切——甚至早在我省察自己的情感之前。有一天，我明白了一切，发觉她之于我，比整个世界都珍贵。

有一天，一个约姆，她没有照常露面。我一打听，才知道她病了。一阵令人作呕的焦虑与恐惧随之攫住了我。阿尔玛病了！倘若病势沉重，那便如何是好？没有她甜蜜的相伴，我在此地还活得下去么？没有她，生命还有何价值？这样自问着，我方才知道，阿尔玛于我已比生命本身更亲，我此生全部的阳光都系于她一身。她不在时，生命便什么都不是；它全部的价值、全部的光亮、它的滋味、它的美，尽皆消散。我感到全然被压垮了。除了她的病，我忘却了一切；同这个相比，我此前所受的一切苦难，都似乎算不得什么了。

正自焦虑之际，我却诧异地发觉，全体族人都为此深深激动。各阶级之间似乎只剩一个念头——她的病。我听得到他们议论纷纷；我看得到他们守在外头探问消息。这似乎是唯一令人挂心的话题，此外一切尽被置之度外。科恩全心扑在她的病势上；城中所有医师或多或少都在为她奔走；凡略谙看护之职的妇人，也都自告奋勇前来伺候。只是他们的态度令我有些惶惑。他们确乎激动，确乎关切入骨，却又算不上悲伤。的确，就我所闻，这个奇异的民族仿佛把生病看成一种福分，而非别样。然而这一点也不妨碍他们对她的至深关切与殷勤照拂。科恩尤其对她尽心竭力。他神思恍惚，沉默寡言，满怀忧念。总的说来，我比以往任何时候都更觉惶惑，也比以往任何时候都更无从索解这些人。我爱他们，却又憎恶他们；对科恩，我兼怀好感与恐怖。他看去像一位忧心如焚的父亲，对病中的孩子怀着至柔至深的爱——对我又满怀细腻的同情；然而我自始至终都知道，他完全做得出把献祭的刀捅进阿尔玛心口、事后还把她吃掉的事来。

===Original===
But my own thoughts were all of Almah. I learned how dear she was. With her the brightness of life had passed; without her existence would be intolerable. Her sweet voice, her tender and gracious manner, her soft touch, her tender, affectionate smile, her mournful yet trustful look﻿—oh, heavens! would all these be mine no more? I could not endure the thought. At first I wandered about, seeking rest and finding none; and at length I sat in my own room, and passed the time in listening, in questioning the attendants, in wondering what I should do if she should be taken from me.

At length on one blessed *jom*, the Kohen came to me with a bright smile.

“Our darling Almah is better,” said he. “Eat, I beseech you. She is very dear to all of us, and we have all felt for her and for you. But now all danger is past. The physicians say that she will soon be well.” There were tears in his eyes as he spoke. It may have been caused by the bright light, but I attributed this to his loving heart, and I forgot that he was a cannibal. I took his hands in mine and pressed them in deep emotion. He looked at me with a sweet and gentle smile.

===Chinese===
可我自己的心思，全都系在阿尔玛身上。我由此明白她是何等珍贵。有她在，生命才有光华；没有她，生存将不堪忍受。她甜美的嗓音，她温婉贤淑的风仪，她柔软的抚触，她那温存深情的微笑，她那哀婉而信赖的眼神——苍天啊！这一切难道从此便不再归我所有了么？这念头教我承受不起。起初我四处徘徊，寻一刻安息而不可得；末了我坐在自己房中，把时光都用于谛听、盘问侍者、心里揣想：倘若她当真被人从我身边夺去，我该怎么办。

终于，在一个蒙受祝福的约姆，科恩带着明朗的笑容来到我这里。

「我们亲爱的阿尔玛好些了，」他说，「求你吃点东西吧。她是我们大家顶顶亲爱的人，我们都为她、也为你难受过。如今危险全都过去了。医师们说她不久就会痊愈。」他说话时眼里含着泪。那也许是明亮的光线刺出来的，我却把它归于他那颗仁爱的心，忘了他是食人的人。我把他的双手握在我手里，情深激动地紧紧握着。他望着我，露出甜美温厚的一笑。

===Original===
“I see it all,” said he, in a low voice﻿—“you love her, Atam-or.”

I pressed his hands harder, but said nothing. Indeed, I could not trust myself to speak.

“I knew it,” said he; “it is but natural. You are both of a different race from us; you are both much alike, and in full sympathy with one another. This draws you together. When I first saw you I thought that you would be a fit companion for her here﻿—that you would lessen her gloom, and that she would be pleasant to you. I found out soon that I was right, and I felt glad, for you at once showed the fullest sympathy with one another. Never till you came was Almah happy with us; but since you have come she has been a different being, and there has been a joyousness in her manner that I never saw before. You have made her forget how to weep; and as for yourself, I hope she has made your life in this strange land seem less painful, Atam-or.”

===Chinese===
「我全看出来了，」他低声说——「你爱她，阿塔莫尔。」

我把他的手握得更紧，却没有作声。实在说，我不敢让自己开口。

「我早就知道了，」他说；「这本是再自然不过的事。你们二位都与我们是异族；你们彼此又何其相像，何其全然相契。这便把你们牵到了一处。我初见你时便想，你在此地正好给她做个伴——你可以排遣她的愁郁，她于你也可以相处得愉快。不久我便发觉所料不差，心里欢喜，因为你们一见之下便显出最深切的投契。在你来之前，阿尔玛同我们在一起从没有快活过；自从你来了，她便换了一个人，眉宇间有一种我从未见过的欢愉。你教会了她忘掉哭泣；至于你自己，我也盼望她已使你在这片奇土上的日子好过一些，阿塔莫尔。」

===Original===
At all this I was so full of amazement that I could not say one word.

“Pardon me,” continued he, “if I have said anything that may seem like an intrusion upon your secret and most sacred feelings. I could not have said it had it not been for the deep affection I feel for Almah and for you, and for the reason that I am just now more moved than usual, and have less control over my feelings.”

Saying this, he pressed my hand and left me. It was not the custom here to shake hands, but with his usual amiability he had adopted my custom, and used it as naturally as though he had been to the manner born.

===Chinese===
这一番话听得我惊愕万分，竟一句也说不出。

「恕我冒昧，」他接着说，「倘若我方才的话，像是唐突了你心中隐秘而最神圣的情感。若不是我对阿尔玛与你怀着深挚的情感，若不是我此刻心绪激荡异乎寻常、难以自制，我是决计说不出口的。」

说罢，他握了握我的手，便离我而去。握手本非此地的习俗，但他以素来的和善把我的习俗学了去，用得那样自然，仿佛生来便谙于此道。

===Original===
I was encouraged now. The mild Kohen came often to cheer me. He talked much about Almah﻿—about her sweet and gracious disposition, the love that all felt for her, the deep and intense interest which her illness had aroused. In all this he seemed more like a man of my own race than before, and in his eager desire for her recovery he failed to exhibit that love for death which was his nature. So it seemed: yet this desire for her recovery did not arise out of any lack of love for death; its true cause I was to learn afterward; and I was to know that if he desired Almah’s recovery now, it was only that she might live long enough to encounter death in a more terrific form. But just then all this was unknown, and I judged him by myself.

At last I learned that she was much better, and would be out on the following *jom*. This intelligence filled me with a fever of eager anticipation, so great that I could think of nothing else. Sleep was impossible. I could only wait, and try as best I might to quell my impatience. At last the time came. I sat waiting. The curtain was drawn aside. I sprang up, and, hurrying toward her, I caught her in my arms and wept for joy. Ah me, how pale she looked! She bore still the marks of her illness. She seemed deeply embarrassed and agitated at the fervor of my greeting; while I, instead of apologizing or trying to excuse myself, only grew more agitated still.

“Oh, Almah,” I cried. “I should have died if you had not come back to me! Oh, Almah, I love you better than life and I never knew how dearly I loved you till I thought that I had lost you! Oh, forgive me, but I must tell you﻿—and don’t weep, darling.”

===Chinese===
这下我受了鼓舞。温和的科恩常来宽解我。他谈起阿尔玛的时候极多——谈她温婉仁厚的性情，谈众人对她的爱，谈她的病所激起的至深关切。这一切使他比先前更像一个与我同族的人；在他切盼她痊愈的热望里，他竟不曾显露那出自天性的尚死之爱。表面看来如此：然而他那盼她痊愈之心，绝非因尚死之爱有丝毫欠缺；其中真正的缘由，我是日后才得知的；我后来才知道，他此刻之盼阿尔玛痊愈，不过是想让她活得足够长久，好去遭遇一种更为可怖的死。只是那时这一切都无从知晓，我便拿自己去揣度他。

末了我得知她已大有起色，下一个约姆便能出外走动了。这消息使我满怀热切的期待，亢奋得再想不动别的。安睡是不可能了。我唯有等待，竭尽所能按捺自己的急切。那时刻终于到了。我坐着等候。帘幕拉开。我一跃而起，抢步迎上前去，将她搂进怀里，喜极而泣。唉，她看去多么苍白！她面上还带着病容。我迎候得那样热烈，使她深感窘迫激动；而我非但不曾赔礼解嘲，反倒愈发不能自持。

「啊，阿尔玛，」我喊道，「你若不回到我身边来，我早已活不成了！啊，阿尔玛，我爱你胜过生命；直到自以为已经失去你，我才知道我爱你爱得有多深！啊，原谅我，可我必须告诉你——别哭，亲爱的。」

===Original===
She was weeping as I spoke. She said nothing, but twined her arms around my neck and wept on my breast. After this we had much to say that we had never mentioned before. I cannot tell the sweet words that she said to me; but I now learned that she had loved me from the first﻿—when I came to her in her loneliness, when she was homesick and heartsick; and I came, a kindred nature, of a race more like her own; and she saw in me the only one of all around her whom it was possible not to detest, and therefore she loved me.

We had many things to say to one another, and long exchanges of confidence to make. She now for the first time told me all the sorrow that she had endured in her captivity﻿—sorrow which she had kept silent and shut up deep within her breast. At first her life here had been so terrible that it had brought her down nearly to death. After this she had sunk into dull despair; she had grown familiar with horrors and lived in a state of unnatural calm. From this my arrival had roused her. The display of feeling on my part had brought back all her old self, and roused anew all those feelings which in her had become dormant. The darkness, the bloodshed, the sacrifices, all these affected me as they had once affected her. I had the same fear of death which she had. When I had gone with her to the *cheder nebilin*, when I had used my *sepet-ram* to save life, she had perceived in me feelings and impulses to which all her own nature responded. Finally, when I asked about the Mista Kosek, she warned me not to go. When I did go she was with me in thought and suffered all that I felt, until the moment when I was brought back and laid senseless at her feet.

“Then,” said Almah, “I felt the full meaning of all that lies before us.”

===Chinese===
我说话时她已在垂泪。她一言不发，只把双臂绕上我的脖颈，伏在我胸前哭泣。此后我们有许多话要说，都是从前不曾提及的。她对我说的那些甜美言语，我无从转述；但我由此得知，她从一开始便爱着我——当我在她的孤寂中来到她身边，当她思乡成疾、肝肠寸断的时候；而我来了，是个心性相通的人，出自一个与她更相近的种族；在她周遭的一切人当中，她看出唯有我尚可不去憎嫌，于是她便爱上了我。

我们彼此有许多话要讲，有许多衷曲要长久倾吐。她这时才第一次向我诉说了她被掳以来所受的全部苦楚——那苦楚她一向缄口不言，深深锁在胸中。起初，她在此地的生活可怕之极，几乎把她拖到了死亡的边缘。此后她便沉入木然的绝望；她对种种惨事习以为常，活在一种反常的平静里。我的到来将她从这状态中唤醒。我情感的流露使她全然恢复了旧日的自己，重新唤起她心中那些已然沉睡的情感。黑暗、流血、献祭，这一切刺痛我，正如它们当初刺痛她一样。我对死亡的畏惧，与她的一般无二。当我随她去那赫德尔·内比林（亡者之厅），当我用塞佩特-拉姆去救人性命，她便在我身上看出种种情感与冲动，与她自己的天性处处相应。末了，当我问起米斯塔·科塞克（黑暗盛宴），她曾警告我莫去。及至我当真去了，她的心神便一路伴着我，把我所感的苦痛一一陪着我受，直到我被抬回来、不省人事地放在她脚边的那一刻。

「那时，」阿尔玛说，「我才痛感横在我们面前的一切，其中的全部含义。」

===Original===
“What do you mean by that?” I asked, anxiously. “You speak as though there were something yet﻿—worse than what has already been; yet nothing can possibly be worse. We have seen the worst; let us now try to shake off these grisly thoughts, and be happy with one another. Your strength will soon be back, and while we have one another we can be happy even in this gloom.”

“Ah me,” said Almah, “it would be better now to die. I could die happy now, since I know that you love me.”

“Death!” said I; “do not talk of it﻿—do not mention that word. It is more abhorrent than ever. No, Almah, let us live and love﻿—let us hope﻿—let us fly.”

“Impossible!” said she, in a mournful voice. “We cannot fly. There is no hope. We must face the future, and make up our minds to bear our fate.”

===Chinese===
「你这话是什么意思？」我焦灼地问。「你说得仿佛还有什么在后面——比已经发生的更坏；可再没有什么能更坏了。最坏的我们都已见过；且让我们尽力抛开这些狰狞的念头，彼此快活度日。你的气力不久便会回来，只要我们还彼此相守，纵在这幽暗里也能快活。」

「唉，」阿尔玛说，「如今倒不如死了的好。如今我可以含笑而死，因为我知道你爱我。」

「死！」我说；「别谈它——休要提起那个字。它比以往任何时候都可憎。不，阿尔玛，让我们活着、相爱——让我们存着希望——让我们逃走。」

「不可能！」她声音凄楚地说。「我们逃不掉。没有希望。我们必须面对将来，横下心来承当我们的命运。」

===Original===
“Fate!” I repeated, looking at her in wonder and in deep concern. “What do you mean by our fate? Is there anything more which you know and which I have not heard?”

“You have heard nothing,” said she, slowly; “and all that you have seen and heard is as nothing compared with what lies before us. For you and for me there is a fate﻿—inconceivable, abhorrent, tremendous!﻿—a fate of which I dare not speak or even think, and from which there is no escape whatever.”

As Almah said this she looked at me with an expression in which terror and anguish were striving with love. Her cheeks, which shortly before had flushed rosy red in sweet confusion, were now pallid, her lips ashen; her eyes were full of a wild despair. I looked at her in wonder, and could not say a word.

“Oh, Atam-or,” said she, “I am afraid of death!”

===Chinese===
「命运！」我重复道，又惊又忧地望着她。「你说我们的命运，是什么意思？还有什么你知道、而我未曾听说过的事么？」

「你什么也没有听说过，」她缓缓地说；「你所见所闻的一切，比起横在我们面前的，都算不得什么。为你，为我，都有一重命运在——不可思议、可憎、骇人！——那命运我不敢说出口，甚至不敢去想，而且无论如何也逃不脱。」

阿尔玛说这话时望着我，那神情里，恐怖与苦痛正同爱恋交相挣扎。她的双颊，片刻之前还因甜美的羞赧泛着玫瑰色，此刻已灰白如纸，嘴唇毫无血色；她的眼中盛满狂乱的绝望。我惊愕地望着她，一句话也说不出来。

「啊，阿塔莫尔，」她说，「我怕死！」

===Original===
“Almah,” said I, “why will you speak of death? What is this fate which you fear so much?”

“It is this,” said she hurriedly and with a shudder, “you and I are singled out. I have been reserved for years until one should be found who might be joined with me. You came. I saw it all at once. I have known it﻿—dreaded it﻿—tried to fight against it. But it was of no use. Oh, Atam-or, our love means death; for the very fact that you love me and I love you seals our doom!”

“Our doom? What doom?”

“The sacrifice!” exclaimed Almah, with another shudder. In her voice and look there was a terrible meaning, which I could not fail to take. I understood it now, and my blood curdled in my veins. Almah clung to me despairingly.

===Chinese===
「阿尔玛，」我说，「你为什么偏要谈死？你如此惧怕的这个命运，究竟是什么？」

「是这样，」她急促地说，又打了个寒噤，「你我被人拣选出来了。多年来我一直被留存至今，专等有一个人出现，可以同我结作一处。你来了。我当即看穿了一切。我早已知道——早已惧怕——早已试图抗争。可是没有用。啊，阿塔莫尔，我们的爱就意味着死亡；因为恰是你爱我、我爱你这一事实，便注定了我们的厄运！」

「我们的厄运？什么厄运？」

「献祭！」阿尔玛失声喊道，又是一阵战栗。她的声音和神情里有一种可怕的含义，我不可能领会不到。我此刻全懂了，血液在我血管里凝结成冰。阿尔玛绝望地紧偎着我。

===Original===
“Do not leave me!” she cried﻿—“do not leave me! I have no one but you. The sacrifice, the sacrifice! It is our doom the great sacrifice﻿—at the end of the dark season. It is at the *amir*. We must go there to meet our doom.”

“The *amir*?” I asked; “what is that?”

“It is the metropolis,” said she.

I was utterly overwhelmed, yet still I tried to console her; but the attempt was vain.

===Chinese===
「不要离开我！」她哭喊道——「不要离开我！我除了你再无一人。献祭啊，献祭！我们的厄运便是那场大献祭——就在黑暗季将尽之时。就在阿米尔（amir）。我们必须上那里去赴我们的厄运。」

「阿米尔？」我问；「那是什么？」

「那是都城。」她说。

我全然被压倒了，却仍试图宽慰她；然而徒劳无功。

===Original===
“Oh!” she cried, “you will not understand. The sacrifice is but a part﻿—it is but the beginning. Death is terrible; yet it may be endured﻿—if there is only death. But oh!﻿—oh think!﻿—think of that which comes after﻿—the Mista Kosek!”

Now the full meaning flashed upon me, and I saw it all. In an instant there arose in my mind the awful sacrifice on the pyramid and the unutterable horror of the Mista Kosek. Oh, horror, horror, horror! Oh, hideous abomination and deed without a name! I could not speak. I caught her in my arms, and we both wept passionately.

The happiness of our love was now darkened by this tremendous cloud that lowered before us. The shock of this discovery was overpowering, and some time elapsed before I could rally from it. Though Almah’s love was sweet beyond expression, and though as the time passed I saw that every *jom* she regained more and more of her former health and strength, still I could not forget what had been revealed. We were happy with one another, yet our happiness was clouded, and amid the brightness of our love there was ever present the dread spectre of our appalling doom.

===Chinese===
「啊！」她喊道，「你不会明白的。献祭只是一部分——只是个开端。死是可怕的；可死还忍受得住——倘若单单只有死。可是啊！——啊，你想想！——想想随后到来的那个——米斯塔·科塞克！」

此刻，全部的含义如电光般照彻我心，我一切都明白了。转瞬间，我心中浮起了金字塔上那可怖的献祭，与米斯塔·科塞克那难以言宣的恐怖。啊，恐怖，恐怖，恐怖！啊，可憎的邪行，无以名状的罪孽！我说不出话来。我把她搂进怀中，我们两个抱头痛哭。

我们爱情的幸福，如今被压在头顶的一大团阴云罩暗了。这一发现的打击势不可当，过了好些时日，我才缓过劲来。阿尔玛的爱甜美得无可言喻，随着时日推移，我也眼见她一个约姆又一个约姆地恢复着旧日的健康与气力，然而那已被揭示的一切，我总不能忘怀。我们彼此是幸福的，可我们的幸福罩着阴影；在爱情的光辉之中，始终盘踞着我们那骇人厄运的可怕鬼影。

===Original===
These feelings, however, grew fainter. Hope is ever ready to arise; and I began to think that these people, though given to evil ways, were after all kindhearted, and might listen to entreaty. Above all, there was the Kohen, so benevolent, so self-denying, so amiable, so sympathetic. I could not forget all that he had said during Almah’s illness, and it seemed more than probable that an appeal to his better nature might not be without effect. I said as much to Almah.

“The Kohen,” said she; “why, he can do nothing.”

“Why not? He is the chief man here, and ought to have great influence.”

===Chinese===
然而这些情感渐渐淡了下去。希望总是随时乐于升起；我开始想，这些人虽然习于恶道，心地究竟是善良的，或许肯听恳求。尤其是还有科恩——那样仁慈，那样克己，那样和蔼，那样富于同情。他在阿尔玛病中说过的那一番话，我不能忘怀；向他天性中善良的一面呼吁，看来多半不至于全无效果。我便把这意思对阿尔玛说了。

「科恩，」她说；「唉，他什么也做不成。」

「为什么做不成？他是此地为首之人，理应大有势力。」

===Original===
“You don’t understand,” said she, with a sigh. “The Kohen is the lowest and least influential man in the city.”

“Why, who are influential if he is not?” I asked.

“The paupers,” said Almah.

“The paupers!” I exclaimed, in amazement.

===Chinese===
「你不明白，」她叹口气说。「科恩是这城中身分最低、最没有势力的人。」

「咦，他若算不得有势力，谁还算得？」我问。

「赤贫者。」阿尔玛说。

「赤贫者！」我惊呼，愕然万分。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Almah. “Here among these people the paupers form the most honored, influential, and envied portion of the community.”

This was incomprehensible. Almah tried to explain, but to no purpose, and I determined to talk to the Kohen.

===Chinese===
「正是，」阿尔玛说。「在这些人们中间，赤贫者是最受尊崇、最有势力、也最遭人艳羡的一群。」

这真是无从索解。阿尔玛试着解释，却全然无用，于是我决意去同科恩谈一谈。

## XV The Kohen Is Inexorable / 十五、科恩不肯通融

===Original===
I determined to talk to the Kohen, and try for myself whether he might not be accessible to pity. This greatest of cannibals might, indeed, have his little peculiarities, I thought, and who has not?﻿—yet at bottom he seemed full of tender and benevolent feeling; and as he evidently spent his whole time in the endeavor to make us happy, it seemed not unlikely that he might do something for our happiness in a case where our very existence was at stake.

The Kohen listened with deep attention as I stated my case. I did this fully and frankly. I talked of my love for Almah and of Almah’s love for me; our hope that we might be united so as to live happily in reciprocal affection; and I was going on to speak of the dread that was in my heart when he interrupted me:

“You speak of being united,” said he. “You talk strangely. Of course you mean that you wish to be separated.”

===Chinese===
我打定主意去同科恩谈一谈，亲自试探他究竟吃不吃怜悯这一套。这位食人者中的魁首，诚然难免有些小小的怪癖，我想，谁又没有呢？——然而细究底里，他似乎满怀温厚仁善之情；况且他分明把全部光阴都花在设法使我们快活上头，那么，在一件我们性命攸关的事情上，他未必便不肯为我们的幸福出一把力。

我陈述情由时，科恩凝神谛听。我说得坦率而详尽。我谈到我对阿尔玛的爱，也谈到阿尔玛对我的爱；谈到我们盼望结为一体，在两心相照中幸福地过活；我正要接着吐露心底那份恐惧，他却打断了我：

「你说结为一体，」他说，「你这话说得可怪。你当然是说，你盼望着分离。」

===Original===
“Separated!” I exclaimed. “What do you mean? Of course we wish to be united.”

The Kohen stared at me as I said this with the look of one who was quite puzzled; and I then went on to speak of the fate that was before us, and to entreat his sympathy and his aid that we might be saved from so hideous a doom. To all these words the Kohen listened with an air of amazement, as though I were saying incomprehensible things.

“You have a gentle and an affectionate nature,” I said﻿—“a nature full of sympathy with others, and noble self-denial.”

“Of course,” said the Kohen, quickly, as though glad to get hold of something which he could understand, “of course we are all so, for we are so made. It is our nature. Who is there who is not self-denying? No one can help that.”

This sounded strange indeed; but I did not care to criticize it. I came to my purpose direct and said,

“Save us from our fate.”

===Chinese===
「分离！」我失声叫道，「这是什么话？我们当然是盼望结合呀。」

我说这话时，科恩直瞪着我，那神情全然是一头雾水；于是我又接着说起横在我们面前的命运，恳求他垂怜相助，救我们脱离那样骇人的下场。这些话，科恩听得满面惊愕，仿佛我在讲些全然无法索解的东西。

「您生性温厚慈爱，」我说——「一副体恤他人、贵重克己的心肠。」

「那自然，」科恩赶忙应道，仿佛庆幸抓住了一件他听得懂的东西，「我们个个如此，因为我们生来便是这样。这是天性。天下谁人不克己？谁也由不得自己不这样。」

这话听来委实古怪，我却懒得同他理论，直截了当说明来意：

「救我们逃出这命运吧。」

===Original===
“Your fate?”

“Yes, from death﻿—that death of horror.”

“Death?﻿—horror? What do you mean by horror?” said the Kohen, in an amazement that was sincere and unfeigned. “I cannot comprehend your meaning. It seems as though you actually dislike death; but that is not conceivable. It cannot be possible that you fear death.”

“Fear death!” I exclaimed, “I do﻿—I do. Who is there that does not fear it?”

The Kohen stared.

===Chinese===
「你们的命运？」

「是的，逃出死亡——那可怖之极的死。」

「死亡？——可怖？你说『可怖』是什么意思？」科恩说，那惊愕出于至诚，没有半分假装，「我实在不懂你的意思。照你这话，你竟像当真厌恶死亡；可这叫人无从设想。你会怕死，这断不可能。」

「怕死！」我喊道，「我怕——我怕呀。天下谁人不怕死？」

科恩直瞪着眼。

===Original===
“I do not understand you,” he said.

“Do you not understand,” said I, “that death is abhorrent to humanity?”

“Abhorrent!” said the Kohen; “that is impossible. Is it not the highest blessing? Who is there that does not long for death? Death is the greatest blessing, the chief desire of man﻿—the highest aim. And you﻿—are you not to be envied in having your felicity so near? Above all, in having such a death as that which is appointed for you﻿—so noble, so sublime? You must be mad; your happiness has turned your head.”

All this seemed like hideous mockery, and I stared at the Kohen with a gaze that probably strengthened his opinion of my madness.

===Chinese===
「我不明白你的话。」他说。

「难道你不明白么，」我说，「死亡是人所深恶痛绝的？」

「深恶痛绝！」科恩说，「这不可能。死亡岂不是至高的福分？天下谁人不渴慕死亡？死亡是至大的福分，是人首要的心愿——是最高的鹄的。至于你——你的至福近在眼前，岂不教人艳羡？何况给你派定的又是那样一种死法——那样高贵，那样崇高？你准是疯了；这福分冲昏了你的头。」

这一切听来像恶毒的嘲弄；我直勾勾地瞪着科恩，那眼神多半反倒加深了他认定我疯了的看法。

===Original===
“Do you love death?” I asked at length, in amazement.

“Love death? What a question! Of course I love death﻿—all men do; who does not? Is it not human nature? Do we not instinctively fly to meet it whenever we can? Do we not rush into the jaws of sea-monsters, or throw ourselves within their grasp? Who does not feel within him this intense longing after death as the strongest passion of his heart?”

“I don’t know﻿—I don’t know,” said I. “You are of a different race; I do not understand what you say. But I belong to a race that fears death. I fear death and love life; and I entreat you, I implore you to help me now in my distress, and assist me so that I may save my life and that of Almah.”

“I﻿—I help you!” said the Kohen, in new amazement. “Why do you come to me﻿—to me, of all men? Why, I am nothing here. And help you to live﻿—to live! Who ever heard of such a thing?”

And the Kohen looked at me with the same astonishment which I should evince if a man should ask me to help him to die.

===Chinese===
「你爱死亡么？」我终于忍不住，惊愕地问。

「爱死亡？问的什么话！我当然爱死亡——人人皆爱；谁不爱？这岂不是人类的天性？我们岂不一有机会，便本能地飞身迎上前去？我们岂不扑进海怪的巨颚，或自投其掌握？谁不觉得胸中这一股对死亡的炽烈渴慕，是心头最强的激情？」

「我不知道——我不知道，」我说，「你们是另一个种族；你说的话我无从领会。可我出身在一个怕死的种族。我怕死，爱生；我恳求你，哀求你此刻救我于危难，助我保全我这条命，也保全阿尔玛那条命。」

「我——我帮你！」科恩说，又是一阵惊愕，「你怎么来找我——天下人里偏偏来找我？唉，我在此地算老几。何况是帮你活——活下去！这种事谁听说过？」

科恩望着我时的那副骇然，同有人来求我帮他寻死时我会露出的骇然，一般无二。

===Original===
Still, I persisted in my entreaty for his help.

“Such a request,” said he, “is revolting; you must be mad. Such a request outrages all the instincts of humanity. And even if I could do such violence to my own nature as to help you to such a thing, how do you think I could face my fellow-men, or how could I endure the terrible punishment which would fall upon me?”

“Punishment!” said I. “What! would you be punished?”

“Punished!” said the Kohen. “That, of course, would be inevitable. I should be esteemed an unnatural monster and the chief of criminals. My lot in life now is painful enough; but in this case my punishment would involve me in evils without end. Riches would be poured upon me; I should be raised to the rank of Kohen Gadol; I should be removed farther away than ever from the pauper class﻿—so far, indeed, that all hope in life would be over. I should be made the first and noblest and richest in all the land.”

He spoke these words just as if he had said, “the lowest, meanest, poorest, and most infamous.” It sounded like fresh mockery, and I could not believe but that he was amusing himself at my expense.

===Chinese===
我仍旧不死心，苦苦央求他出手相助。

「这样的请求，」他说，「令人发指；你准是疯了。这样的请求，糟践了人类的一切天性。退一步说，纵使我竟能如此摧折自己的天性，帮你办成这等事，你想我日后还如何见同胞的面？落在头上的可怕惩罚，我又如何消受得起？」

「惩罚！」我说，「什么！你还会受惩罚？」

「受罚！」科恩说，「那自然免不了。我会被众人看做悖逆天性的怪物、罪人之首。我如今的境遇已经够苦的了；可这一来，惩罚便要把我卷进无穷无尽的祸殃。财富会泼水一般浇到我头上来；我会被擢升到科恩·加多尔（大祭司）的高位；我会被挪得离赤贫者阶层愈来愈远——远到一生的指望就此断绝。我会成为全国首屈一指、最高贵、最富有的人。」

他说这些话时的那口气，就仿佛说的是「最低贱、最卑污、最赤贫、最不齿于人」。听来又是一番新的嘲弄，我不能不信他是在拿我开心。

===Original===
“This is cruel,” said I. “You are mocking me.”

“Cruel?﻿—cruel?” said he; “what is cruel? You mean that such a fate would be cruel for me.”

“No, no,” said I; “but alas! I see we cannot understand one another.”

“No,” said the Kohen, musingly, as he looked at me. “No, it seems not; but tell me, Atam-or, is it possible that you really fear death﻿—that you really love life?”

“Fear death!﻿—love life!” I cried. “Who does not? Who can help it? Why do you ask me that?”

===Chinese===
「这未免狠心了，」我说，「你是在嘲弄我。」

「狠心？——狠心？」他说，「什么算狠心？你是说，这样的命运落到我头上，才算狠心。」

「不，不，」我说，「可是唉！我看我们彼此是怎么也说不通了。」

「是啊，」科恩望着我，若有所思地说，「看来是说不通了；不过告诉我，阿塔莫尔，你当真怕死——当真爱生，这是可能的么？」

「怕死！——爱生！」我叫道，「谁不如此？谁能不如此？你为什么问我这个？」

===Original===
The Kohen clasped his hands in amazement.

“If you really fear death,” said he, “what possible thing is there left to love or to hope for? What, then, do you think the highest blessing of man?”

“Long life,” said I, “and riches and requited love.”

At this the Kohen started back, and stared at me as though I were a raving madman.

“Oh, holy shades of night!” he exclaimed. “What is that you say? What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
科恩惊愕得紧攥起双手。

「你若当真怕死，」他说，「那这世上还有什么可爱、可盼的？那么依你看，人至高的福分是什么？」

「长寿，」我说，「还有财富，和两情相悦的爱。」

这话一出，科恩猛地倒退，直瞪着我，仿佛我是个语无伦次的疯子。

「哦，神圣的夜之阴灵！」他失声道，「你说的是什么？你这是什么意思？」

===Original===
“We can never understand one another, I fear,” said I. “The love of life must necessarily be the strongest passion of man. We are so made. We give up everything for life. A long life is everywhere considered as the highest blessing; and there is no one who is willing to die, no matter what his suffering may be. Riches also are desired by all, for poverty is the direst curse that can embitter life; and as to requited love, surely that is the sweetest, purest, and most divine joy that the human heart may know.”

At this the Kohen burst forth in a strain of high excitement:

“Oh, sacred cavern gloom! Oh, divine darkness! Oh, impenetrable abysses of night! What, oh, what is this! Oh, Atam-or, are you mad? Alas! it must be so. Joy has turned your brain; you are quite demented. You call good evil, and evil good; our light is your darkness, and our darkness your light. Yet surely you cannot be altogether insane. Come, come, let us look further. How is it! Try now to recall your reason. A long life﻿—a life, and a long one! Surely there can be no human being in a healthy state of nature who wishes to prolong his life; and as to riches, it is possible that anyone exists who really and honestly desires riches? Impossible! And requited love! Oh, Atam-or, you are mad today! You are always strange, but now you have quite taken leave of your senses. I cannot but love you, and yet I can never understand you. Tell me, and tell me truly, what is it that you consider evils, if these things that you have mentioned are not the very worst?”

He seemed deeply in earnest and much moved. I could not understand him, but could only answer his questions with simple conciseness.

===Chinese===
「恐怕我们永远也谈不拢，」我说，「爱生之心必然是人最强烈的激情。我们生来如此。为了活着，我们甘愿舍弃一切。长寿在天下何处都被视为至福；任凭苦到什么地步，也没有一个人甘愿就死。财富也是人人渴求的，因为贫穷是煎熬人生的最凄惨的诅咒；至于两情相悦的爱，那自然是人心所能领略的最甜美、最纯净、最神圣的欢乐。」

这话引得科恩一阵激越，脱口高呼：

「哦，神圣的洞府幽暗！哦，神性的黑暗！哦，深不可测的夜之渊薮！这是什么，哦，这是什么！阿塔莫尔啊，你莫非疯了？唉！必是如此。欢乐转昏了你的头脑；你全然神志失常了。你把善唤作恶，把恶唤作善；我们的光是你的暗，我们的暗是你的光。不过你总不至于疯到十足。来，来，我们再往深里看一看。这是怎么说的！且试着召回你的理智。长寿——一条命，还要长长久久！凡是天性健全之人，断没有一个想延年益寿的；至于财富，世上果真会有人真心实意渴求财富么？绝无此事！还有两情相悦的爱！哦，阿塔莫尔，你今天是疯了！你素来古怪，如今可全然失了心智。我不能不爱你，却永远无法懂得你。告诉我，老老实实告诉我：你方才举的这些若还算不得顶坏的，那么在你们眼里，什么才算灾祸？」

他看去全然是一片至诚，且深为动容。我无从懂得他，只能以简截了当的话回答他的问题。

===Original===
“Poverty, sickness, and death,” said I, “are evils; but the worst of all evils is unrequited love.”

At these words the Kohen made a gesture of despair.

“It is impossible to understand this,” said he. “You talk calmly; you have not the air of a madman. If your fellow-countrymen are all like you, then your race is an incomprehensible one. Why, death is the greatest blessing. We all long for it; it is the end of our being. As for riches, they are a curse, abhorred by all. Above all, as to love, we shrink from the thought of requital. Death is our chief blessing, poverty our greatest happiness, and unrequited love the sweetest lot of man.”

All this sounded like the ravings of a lunatic, yet the Kohen was not mad. It seemed also like the mockery of some teasing demon; but the gentle and self-denying Kohen was no teasing demon, and mockery with him was impossible. I was therefore more bewildered than ever at this reiteration of sentiments that were so utterly incomprehensible. He, on the other hand, seemed as astonished at my sentiments and as bewildered, and we could find no common ground on which to meet.

===Chinese===
「贫穷、疾病和死亡，」我说，「都是灾祸；而一切灾祸中最甚的，是单相思。」

听了这话，科恩做了个绝望的手势。

「这实在没法懂得，」他说，「你说得心平气和，又没有疯子的做派。倘若你的同胞个个同你一样，那你们这个种族真是个无从索解的种族了。要知道，死亡是至大的福分。我们人人渴慕它；它是我们此生的归宿。至于财富，那是人人厌憎的诅咒。尤其是说到爱，我们一想到回报便要退缩。死亡是我们的至福，贫穷是我们的至乐，单相思则是人间最甜美的命分。」

这一切听来像疯人的呓语，然而科恩并不疯。它又像某个促狭鬼魅的嘲弄；可这位温良克己的科恩绝非促狭的鬼魅，嘲弄于他是断然不可能的事。于是，面对这一迭连声全然无从索解的论调，我比先前愈加惶惑。而他那一边，对我的论调似乎也同样惊愕、同样惶惑；我们竟寻不出一寸可以彼此相接的地基。

===Original===
“I remember now,” said the Kohen, in a musing tone, “having heard of some strange folk at the Amir, who profess to feel as you say you feel, but no one believes that they are in earnest; for although they may even bring themselves to think that they are in earnest in their professions, yet after all everyone thinks that they are self-deceived. For you see, in the first place, these feelings which you profess are utterly unnatural. We are so made that we cannot help loving death; it is a sort of instinct. We are also created in such a way that we cannot help longing after poverty. The pauper must always, among all men, be the most envied of mortals. Nature, too, has made us such that the passion of love, when it arises, is so vehement, so all-consuming that it must always struggle to avoid requital. This is the reason why, when two people find that they love each other, they always separate and avoid one another for the rest of their lives. This is human nature. We cannot help it; and it is this that distinguishes us from the animals. Why, if men were to feel as you say you feel, they would be mere animals. Animals fear death; animals love to accumulate such things as they prize; animals, when they love, go in pairs, and remain with one another. But man, with his intellect, would not be man if he loved life and desired riches and sought for requited love.”

===Chinese===
「我如今想起来了，」科恩用沉思的声调说，「曾听说阿米尔有若干古怪的族人，自称感受同你说的一模一样，只是谁也不信他们出于真心；因为即便他们自己竟也当真以为那些表白发自肺腑，到头来众人依然认定他们在自欺。要知道，首先，你自陈的这些情感全然违逆天性。我们生就如此，不能不爱死；这是一种本能。我们又受造得不能不渴慕贫穷。在一切人中间，赤贫者永远该是世人最艳羡的。造化又把我们造得如此：爱的激情一旦萌动，便那般炽烈、那般焚身蚀骨，以致必定要竭力逃避回报。正因如此，两个人一旦发觉彼此相爱，总要就此分离，余生彼此回避。这是人类的天性。我们无可如何；也正是这一点，把我们同兽类区别开来。唉，人若当真照你说的那样去感受，那便只是禽兽了。禽兽怕死；禽兽爱积攒它们珍视的东西；禽兽相爱便双双成对，厮守不离。而人呢，既有灵明之性，若爱生、求财、寻两情相悦的爱，便不成其为人了。」

===Original===
I sank back in despair. “You cannot mean all this,” I said.

He threw at me a piteous glance. “What else can you believe or feel?” said he.

“The very opposite. We are so made that we hate and fear death; to us he is the King of Terrors. Poverty is terrible also, since it is associated with want and woe; it is, therefore, natural to man to strive after riches. As to the passion of love, that is so vehement that the first and only thought is requital. Unrequited love is anguish beyond expression﻿—anguish so severe that the heart will often break under it.”

The Kohen clasped his hands in new bewilderment.

===Chinese===
我绝望地向后瘫坐下去。「你不可能是当真的。」我说。

他向我投来一瞥哀怜的目光。「不然你还能信什么、感觉什么呢？」他说。

「恰恰相反。我们生来便憎恶死亡、惧怕死亡；在我们，它是恐怖之王。贫穷也是可怕的，因为它同匮乏与愁苦连在一处；所以人力求财富，乃是天性使然。至于爱的激情，它炽烈到那步田地，头一个也是唯一一个念头，便是求得回应。单相思之苦，痛不可言——那痛往往剧烈得叫人心碎。」

科恩再度攥紧双手，平添一重惶惑。

===Original===
“I cannot understand,” said he. “A madman might imagine that he loved life and desired riches; but as to love, why even a madman could not think of requital, for the very nature of the passion of love is the most utter self-surrender, and a shrinking from all requital; wherefore, the feeling that leads one to desire requital cannot be love. I do not know what it can be﻿—indeed, I never heard of such a thing before, and the annals of the human race make no mention of such a feeling. For what is love? It is the ardent outflow of the whole being﻿—the yearning of one human heart to lavish all its treasures upon another. Love is more than self-denial; it is self-surrender and utter self-abnegation. Love gives all away, and cannot possibly receive anything in return. A requital of love would mean selfishness, which would be self-contradiction. The more one loves, the more he must shrink from requital.”

===Chinese===
「我没法懂，」他说，「疯子或许会想象自己爱生、想望财富；可说到爱，只怕连疯子也想不到回报二字，因为爱的激情，其本性便是全然的舍身相与，是对一切回报的退避；所以，引人渴求回报的那种情感，不可能是爱。它究竟是什么，我不知道——老实说，这样的事我从未听闻，人类的史册也从不曾提到这样一种情感。因为爱是什么？爱是整个生命的炽热奔涌——是一颗人心渴望把自家的珍宝尽数倾泻到另一颗人心上去的渴慕。爱不止于克己；爱是舍身相与，是全然的忘我。爱把一切都给出去，断不能收回任何东西作报偿。爱而求报，便意味着自私；而自私，便是否定爱自身的矛盾。爱得愈深，便愈要避忌回报。」

===Original===
“What!” cried I, “among you do lovers never marry?”

“Lovers marry? Never!”

“Do married people never love one another?”

The Kohen shook his head.

“It unfortunately sometimes happens so,” said he, “and then the result is, of course, distressing. For the children’s sake the parents will often remain with one another, but in many cases they separate. No one can tell the misery that ensues where a husband and wife love one another.”

===Chinese===
「什么！」我叫道，「在你们这里，相爱的人们从不结婚么？」

「相爱的人们结婚？从不！」

「结了婚的人就从不相爱么？」

科恩摇了摇头。

「不幸有时偏有这样的情形，」他说，「那结局自然惨不堪言。为了儿女的缘故，做父母的往往还勉强厮守，但许多时候便分道扬镳了。夫妻二人倘若彼此相爱，随之而来的愁惨，那是谁也没有言语说得尽的。」

===Original===
The conversation grew insupportable. I could not follow the Kohen in what seemed the wildest and maddest flights of fancy that ever were known; so I began to talk of other things, and gradually the Kohen was drawn to speak of his own life. The account which he gave of himself was not one whit less strange than his previous remarks, and for this reason I add it here.

===Chinese===
这场谈话再也无法继续下去了。科恩的种种议论，在我看来尽是有史以来最狂放、最癫狂的奇想，我实在无从跟随；于是我开始谈起别的事情来，渐渐地，科恩被引得讲起他自己的身世。他这番自述，比起先前的议论丝毫不减其离奇，为此我把它记在这里。

===Original===
“I was born,” said he, “in the most enviable of positions. My father and mother were among the poorest in the land. Both died when I was a child, and I never saw them. I grew up in the open fields and public caverns, along with the most esteemed paupers. But, unfortunately for me, there was something wanting in my natural disposition. I loved death, of course, and poverty, too, very strongly; but I did not have that eager and energetic passion which is so desirable, nor was I watchful enough over my blessed estate of poverty. Surrounded as I was by those who were only too ready to take advantage of my ignorance or want of vigilance, I soon fell into evil ways, and gradually, in spite of myself, I found wealth pouring in upon me. Designing men succeeded in winning my consent to receive their possessions; and so I gradually fell away from that lofty position in which I was born. I grew richer and richer. My friends warned me, but in vain. I was too weak to resist; in fact, I lacked moral fibre, and had never learned how to say ‘No.’ So I went on, descending lower and lower in the scale of being. I became a capitalist, an *Athon*, a general officer, and finally Kohen.

“At length, on one eventful day, I learned that one of my associates had by a long course of reckless folly become the richest man in all the country. He had become Athon, *Melek*, and at last *Kohen Gadol*. It was a terrible shock, but I trust a salutary one. I at once resolved to reform. That resolution I have steadily kept, and have at least saved myself from descending any lower. It is true, I can hardly hope to become what I once was. It is only too easy to grow rich; and, you know, poverty once forfeited can never return except in rare instances. I have, however, succeeded in getting rid of most of my wealth, chiefly through the fortunate advent of Almah and afterward of yourself. This, I confess, has been my salvation. Neither of you had any scruples about accepting what was bestowed, and so I did not feel as though I was doing you any wrong in giving you all I had in the world. Most of the people of this city have taken advantage of your extraordinary indifference to wealth, and have made themselves paupers at your expense. I had already become your slave, and had received the promise of being elevated to the rank of scullion in the cavern of the Mista Kosek. But now, since this event of your love for Almah, I hope to gain far more. I am almost certain of being made a pauper, and I think I can almost venture to hope some day for the honor of a public death.”

===Chinese===
「我降生在最教人艳羡不过的地位上，」他说，「我的父母位列全国最赤贫的人。二老在我幼年便双双亡故，我从未见过他们。我在旷野和公共窟穴里长大，同最受敬重的赤贫者们一处厮混。可是于我不幸的是，我的天性里缺了些东西。爱死之心，我固然有，爱贫之心也很强烈；但我缺少那种人人称羡的热切而奋发的激情，对我那份赤贫的至福也看守得不够严。我周围尽是些巴不得趁我无知或疏于防范时占便宜的人，我不久便堕入了邪路，身不由己地眼看财富滚滚而来。奸猾之徒终于诱得我点头，收下了他们的财产；于是我从降生时那崇高的地位上节节跌落。我变得愈来愈富。朋友们劝诫我，全是徒劳。我太软弱，抵不住；说实在的，我缺乏道德的筋骨，从来学不会说一个『不』字。我就这样一路走下去，在生存的阶梯上愈跌愈低。我成了资本家，成了阿通，做了将官，最后竟成了科恩。」

「后来，在一个要紧的日子里，我得知我的一位故交，经过长年累月的胡作非为，竟成了全国首富。他做过阿通，做过美勒克，最后竟当上了科恩·加多尔。这于我是可怕的一击，但我信它是一记有益的警钟。我当即立志改过。这一志向我始终坚守不渝，至少使自己没有再往下跌。诚然，我很难指望恢复从前的身份了。人要变富，实在太容易；而你知道，赤贫一旦失守，除了极罕见的机缘，是再也要不回来的。不过，我总算甩脱了大部分财富，这主要是托了阿尔玛、随后又托了你的洪福。我承认，这是我的得救。你们二位收受馈赠从无半分芥蒂，于是我把我全部家当都给了你们，也不觉得是害了你们。本城的大多数人都沾了你们对财富异乎寻常的淡漠的光，借你们之力当上了赤贫者。我呢，早已做了你们的奴隶，还得了擢升的许诺，要升做米斯塔·科塞克洞窟里的厨下杂役。可如今，既然出了你与阿尔玛相爱这桩事，我指望得到的就远不止于此了。我几乎笃定能受封为赤贫者，而且我想，我几乎可以斗胆指望，有朝一日荣获公开处死的殊荣。」

===Original===
To such a story I had nothing to say. It was sheer madness; yet it was terribly suggestive, and showed how utterly hopeless was my effort to secure the assistance of such a man toward my escape from death.

“A public death!” I said, grimly. “That will be very fortunate! And do you think that you will gain the dignity of being eaten up afterward?”

The Kohen shook his head in all seriousness.

“Oh no,” said he; “that would be far beyond my deserts. That is an honor which is only bestowed upon the most distinguished.”

===Chinese===
对这样一番自述，我无话可说。这纯属疯狂；然而它给人的暗示可怖之至：要想靠这样一个人相助而逃脱死亡，我的指望是何等彻底地断绝了。

「公开处死！」我冷冷地说，「那可真是洪福不浅！你还指望事后能荣获被吃掉的体面么？」

科恩一本正经地摇了摇头。

「哦，不，」他说，「那可远远超过我的功德。那份殊荣，是只授予最杰出的人物的。」

## XVI The Kosekin / 十六、科塞金人

===Original===
These people call themselves the “Kosekin.” Their chief characteristic, or, at least, their most prominent one, is their love of darkness, which perhaps is due to their habit of dwelling in caves. Another feeling, equally strong and perhaps connected with this, is their love of death and dislike of life. This is visible in many ways, and affects all their character. It leads to a passionate self-denial, an incessant effort to benefit others at their own expense. Each one hates life and longs for death. He, therefore, hates riches, and all things that are associated with life.

Among the Kosekin everyone makes perpetual efforts to serve others, which, however, are perpetually baffled by the unselfishness of these others. People thus spend years in trying to overreach one another, so as to make others richer than themselves. In a race each one tries to keep behind; but as this leads to confusion, there is then a universal effort for each one to be first, so as to put his neighbor in the honorable position of the rear. It is the same way in a hunt. Each one presses forward, so as to honor his companion by leaving him behind. Instead of injuring, everyone tries to benefit his neighbor. When one has been benefited by another, he is filled with a passion which may be called Kosekin revenge﻿—namely, a sleepless and vehement desire to bestow some adequate and corresponding benefit on the other. Feuds are thus kept up among families and wars among nations. For no one is willing to accept from another any kindness, any gift, or any honor, and all are continually on the watch to prevent themselves from being overreached in this way. Those who are less watchful than others are overwhelmed with gifts by designing men, who wish to attain to the pauper class. The position of Almah and myself illustrates this. Our ignorance of the blessings and honors of poverty led us to receive whatever was offered us. Taking advantage of our innocence and ignorance, the whole city thereupon proceeded to bestow their property upon us, and all became paupers through our fortunate arrival.

===Chinese===
这族人自称「科塞金人」。他们最主要的特征——至少是最显著的特征——便是崇暗，这或许源自他们穴居洞窟的习性。还有一种感情与此同样强烈，或许也与之相关，便是尚死而厌生。这在许多方面都看得出来，并浸染了他们的全部性情。它引出一种炽烈的克己，一种不计己利、孜孜不倦造福他人的努力。人人皆厌生而求死。因此他憎恶财富，也憎恶一切与生相系之物。

在科塞金人中间，人人都在不停地设法服侍他人，而这些努力又无时不被他人的无私所挫败。于是人们经年累月地彼此算计，务求使旁人比自己更富。赛跑时人人争相殿后；但这会引起混乱，于是众人又一齐力争抢先，好把左邻右舍推上殿后那光荣的位置。围猎时也是一样：人人奋力向前，以把同伴抛在身后为荣。非但无人加害于邻，反倒人人设法施惠于邻。一个人受了旁人的恩惠，心中便燃起一种不妨称作「科塞金式复仇」的激情——即一种寤寐不忘、炽烈难遏的欲望，务求回赠对方一份相当而对等的恩惠。家族间的世仇与邦国间的战事，便由此绵延不绝。因为谁也不肯受旁人半点好意、半件馈赠、半分荣宠，人人都时刻提防着不被旁人这样占了便宜。那些提防得不如旁人周密的人，便被处心积虑之徒用馈赠压得抬不起头来——此辈正是要借此跻身赤贫者之列。我与阿尔玛的处境正好印证了这一点。我们不知贫穷原是福分与荣耀，旁人送什么便收什么。于是全城的人趁着我们天真无知，争相把家产倾赠给我们，我们的侥幸到来，竟使满城人都成了赤贫者。
===Original===
No one ever injures another unless by accident, and when this occurs it affords the highest joy to the injured party.
