Even this, however, had not changed the sentiments of the condemned, and I learned that they were laboring incessantly, notwithstanding their severe punishment, to disseminate their peculiar doctrines. These were formulated as follows:

1. A man should not love others better than himself.
2. Life is not an evil to be got rid of.
3. Other things are to be preferred to death.
4. Poverty is not the best state for man.
5. Unrequited love is not the greatest happiness.
6. Lovers may sometimes marry.
7. To serve is not more honorable than to command.
8. Defeat is not more glorious than victory.
9. To save a life should not be regarded as a criminal offence.
10. The paupers should be forced to take a certain amount of wealth, to relieve the necessities of the rich.
===Chinese===
大祭司鼓吹：自私才是生活的真正法则，没有它，任何国家都休想兴旺。持类似见解的尚有寥寥数人，但都受尽大众的极度鄙夷，还要领受法律最严厉的惩处：他们个个被赋予巨额的财富，被迫过着极尽豪奢的生活，拥有浩大的扈从，在政治与宗教中执掌最高的权柄。然而即便如此，也没能改变这些罪人的信念；我得知，他们尽管受着严惩，仍在不懈地劳作，传播他们那套独特的教义。教义成文如下：

1. 人不应爱他人胜过爱自己。
2. 生命并非亟须摆脱的祸害。
3. 有比死亡更可取的事物。
4. 贫穷并非人的最佳境况。
5. 单相思并非至福。
6. 恋人有时可以成婚。
7. 服侍人并不比指挥人更光荣。
8. 败绩并不比胜利更荣耀。
9. 救人一命不应视为罪行。
10. 应当强制赤贫者领受一定数量的财富，以纾富人的困厄。
===Original===
These articles were considered both by the Kohen Gadol and by Layelah to be remarkable for their audacity, and were altogether too advanced for mention by any except the chosen few. With the multitude he had to deal differently, and had to work his way by concealing his opinions. He had made a great conspiracy, in which he was still engaged, and had gained immense numbers of adherents by allowing them to give him their whole wealth. Through his assistance many Athons and Kohens and Meleks had become artisans laborers, and even paupers; but all were bound by him to the strictest secrecy. If anyone should divulge the secret, it would be ruin to him and to many others; for they would at once be punished by the bestowal of the extremest wealth, by degradation to the rank of rulers and commanders, and by the severest rigors of luxury, power, splendor, and magnificence known among the Kosekin. Overwhelmed thus with the cares of government, crushed under the weight of authority and autocratic rule, surrounded by countless slaves all ready to die for them, their lives would be embittered and their punishment would be more than they could bear. But the philosophic Kohen Gadol dared all these punishments, and pursued his way calmly and pertinaciously.

Nothing surprised the Kohen Gadol so much as the manner in which I received his confidences. He half expected to startle me by his boldness, but was himself confounded by my words. I told him that in my country self was the chief consideration, self-preservation the law of nature; death the King of Terrors; wealth the object of universal search, poverty the worst of evils; unrequited love nothing less than anguish and despair; to command others the highest glory; victory, honor; defeat, intolerable shame; and other things of the same sort, all of which sounded in his ears, as he said, with such tremendous force that they were like peals of thunder. He shook his head despondently; he could not believe that such views as mine could ever be attained to among the Kosekin. But Layelah was bolder, and with all a woman’s impetuosity grasped at my fullest meaning and held it firm.
===Chinese===
这些条文，在大祭司与莱拉看来已是大胆得惊人，除寥寥入选的心腹之外，任谁也提不得——实在是太超前了。对大众，他只好另有一套做法，把见解掩藏起来，迂回行事。他布下了一场浩大的密谋，至今仍在其中经营；他借着准许信徒把全部家财相赠，纠合了为数极众的同道。经他援手，许多阿通、科恩与美勒克做了匠人、劳作者，甚至做了赤贫者；但所有人都被他以最严酷的缄默约束缚定。一旦有人泄露机密，他本人与许多旁人便都要遭殃：他们将立即受罚——被授予登峰造极的财富，被贬入统治者与统帅的品级，领受科塞金人所知的最严酷的豪奢、权势、富丽与堂皇。政务的烦累压得他们透不过气，权柄与专断统治的重负将他们碾碎，身边还围着无数甘愿替他们去死的奴隶——他们的日子将被苦水泡透，惩罚将重得叫他们担当不起。然而这位哲人大祭司甘冒这一切惩罚，沉着而顽韧地走自己的路。

最叫大祭司吃惊的，莫过我听他倾吐心腹之谈时的神色。他原指望自己的大胆多半会把我吓住，不料反倒被我的话说得怔住了。我告诉他：在我的国度，自我是头一份的考量，自保是天性的法则；死亡是恐怖之王；财富是举世追逐的目标，贫穷是诸恶之最；单相思无异于绞痛与绝望；指挥他人是无上的光荣；胜利是荣耀，败绩是难堪的奇耻；还有诸如此类的许多话——据他自己说，这一切在他耳中字字如雷霆炸响，声势骇人。他垂头丧气地摇着头；他不信像我这样的见解，在科塞金人中间终究会有实现的一日。莱拉却更大胆，以女子全部的锐气，一把攫住我话里最彻底的含义，牢牢握定。
===Original===
“He is right,” said Layelah﻿—“the heaven-born Atam-or. He shall be our teacher. The rich shall be esteemed, the poor shall be downtrodden; to rule over others shall be glorious, to serve shall be base; victory shall be an honor, defeat a shame; selfishness, self-seeking, luxury, and indulgence shall be virtues; poverty, want, and squalor shall be things of abhorrence and contempt.”

The face of Layelah glowed with enthusiasm as she said these words, and I saw in her a daring, intrepid, and high-hearted woman, full of a woman’s headlong impetuosity and disregard of consequences. In me she saw one who seemed to her like a prophet and teacher of a new order of things, and her whole soul responded to the principles which I announced. It required immense strength of mind and firmness of soul to separate herself from the prevalent sentiment of her nation; and though nature had done much for her in giving her a larger portion of original selfishness than was common to her people, still she was a child of the Kosekin, and her daring was all the more remarkable. And so she went further than her father, and adopted my extreme views when he shrank back, and dared more unflinchingly the extremest rigors of the national law, and all that the Kosekin could inflict in the way of wealth, luxury, supreme command, palatial abodes, vast retinues of slaves, and the immense degradation of the queenly office.
===Chinese===
「他说得对，」莱拉道，「这位天降的阿塔莫尔。他该做我们的老师。富人将受尊崇，穷人将遭践踏；统治他人将是光荣，服侍人将是卑贱；胜利将是荣耀，败绩将是耻辱；自私、营己、奢靡、放纵，都将成为美德；贫穷、匮乏、污秽，都将成为可憎可鄙之物。」

说这些话时，莱拉的面庞焕发着热诚；我在她身上看到一个大胆、无畏、心气高迈的女子，满怀着女子那种不顾一切后果的一往直前。在她眼里，我仿佛是一位新秩序的先知与导师，她的整个灵魂都在应和我宣布的这些原则。要从国人普遍的情感中把自己抽拔出来，需要绝大的心力与灵魂的坚毅；虽然造化待她不薄，赋给她的那份天生自私比族人惯常的更丰厚，但她终究是科塞金的孩子，她的胆气也因此愈见了不起。于是她比父亲走得更远：父亲退缩之处，她采纳了我那些极端的见解；国法最严酷的刑罚，她毫不打颤地承当；科塞金人所能施加的一切——财富、奢靡、至高的统辖、宫殿般的居所、成群成队的奴隶，以及女王之职那无边的贬辱——她都更毫不畏缩地敢于领受。
===Original===
I spoke to her in a warning voice about her rashness.

“Oh,” said she, “I have counted the cost, and am ready to accept all that they can inflict. I embrace the good cause, and will not give it up﻿—no, not even if they could increase my wealth a thousandfold, and sentence me to live a hundred seasons. I can bear their utmost inflictions of wealth, power, magnificence; I could even bear being condemned to live forever in the light. Oh, my friend, it is the conviction of right and the support of conscience that strengthens one to bear the greatest evils that man can inflict.”
===Chinese===
我用告诫的口吻，同她说起她的孟浪。

「哦，」她说，「这代价我早掂量过了，他们能加的，我全预备承受。我拥抱这桩正义的事业，决不放弃——不，哪怕他们能把我的财富添上一千倍，判我在光明里再活一百个季，我也不放弃。财富、权势、豪奢，他们顶重的刑罚我受得住；哪怕判我永生永世住在光明里，我也受得住。哦，我的朋友，正是对正义的信念、良心的支撑，给人力量去承受人类所能加身的最重祸殃。」
===Original===
From these words it was evident to me that Layelah was a true child of the Kosekin; for though she was of advanced sentiments she still used the language of her people, and spoke of the punishments of the law as though they were punishments in reality. Now, to me and to Almah these so-called punishments seemed rewards.

It was impossible for me to avoid feeling a very strong regard for this enthusiastic and beautiful girl; all the more, indeed, because she evinced such an undisguised admiration for me. She evidently considered me some superior being, from some superior race; and although my broken and faulty way of speaking the language was something of a trial, still she seemed to consider every word I uttered as a maxim of the highest wisdom. The tritest of truths, the commonest of platitudes, the most familiar of proverbs or old saws current among us were eagerly seized by Layelah, and accepted as truths almost divine﻿—as new doctrines for the guidance of the human race. These she would discuss with me; she would put them into better and more striking language, and ask for my opinion. Then she would write them down.
===Chinese===
从这些话里我听得分明：莱拉终究是科塞金人真正的孩子。她见解尽管超前，用的却仍是族人的语言，说起国法的惩罚来，倒仿佛那些惩罚真就是惩罚似的。而在我与阿尔玛看来，这些所谓的惩罚，分明全是赏赐。

对这位热诚而美丽的姑娘，我难以不生出一股深厚的情意；而她对我流露的那份毫无掩饰的倾慕，愈发加深了这份情意。她分明把我当作某种更高等的生命，来自某个更高等的族类；我讲他们的话虽说结结巴巴、破绽百出，不免叫人为难，她却似乎把我吐出的每一个字，都当作至高智慧的格言。那些最俗常的常理、最平庸的老生常谈、我们中间流传最熟的谚语古话，莱拉都急切地一把抓住，当作近乎神启的真理——当作指引人类的新教义。这些她要同我逐条推究，把它们改成更好、更警辟的言语，再请我评断；然后她便一一记下。
===Original===
For the Kosekin knew the art of writing. They had an alphabet of their own, which was at once simple and very scientific. There were no vowels, but only consonant sounds, the vowels being supplied in reading, just as if one should write the words *fthr* or *dghtr*, and read them “father” and “daughter.” Their letters were as follows: *P*, *K*, *T*, *B*, *G*, *D*, *F*, *Ch*, *Th*, *M*, *L*, *N*, *S*, *H*, *R*. There were also three others, which have no equivalents in English.
===Chinese===
因为科塞金人本就通晓书写之术。他们有自己的字母，既简单，又极合学理。其中没有元音，只有辅音，元音在诵读时自行补足——就像有人把词写成 fthr 或 dghtr，读作 father（父亲）、daughter（女儿）。他们的字母如下：P、K、T、B、G、D、F、Ch、Th、M、L、N、S、H、R。另有三个，英文中没有与之相当的音。
===Original===
It soon became evident to me that Layelah had a complete ascendancy over her father; that she was not only the Malca of the *amir*, but the presiding spirit and the chief administrative genius of the whole nation of the Kosekin. She seemed to be a new Semiramis﻿—one who might revolutionize an empire and introduce a new order of things. Such, indeed, was her high ambition, and she plainly avowed it to me; but what was more, she frankly informed me that she regarded me as a Heaven-sent teacher﻿—as one who in this darkness could tell her of the nations of light﻿—who could instruct her in the wisdom of other and greater races, and help her to accomplish her grand designs.

As for Almah, she seemed quite beneath the notice of the aspiring Layelah. She never noticed her, she never spoke of her, and she always made her visits to me after Almah had gone.
===Chinese===
我不久便看清：莱拉对父亲握着全然的主导之权；她不但是阿米尔的玛尔卡，更是整个科塞金民族的主持之魂与行政奇才。她仿佛是一位新出的塞米拉米斯（Semiramis）——那种足以倾覆一个帝国、开出新局面的人物。这确乎正是她的宏愿，她也向我坦然自认；更有甚者，她直截了当地告诉我：她把我看作上天遣来的老师——在这黑暗之中，唯有我能对她讲说光明的诸国——能把别的、更伟大族类的智慧教给她，助她成就宏图。

至于阿尔玛，在这位志存高远的莱拉眼里，似乎全然不值一顾。她从不理睬阿尔玛，也从不起提她；她的来访，总在阿尔玛离去之后。

## XX The Dark Maiden Layelah / 二十、黑眸少女莱拉

===Original===
Layelah at length began to make pointed remarks about Almah.

“She loves you,” said she, “and you love her. How is it that you do not give each other up?”

“I would die rather than give up Almah,” said I.

Layelah smiled. “That sounds strange to the Kosekin,” said she, “for here to give up your love and to die are both esteemed the greatest possible blessings. But Almah should give you up. It is the women with us who make the beginning. Women generally fall in love first, and it is expected that they will tell their love first. The delicacy of a woman’s feelings makes this natural, for if a man tells his love to a woman who does not love him, it shocks her modesty; while if a woman tells a man, he has no modesty to shock.”

===Chinese===
莱拉终于开始就阿尔玛说出些语带机锋的话来。

「她爱你，」她说，「你也爱她。你们两个，怎么竟谁也不肯让出谁呢？」

「我宁死也不让出阿尔玛。」我说。

莱拉嫣然一笑。「这话在科塞金人听来未免奇怪，」她说，「因为在我们这里，让出所爱与舍身赴死，同被视为至大的福祉。不过，该让出你的是阿尔玛。在我们这里，先行一步的向来是女人。女人总是先堕入情网，人们也指望她们先行吐露衷情。女子情思纤柔，这原是顺理成章的事：倘若一个男人向一个并不爱他的女人倾诉爱情，便惊犯了她的娇羞；而一个女人向男人倾诉，男人却没有什么娇羞可惊犯的。」

===Original===
“That is strange,” said I; “but suppose the man does not love the woman?”

“Why, no woman wants to be loved; she only wants to love.”

At this I felt somewhat bewildered.

“That,” said Layelah, “is unrequited love, which is the chief blessing here, though for my part I am a philosopher, and would wish when I love to be loved in return.”

===Chinese===
「这确是奇谈，」我说；「可倘若那男人并不爱那女人呢？」

「嗐，没有哪个女人要人爱她；她只要自己去爱人。」

听了这话，我不免有些茫然。

「那便是，」莱拉说，「单相思——这正是我们这里头一等的福气；不过说到我自己，我是个哲学家，我若爱了人，还是盼着人家回报的。」

===Original===
“And then,” said I, “if so, would you give up your lover, in accordance with the custom of your country?”

Layelah’s dark eyes rested on me for a moment with a glance of intense earnestness and profound meaning. She drew a long breath, and then said, in a low, tremulous voice,

“Never!”

===Chinese===
「那么，」我说，「果真如此，你可愿意依你们邦国的风俗，让出你的情人么？」

莱拉那双黑黝黝的眼睛在我身上停留了片刻，那目光恳切之至，又深意无穷。她深深吸了一口气，随即用低微而颤抖的声音说道：

「决不！」

===Original===
Layelah was constantly with me, and at length used to come at an earlier time, when Almah was present. Her manner toward Almah was full of the usual Kosekin courtesy and gracious cordiality. She was still intent upon learning from me the manners, customs, and principles of action of the race to which I belonged. She had an insatiable thirst for knowledge, and her curiosity extended to all of those great inventions which are the wonder of Christendom. Locomotives and steamboats were described to her under the names of “horses of fire” and “ships of fire”; printing was “letters of power”; the electric telegraph, “messages of lightning”; the organ, “lute of giants,” and so on. Yet, in spite of the eagerness with which she made her inquiries, and the diligence with which she noted all down, I could see that there was in her mind something lying beneath it all﻿—a far more earnest purpose, and a far more personal one, than the pursuit of useful knowledge.

Layelah was watchful of Almah; she seemed studying her to see how far this woman of another race differed from the Kosekin. She would often turn from me and talk with Almah for a long time, questioning her about her people and their ways. Almah’s manner was somewhat reserved, and it was rendered somewhat more so from the fact that her mind was always full of the prospect of our impending doom. Each *jom* as it came and went brought us nearer to that awful time, and the hour was surely coming when we should be taken to the outer square and to the top of the pyramid of sacrifice.

Once Layelah sat for some time silent and involved in thought. At length she began to speak to me.

===Chinese===
莱拉时时陪伴着我，到后来索性来得更早，阿尔玛在座时也到场。她对阿尔玛的态度，尽是科塞金人惯有的礼数与和蔼可亲。她仍一门心思要向我讨教我所属种族的仪节、风俗与行事为人的准则。她求知若渴，永不餍足，好奇心遍及那些堪称基督教世界奇观的伟大发明。火车头与轮船，我讲给她听时，名之曰「火马」与「火船」；印刷术是「威力之文」；电报是「闪电之信」；管风琴是「巨人的鲁特琴」，诸如此类。然而，任凭她发问如何殷切、笔录如何勤勉，我总看得出，这一切之下，她心底另有所蓄——那志图远比研求有用之学来得恳切，也远比之更切于她自身。

莱拉留意着阿尔玛；她像是在揣摩她，要看出这个异族女子究竟与科塞金人相异到何等地步。她时常撇下我，同阿尔玛长谈，询问她的族人及其风习。阿尔玛的态度略嫌矜持，而心里总悬着我们迫在眉睫的厄运，便愈发矜持了。约姆一个接一个地来、又一个接一个地去，把我们步步带近那可怖的时辰；时候总要到来的——我们将被带到外面的广场上，带上献祭金字塔的顶巅。

有一回，莱拉默坐了半晌，沉在思索里。终于，她向我开了口。

===Original===
“Almah,” said she, “is very different from us. She loves you and you love her. She ought to give you up. Almah, you ought to give up Atam-or, since you love him.”

Almah looked confused, and made some reply to the effect that she belonged to a different race with different customs.

“But you should follow our customs. You are one of us now. You can easily find another who will take him.”

Almah threw a piteous glance at me and said nothing.

“I,” said Layelah, “will take him.”

===Chinese===
「阿尔玛，」她说，「同我们大不相同。她爱你，你也爱她。照理她该让出你才是。阿尔玛，你既然爱他，就该把阿塔莫尔让出来。」

阿尔玛面有窘色，回了几句话，大意是自己属于另一个种族，自有另一套风俗。

「可你该遵行我们的风俗才是。你如今已是我们中间的一人了。你不难另寻一个肯收留他的人。」

阿尔玛向我投来哀怜的一瞥，一言不发。

「我，」莱拉说，「便肯收留他。」

===Original===
She spoke these words with an air of magnanimity, as though putting it in the light of a favor to Almah; but Almah did not make any reply, and after some silence Layelah spoke of something else.

Not long after we were alone together, and Layelah returned to the subject. She referred to Almah’s want of sympathy with the manners of the Kosekin, and asserted that she ought to aim after a separation.

“I love her,” said I, with great warmth, “and will never give her up.”

“But she must give you up; it is the woman’s place to take the first step. I should be willing to take you.”

===Chinese===
她说这话时带着一派宽宏大量的气度，仿佛这是施与阿尔玛的恩惠似的；阿尔玛却没有答言，默然片刻之后，莱拉便谈起别的来了。

不久之后，房中只剩我们两个，莱拉又回到了这个题目上。她说起阿尔玛对科塞金人风习的格格不入，断言她应当志在求一场「分离」。

「我爱她，」我很激动地说，「永远也不让出她。」

「可她必须让出你；先行一步本是我们女人的本分。我倒乐意收留你。」

===Original===
As Layelah said this she looked at me very earnestly, as if anxious to see how I accepted this offer. It was for me a most embarrassing moment. I loved Almah, but Layelah also was most agreeable, and I liked her very much; indeed, so much so that I could not bear to say anything that might hurt her feelings. Among all the Kosekin there was not one who was not infinitely inferior to her in my eyes. Still, I loved Almah, and I told her so again, thinking that in this way I might repel her without giving offence.

But Layelah was quite ready with her reply.

“If you love Almah,” said she, “that is the very reason why you should marry me.”

This made me feel more embarrassed than ever.

===Chinese===
莱拉说这话时，眼睛恳切地望着我，仿佛急欲看看我如何领受这份美意。这于我真是万分难堪的一刻。我爱阿尔玛，可莱拉也这般叫人心悦，我是很喜欢她的；喜欢到什么地步呢——竟不忍说出半句可能伤她心的话。遍数科塞金人，在我眼里没有一个不比她逊色万万。然而我终究爱的是阿尔玛，于是我又把这层意思说了出来，心想这样或许既能回绝她，又不至于开罪于她。

不料莱拉的答话早已成竹在胸。

「你若是爱阿尔玛，」她说，「那正是你该娶我的缘故。」

这一下，我比先前更窘了。

===Original===
I stammered something about my own feelings﻿—the manners and customs of my race﻿—and the fear that I had of acting against my own principles. “Besides,” I added, “I’m afraid it would make you unhappy.”

“Oh no,” said Layelah, briskly; “on the contrary, it would make me very happy indeed.”

I began to be more and more aghast at this tremendous frankness, and was utterly at a loss what to say.

“My father,” continued Layelah, “is different from the other Kosekin, and so am I. I seek requital for love, and do not think it an evil.”

===Chinese===
我结结巴巴，语无伦次，说了些自己的心意呀——我族的仪俗呀——又说我怕违背自己的操守之类的话。「况且，」我补上一句，「我怕这会让你不幸的。」

「嗐，才不会呢，」莱拉爽利地答道；「恰恰相反，那会叫我快活得很哩。」

面对这惊人的坦率，我愈发瞠目结舌，全然不知说什么才好。

「我父亲，」莱拉接着说，「就与旁的科塞金人不同，我也是如此。我求爱的回报，并不以为那是什么恶事。」

===Original===
A sudden thought now suggested itself, and I caught at it as a last resort.

“You have,” said I, “some lover among the Kosekin. Why do you not marry him?”

Layelah smiled.

“I have no lover that I love,” said she, “among the Kosekin.”

My feeble effort was thus a miserable failure. I was about saying something concerning the Kosekin alphabet or something else of an equally appropriate nature, when she prevented me.

===Chinese===
这时我心里忽地闪出一个念头，慌忙抓住它，权当最后一策。

「你在科塞金人里，」我说，「总有位情人吧。你何不嫁给他呢？」

莱拉嫣然一笑。

「我在科塞金人当中，」她说，「并没有我爱的心上人。」

我这点无力的挣扎就此惨败。我正想谈些科塞金人的字母、或是别的同样合宜得体的话头，她却不等我开口。

===Original===
“Atam-or,” said she, in a low voice.

“Layelah,” said I, with my mind full of confusion.

“I love you!”

She sat looking at me with her beautiful face all aglow her dark eyes fixed on mine with an intense and eager gaze. I looked at her and said not one single word. Layelah was the first to break the awkward silence.

===Chinese===
「阿塔莫尔。」她低声唤道。

「莱拉。」我应道，心里乱作一团。

「我爱你！」

她坐在那里望着我，俏丽的脸庞焕发着光彩，一双黑眸凝定在我的眼睛上，目光灼灼，热切逼人。我望着她，一个字也说不出来。这难堪的沉默，终究还是莱拉先打破的。

===Original===
“You love Almah, Atam-or; but say, do you not love me? You smile at me, you meet me always when I come with warm greetings, and you seem to enjoy yourself in my society. Say, Atam-or, do you not love me?”

This was a perilous and a tremendous moment. The fact is, I did like Layelah very much indeed, and I wanted to tell her so; but my ignorance of the language did not allow me to observe those nice distinctions of meaning which exist between the words “like” and “love.” I knew no other word than the one Kosekin word meaning “love,” and could not think of any meaning “like.” It was, therefore, a very trying position for me.

“Dear Layelah,” said I, floundering and stammering in my confusion, “I love you; I﻿—”

But here I was interrupted without waiting for any further words; the beautiful creature flung her arms around me and clung to me with a fond embrace. As for me, I was utterly confounded, bewildered, and desperate. I thought of my darling Almah, whom alone I loved. It seemed at that moment as though I was not only false to her, but as if I was even endangering her life. My only thought now was to clear up my meaning.

===Chinese===
「你爱阿尔玛，阿塔莫尔；可是你说，你就不爱我么？你朝我笑，我一到来，你总笑脸相迎、殷殷问暖，同我在一起你似乎也很快活。说呀，阿塔莫尔，你到底爱不爱我？」

这是一个凶险而又非同小可的时刻。实情是，我确实非常喜欢莱拉，也正想这样告诉她；怎奈我对这语言所知有限，拿捏不住「喜欢」与「爱」这两个字眼之间那些精微的分际。科塞金语里，我知道的只有一个意为「爱」的词，怎么也想不出一个意为「喜欢」的词来。因此，这于我实在是一个极其难堪的处境。

「亲爱的莱拉，」我说，窘迫得张皇失措、期期艾艾，「我爱你；我——」

可不等我再说下去，话就被人截住了；这丽人张开双臂扑住我，痴痴地紧紧搂住了我。至于我，简直是方寸大乱，又惊又惑，走投无路。我想起我心爱的阿尔玛，我唯一所爱的人。那一刻，我仿佛不只是对她负心，简直还是在拿她的性命冒险。此刻我一心想的，就是把我的本意剖白清楚。

===Original===
“Dear Layelah,” said I, as I sat with her arms around me, and with my own around her slender waist, “I do not want to hurt your feelings.”

“Oh, Atam-or! oh, my love! never, never did I know such bliss as this.”

Here again I was overwhelmed, but I still persisted in my effort.

“Dear Layelah,” said I, “I love Almah most dearly and most tenderly.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的莱拉，」我说——我坐在那里，被她双臂搂着，自己的一条手臂也环着她纤细的腰身——「我不愿伤你的心。」

「啊，阿塔莫尔！啊，我的爱人！我从来没有、从来没有尝过这样的幸福。」

这一下我又被击垮了，但我仍旧不肯罢休，继续竭力剖白。

「亲爱的莱拉，」我说，「我爱阿尔玛，爱得至深至切。」

===Original===
“Oh, Atam-or, why speak of that? I know it well. And so by our Kosekin law you give her up; among us, lovers never marry. So you take me, your own Layelah, and you will have me for your bride; and my love for you is ten thousand times stronger than that of the cold and melancholy Almah. She may marry my papa.”

This suggestion filled me with dismay.

“Oh no,” said I. “Never, never will I give up Almah!”

“Certainly not,” said Layelah; “you do not give her up﻿—she gives you up.”

===Chinese===
「啊，阿塔莫尔，提这个做什么？我心里明白得很。这么着，照我们科塞金的法律，你便让出她了；我们这里，相爱的人从不结亲。于是你收下我，收下你自家的莱拉，娶我做新娘；而我对你的爱，比那冷冰冰、惨戚戚的阿尔玛要强烈一万倍呢。她可以嫁给我爸爸。」

这个提议吓得我魂飞魄散。

「啊，不行，」我说。「我永远、永远不让出阿尔玛！」

「那自然，」莱拉说；「不是你让出她——是她让出你。」

===Original===
“She never will,” said I.

“Oh yes,” said Layelah; “I will tell her that you wish it.”

“I do not wish it,” said I. “I love her, and will never give her up.”

“It’s all the same,” said Layelah. “You cannot marry her at all. No one will marry you. You and Almah are victims and the State has given you the matchless honor of death. Common people who love one another may marry if they choose, and take the punishment which the law assigns but illustrious victims who love cannot marry, and so, my Atam-or, you have only me.”

===Chinese===
「她永远不会的。」我说。

「哦，会的，」莱拉说；「我去告诉她，就说这是你的心愿。」

「我没有这个心愿，」我说。「我爱她，永远也不让出她。」

「横竖是一回事，」莱拉说。「你根本娶不成她。没有人会替你们成亲。你和阿尔玛是钦定的人牲，邦国已把死亡这份无与伦比的尊荣赐给了你们。寻常百姓若两情相悦，尽可自行成婚，再自去领受法律所判的刑罚；可钟情的显贵人牲却不得成婚。所以呀，我的阿塔莫尔，你眼下只有我一个。」

===Original===
I need not say that all this was excessively embarrassing. I was certainly fond of Layelah, and liked her too much to hurt her feelings. Had I been one of the Kosekin I might perhaps have managed better; but being a European, a man of the Aryan race﻿—being such, and sitting there with the beautiful Layelah lavishing all her affections upon me﻿—why, it stands to reason that I could not have the heart to wound her feelings in any way. I was taken at an utter disadvantage. Never in my life had I heard of women taking the initiative. Layelah had proposed to me, she would not listen to refusal, and I had not the heart to wound her. I had made all the fight I could by persisting in asserting my love for Almah, but all my assertions were brushed lightly aside as trivial things.

Let any gentleman put himself in my situation, and ask himself what he would do. What would he do if such a thing could happen to him at home? But there such a thing could not happen, and so there is no use in supposing an impossible case. At any rate I think I deserve sympathy. Who could keep his presence of mind under such circumstances? With us a young lady who loves one man can easily repel another suitor; but here it was very different, for how could I repel Layelah? Could I turn upon her and say “Unhand me”? Could I say “Away! I am another’s”? Of course I couldn’t; and what’s worse, if I had said such things Layelah would have smiled me down into silence. The fact is, it doesn’t do for women to take the initiative﻿—it’s not fair. I had stood a good deal among the Kosekin. Their love of darkness, their passion for death, their contempt of riches, their yearning after unrequited love, their human sacrifices, their cannibalism, all had more or less become familiar to me, and I had learned to acquiesce in silence; but now when it came to this﻿—that a woman should propose to a man﻿—it really was more than a fellow could stand. I felt this at that moment very forcibly; but then the worst of it was that Layelah was so confoundedly pretty, and had such a nice way with her, that hang me if I knew what to say.

Meanwhile Layelah was not silent; she had all her wits about her.

===Chinese===
无须说，这一切实在窘迫到了极处。我固然倾心于莱拉，喜欢她到了不忍伤她心的地步。我若是个科塞金人，或许还能应对裕如；可我偏偏是个欧洲人，一个雅利安人种的男儿——既是这样一个人，又坐在那里，任美丽的莱拉把满腔柔情向我倾泻——唉，可想而知，我无论如何也狠不下心去刺伤她。我落了个措手不及、全然下风。我平生从未听说过女子主动求亲这等事。莱拉向我求了亲，又不肯听一个「不」字，而我又无心伤她。我竭尽全力地抵挡，一味坚陈我对阿尔玛的爱，可我这些声言，全被她轻轻拂到一边，只当作无关紧要的细事。

且教随便哪位绅士设身处地替我想一想，问问自己会怎么办。倘若这样的事出在他自己家里，他又当如何？只是这种事在我们那边根本不会发生，所以去假设一个不可能的案子，也是枉然。可无论如何，我想我总该得到几分同情。处此境地，谁还能镇定自若？在我们那里，一位倾心于某个男子的少女，要回绝另一位求婚者是再容易不过的事；可这里却大不相同，教我怎样去回绝莱拉呢？难道我能翻脸向她喝一声「放开我」？难道我能说「去！我已是别人的人了」？我自然不能；更糟的是，我若真说了这样的话，莱拉只消嫣然一笑，便能把我笑得哑口无言。说到底，女子主动终究不成体统——这于男子不公平。我在科塞金人中间也算是久经风浪的了。他们的崇暗，他们的尚死，他们的鄙夷钱财，他们对单相思的渴慕，他们的人牲献祭，他们的食人之风，这一切我或多或少都已司空见惯，学会了默默隐忍；可如今事情竟到了这一步——一个女子竟向男子求亲——这实在叫人消受不起。那一刻我痛感此理；而最糟的是，莱拉偏生俏丽得叫人没法儿，又有那样一副讨人喜欢的好性儿，教我说什么好——绞死我也答不上来。

这当口莱拉也没有闲着；她机敏伶俐，心思一丝不乱。

===Original===
“Dear papa,” said she, “would make such a nice husband for Almah. He is a widower, you know. I could easily persuade him to marry her. He always does whatever I ask him to do.”

“But victims cannot marry, you said.”

“No,” said Layelah, sweetly, “they cannot marry one another, but Almah may marry dear papa, and then you and I can be married, and it will be all very nice indeed.”

At this I started away.

“No,” said I, indignantly, “it won’t be nice. I’m engaged to be married to Almah, and I’m not going to give her up.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的爸爸，」她说，「给阿尔玛做丈夫再合适不过了。他是个鳏夫，你是知道的。我要劝他娶她，易如反掌。我求他办的事，他从来没有不办的。」

「可你方才说过，人牲不能成婚。」

「不错，」莱拉甜甜地说，「人牲与人牲不能彼此成婚；可阿尔玛尽可以嫁亲爱的爸爸，然后你和我便能成亲，一切就都再美满不过了。」

听了这话，我猛地挣开身子。

「不，」我愤然道，「这才不美满呢。我已同阿尔玛定了婚约，我不打算让出她。」

===Original===
“Oh, but she gives you up, you know,” said Layelah, quietly.

“Well, but I’m not going to be given up.”

“Why, how unreasonable you are, you foolish boy!” said Layelah, in her most caressing manner. “You have nothing at all to do with it.”

At this I was in fresh despair, and then a new thought came, which I seized upon.

===Chinese===
「哦，可你知道，是她让出你呀。」莱拉从容不迫地说。

「哼，可我不打算被人让出去。」

「哎呀，你多不讲理呀，你这傻孩子！」莱拉说着，拿出她最娇昵温存的模样。「这事根本就由不得你。」

这一下我又陷入新的绝望；忽然又生出一计，我赶紧把它抓住了。

===Original===
“See here,” said I, “why can’t I marry both of you? I’m engaged to Almah, and I love her better than all the world. Let me marry her and you too.”

At this Layelah laughed long and merrily. Peal after peal of laughter, musical and most merry, burst from her. It was contagious; I could not help joining in, and so we both sat laughing. It was a long time before we regained our self-control.

“Why, that’s downright bigamy!” exclaimed Layelah with fresh laughter. “Why, Atam-or, you’re mad!” and so she went off again in fresh peals of laughter. It was evident that my proposal was not at all shocking, but simply comical, ridiculous, and inconceivable in its absurdity. It was to her what the remark of some despairing beauty would be among us who, when pressed by two lovers should express a confused willingness to marry both. It was evident that Layelah accepted it as a ludicrous jest.

Laughter was all very well, of course; but I was serious and felt that I ought not to part with Layelah without some better understanding, and so I once more made an effort.

===Chinese===
「你听我说，」我道，「我何不把你们两个都娶了呢？我同阿尔玛有婚约在身，我爱她胜过世上的一切。就让我娶了她，也娶了你吧。」

这话引得莱拉纵情大笑，笑得又久又欢。一阵又一阵笑声从她那里迸出来，如乐音一般，欢畅无比。笑声是会感染的，我也忍不住跟着笑，于是我们两个便坐在那里笑作一团。好半天，我们才收住这股劲儿。

「哎呀，这可是不折不扣的重婚呀！」莱拉叫了起来，又爆出一阵新的笑声。「哎呀，阿塔莫尔，你疯啦！」说着她复又咯咯地笑个不住。显然，我这一提议在她看来全不骇人听闻，只是滑稽、荒唐、荒诞得匪夷所思罢了。在我们那边，若有哪位让两个求婚者逼得走投无路的美人，慌乱之间表示两个都肯嫁，那话儿在人听来便是这般光景。莱拉分明只把它当作一句逗趣的戏言。

笑归笑，自然无妨；可我是认真的，觉得总不该同莱拉就此作别，而不把话说得更明白些，于是我又作了一回努力。

===Original===
“All this,” said I, in a mournful tone, “is a mere mockery. What have I to say about love and marriage? If you loved me as you say, you would not laugh, but weep. You forget what I am. What am I? A victim, and doomed﻿—doomed to a hideous fate﻿—a fate of horror unutterable. You cannot even begin to imagine the anguish with which I look forward to that fate which impends over me and Almah. Marriage﻿—idle word! What have I to do with marriage? What has Almah? There is only one marriage before us﻿—the dread marriage with death! Why talk of love to the dying? The tremendous ordeal, the sacrifice, is before us and after that there remains the hideous Mista Kosek!”

At this Layelah sprang up, with her whole face and attitude full of life and energy.

“I know, I know,” said she, quickly; “I have arranged for all. Your life shall be saved. Do you think that I have consented to your death? Never! You are mine. I will save you. I will show you what we can do. You shall escape.”

===Chinese===
「这一切，」我以凄恻的声调说道，「不过是徒然的戏弄罢了。我这待死之人，还有什么情爱婚嫁可谈？你若真像你说的那样爱我，就不该笑，倒该哭才是。你忘了我是什么人。我是什么人？是个钦定的人牲，在劫难逃——注定要遭一桩凶残的厄运——一场说不出口的骇人下场。我和阿尔玛头上悬着那样的厄运，我是怀着怎样的创楚遥望着它，这你连想都无从想起。婚嫁——空话一句！我同婚嫁有什么相干？阿尔玛又有什么相干？摆在我们面前的婚事只有一桩——与死亡的那场可怖的婚礼！何苦对着将死之人谈情说爱？那场大劫、那场献祭就在眼前，而献祭之后，还有骇人的米斯塔·科塞克等着我们！」

听了这话，莱拉霍地站起，眉目之间、举手投足全是生气与活力。

「我知道，我知道，」她急急地说；「一切都已安排停当。你的性命一定保得住。你道我肯应下你的死么？决不！你是我的。我要救你。我要叫你瞧瞧我们的本事。你一定能逃出去。」

===Original===
“Can you really save me?” I cried.

“I can.”

“What! in spite of the whole nation?”

Layelah laughed scornfully.

===Chinese===
「你当真能救我？」我叫道。

「能。」

「什么！任凭全国之力也拦不住？」

莱拉轻蔑地一笑。

===Original===
“I can save you,” said she. “We can fly. There are other nations beside ours. We can find some land among the Gojin where we can live in peace. The Gojin are not like us.”

“But Almah?” said I.

The face of Layelah clouded.

“I can only save you,” said she.

“Then I will stay and die with Almah,” said I, obstinately.

===Chinese===
「我能救你，」她说。「我们能远走高飞。世上除我们这个邦国，还有别的邦国。我们能在戈金人（Gojin）那里寻得一块土地，太太平平地过活。戈金人同我们不一样。」

「可是阿尔玛呢？」我说。

莱拉的脸色黯了下来。

「我救得了的只有你一个。」她说。

「那么我就留下来，同阿尔玛一起死。」我执拗地说。

===Original===
“What!” said Layelah, “do you not fear death?”

“Of course I do,” said I; “but I’d rather die than lose Almah.”

“But it’s impossible to save both of you.”

“Then leave me and save Almah,” said I.

“What! would you give up your life for Almah?”

===Chinese===
「什么！」莱拉说，「你难道不怕死？」

「我自然怕，」我说；「可我宁可死，也不肯失去阿尔玛。」

「可要救你们两个，是办不到的。」

「那就撇下我，去救阿尔玛。」我说。

「什么！你肯为阿尔玛舍了性命？」

===Original===
“Yes, and a thousand lives,” said I.

“Why,” said Layelah, “now you talk just like the Kosekin. You might as well be one of us. You love death for the sake of Almah. Why not be more like the Kosekin, and seek after a separation from Almah?”

Layelah was not at all offended at my declaration of love for Almah. She uttered these words in a lively tone, and then said that it was time for her to go.

===Chinese===
「肯，有一千条命我也肯。」我说。

「哎呀，」莱拉说，「你这会儿说话，竟同科塞金人一模一样了。你索性做我们中的一员也罢。你为了阿尔玛的缘故，竟爱上死亡了。何不再学学科塞金人的样，去同阿尔玛求一场『分离』呢？」

我这一番对阿尔玛的爱情表白，莱拉丝毫不见怪。她这番话说得兴致勃勃，说完便道，她该走了。

## XXI The Flying Monster / 二十一、飞行巨怪

===Original===
I retired to bed, but could not sleep. The offer of escape filled me with excited thoughts. These made sleep impossible, and as I lay awake I thought that perhaps it would be well to know what might be Layelah’s plan of escape, for I might then make use of it to save Almah. I determined to find out all about it on the following *jom*﻿—to question her as to the lands of the Gojin, to learn all her purpose. It might be that I could make use of that very plan to save Almah; but if not, why then I was resolved to remain and meet my fate with her. If Layelah could be induced to take both of us, I was of course resolved to go, trusting to chance as to the claims of Layelah upon me, and determined at all hazards to be faithful to Almah; but if she should positively refuse to save Almah, then I thought it possible that I might be able to find in Layelah’s plan of escape something of which I might avail myself. I could not imagine what it was, but it seemed to me that it might be something quite feasible, especially for a desperate man. The only thought I had was of escape by means of some boat over the seas. In a boat I would be at home. I could make use of a sail so as to elude pursuit, and could guide myself by the stars. The only thing that I wanted to know was the situation of the lands of the Gojin.

===Chinese===
我回房就寝，却无法入睡。逃离的提议搅得我满腔亢奋的念头，睡眠遂成不可能。醒着躺在那里，我心想，或许不妨先弄清莱拉的逃离计划究竟是怎样一回事——如此我便可借它来救阿尔玛。我打定主意，第二个约姆便把此事问个水落石出：盘问她戈金人（Gojin）诸地的情形，摸清她的全部意图。说不定恰恰是这一计划可用来救阿尔玛；倘若不能，那我也已横下心来留下，与她同赴命运。倘若能说动莱拉把我们两个一并带走，那我自然决意同行——至于莱拉在我身上的权利主张，只有听凭机缘——并且不避一切凶险，对阿尔玛矢志不移；可她若断然拒绝搭救阿尔玛，那么我想，说不定还能从莱拉的逃离计划里寻出些可供我借重的门道。我想象不出那是什么，但在我看来，它兴许颇为可行，对一个亡命之徒尤其是。我心头唯一的念头，便是驾一叶小舟渡海而逃。到了船上我便如鱼得水：我可以扬帆避开追兵，可以观星辨向。我唯一想知道的，是戈金人国土的方位。

===Original===
On the following *jom* the Kohen Gadol and Layelah came quite early and spent much time. I was surprised to see the Kohen Gadol devoting himself in an absurd fashion to Almah. It at once occurred to me that Layelah had obtained her father’s cooperation in her scheme, and that the old villain actually imagined that he could win the hand of Almah. To Almah herself I had said nothing whatever about the proposal of Layelah, so that she was quite ignorant of the intentions of her companion; but it was excessively annoying to me to see such proceedings going on under my own eyes. At the same time I felt that it would be both unwise and uncivil to interfere; and I was also quite sure that Almah’s affections were not to be diverted from me by anyone, much less by such an elderly party as the Kohen Gadol. It was very trying, however, and, in spite of my confidence in Almah, my jealousy was excited, and I began to think that the party of philosophical Radicals were not so agreeable as the orthodox cannibals whom I first met. As for Layelah, she seemed quite unconscious of any disturbance in my mind. She was as amiable, as sprightly, as inquisitive, and as affectionate as ever. She even outdid herself, and devoted herself to me with an abandon that was quite irresistible.

===Chinese===
翌日约姆，大祭司科恩·加多尔同莱拉一大早就来了，盘桓良久。我诧异地看到，大祭司竟以一副荒唐相向阿尔玛大献殷勤。我立刻醒悟：莱拉已把她父亲拉进了她的谋划，而这老奸贼竟当真幻想能赢得阿尔玛的芳心。莱拉的提议我对阿尔玛只字未提，故而她全然不知这位同伴的用意；但眼看着这番行径在我眼皮底下搬演，实在令我气恼。与此同时我又觉得，从中作梗既有失明智，亦有失体面；何况我十分笃定，阿尔玛的情意绝非任何人所能从我这里引开，更遑论大祭司这等年纪的人物。然而这滋味着实难熬：尽管我对阿尔玛满怀信心，妒意仍被撩动起来；我开始觉得，哲学激进派这一党人，到底不如我初来时结识的那些正统食人者可亲。至于莱拉，她似乎全然不曾察觉我心中有何烦乱，依旧那样和蔼、那样活泼、那样好奇、那样多情，甚至比平日更甚，以一种令人无从抗拒的酣畅全身心倾注在我身上。

===Original===
After Almah had left me, Layelah came again, and this time she was alone.

“I have come,” said she, “to show you the way in which we can escape, whenever you decide to do so.”

It was the thing above all others which I wished to know, and therefore I questioned her eagerly about it; but to all of my questions she only replied that she would show me, and I might judge for myself.

Layelah led the way, and I followed her. We traversed long galleries and vast halls, all of which were quite empty. It was the sleeping-time, and only those were visible who had some duties which kept them up later than usual. Faint, twinkling lights but feebly illuminated the general gloom. At length we came to an immense cavern, which was darker than ever, and without any lamps at all. Through a vast portal, which was closed with a barred gateway, the beams of the brilliant aurora penetrated and disclosed something of the interior.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛离去后，莱拉又来了，这一回她是独自一人。

「我来，」她说，「是要让你看看我们逃走的路径，只等你拿定主意。」

这正是我万般想知的事，于是急切地向她追问；但对所有的问题，她只答道：她自会指给我看，我可以自行判断。

莱拉在前引路，我紧随其后。我们穿行过一道道长廊、一座座广堂，尽皆空寂无人。此刻正是安睡的时辰，唯有身负职事、较往常熬得更晚的人才得一见。微弱闪烁的灯火，只淡淡照破那弥天的幽暗。终于我们来到一座浩大的洞窟，这里比先前更黑，竟连一盏灯也没有。一道巨门装着栅栏门扉，掩闭着；绚烂的极光透过门来，照出窟内的大致形影。

===Original===
Here Layelah stopped and peered through the gloom while I stood waiting by her side, wondering what means of escape could be found in this cavern. As I stood I heard through the still air the sound as of living things. For a time I saw nothing, but at length I descried a vast, shadowy form moving forward toward the portal, where the darkness was less. It was a form of portentous size and fearful shape, and I could not make out at first the nature of it. It surpassed all that I had ever seen. Its head was large and its jaws long, armed with rows of terrible teeth like those of a crocodile. Its body was of great size. It walked on its hind-legs, so as to maintain itself in an upright attitude, and in that position its height was over twelve feet. But the most amazing thing about this monster has yet to be told. As it walked its forearms waved and fluttered, and I saw descending from them what seemed like vast folded leathern wings, which shook and swayed in the air at every step. Its pace was about as fast as that of a man, and it moved with ease and lightness. It seemed like some enormous bat, or rather like a winged crocodile, or yet again like one of those monstrous dragons of which I had read, but in whose actual existence I had never believed. Yet here I saw one living and moving before me﻿—an actual dragon, with the exception of a tail; for that appendage, which plays so great a part in all the pictures of dragons, had no place here. This beast had but a short caudal appendage, and all its terrors lay in its jaws and in its wings.

===Chinese===
莱拉在这里站住，透过幽暗张望；我立在她身旁等候，心里暗自纳罕：这洞窟里能寻出什么逃走的法子。正立间，我听见凝滞的空气里传来似有活物的声息。一时我什么也看不见，但终于辨出一个庞大朦胧的形体，正朝门洞这边移近——那里暗色稍淡。那形体大得可畏，形状骇人，起初我竟认不出它是何等性质的物事。它超出我平生所见的一切。它头颅巨大，颚部修长，森然排列着一排排可怖的牙齿，宛如鳄鱼。它身躯庞大，以两后肢行走，借以保持直立的姿式；如此立定，身高逾十二英尺。但这怪物最令人惊叹的一端，尚待道来。它行走时前臂挥舞扑闪，我看见自那前臂垂下两幅仿佛折叠的巨大革质翅膀，每迈一步，便在空中震颤摇荡。它步速约与人行相当，举动轻快自如。它像一只硕大无朋的蝙蝠，毋宁说像一头生了翅膀的鳄鱼，再不然就像我读到过的那类巨龙——只是它们的真实存在，我从不曾相信。然而此刻，我竟看见一条活龙在我面前行走蠕动——一条货真价实的龙，只除了一条尾巴：因为那在一切龙的图像里担当重位的部件，在此并无位置。这畜生只有短短一截尾梢，它全部的可怖，全在它的颚与它的翼。

===Original===
For a moment I stood almost lifeless with terror and surprise. Then I shrank back, but Layelah laid her hand on my arm.

“Don’t be afraid,” said she; “it’s only an athaleb.”

“But won’t it﻿—won’t it bite?” I asked, with a shudder.

“Oh no,” said Layelah; “it swallows its victuals whole.”

At this I shrank away still farther.

“Don’t be afraid,” said Layelah again. “Its jaws are muzzled, and, besides, it’s a tame athaleb. Its jaws are unmuzzled only at feeding-time. But this one is very tame. There are three or four others in here, and all as tame as I am. They all know me. Come up nearer; don’t be afraid. These athalebs are easily tamed.”

===Chinese===
一时间我恐惧与惊愕交加，几乎僵立如死。我往后退缩，莱拉却把手搭上我的手臂。

「别怕，」她说，「不过是一头阿塔莱布。」

「可它不会——不会咬人么？」我打着寒噤问。

「哦，不会，」莱拉说，「它进食是囫囵吞的。」

一听这话，我愈发缩远了。

「别怕，」莱拉又说，「它的颚上戴了口络；再说，这是一头驯熟了的阿塔莱布。只有到喂食的时候才解开口络。这一头尤其驯熟。这里面还有三四头别的，全都同我一样驯。它们个个认得我。走近些，别怕。这些阿塔莱布是容易驯养的。」

===Original===
“How can such tremendous monsters be tamed?” I asked, in an incredulous tone.

“Oh, man can tame anything. The athalebs are very docile when they are taken young. They are very long lived. This one has been in service here for a hundred seasons and more.”

At this I began to regain my confidence, and as Layelah moved nearer to the athaleb I accompanied her. A nearer view, however, was by no means reassuring. The dragon look of the athaleb was stronger than ever, for I could see that all its body was covered with scales. On its neck and back was a long ridge of coarse hair, and the sweep of its vast arms was enormous. It was with a quaking heart that I stood near; but the coolness of Layelah reassured me, for she went close up, as a boy would go up to a tame elephant, and she stroked his enormous back, and the monster bent down his terrible head and seemed pleased.

===Chinese===
「这样骇人的巨怪，如何驯得服？」我用不敢相信的口气问。

「哦，人没有驯不服的东西。阿塔莱布自幼捕来，十分驯良。它们寿数极长。这一头在此当差，已历一百多个季了。」

听了这话，我的胆气渐渐回转；莱拉向那阿塔莱布走近，我便随她同去。然而近观之下，绝难令人安心。阿塔莱布那副龙形愈发逼人，因为我看得分明，它周身覆满鳞甲，颈上背上耸起一长列粗硬鬣毛，那双巨臂张开的幅面浩大惊人。我立在近旁，心头突突直跳；但莱拉的镇定使我安下心来——她径直走上前去，像少年走近一头驯象那样，抚摩它庞大的脊背，那巨怪便低下它可怕的头颅，似乎很是受用。

===Original===
“This,” said Layelah, “is the way we have of escaping.”

“This!” I exclaimed, doubtfully.

“Yes,” said she. “He is trained to the service. We can mount on his back, and he will fly with us wherever I choose to guide him.”

“What!” I exclaimed, as I shrank back﻿—“fly! Do you mean to say that you will mount this hideous monster, and trust yourself to him?”

“Certainly,” said Layelah, quietly. “He is very docile. There is harness here with which we can guide him. Should you like to see him harnessed?”

“Very much indeed,” said I.

===Chinese===
「这，」莱拉说，「便是我们逃走的凭仗。」

「这个！」我满腹狐疑，失声道。

「正是，」她说，「它受过这差事的调教。我们可以骑上它的背，我爱把它引到哪里，它便驮着我们飞到哪里。」

「什么！」我一面退缩一面失声道——「飞！你是说，你要骑上这头面目可憎的巨怪，把自己托付给它？」

「当然，」莱拉安然地说，「它极驯良。这里有挽具，可供我们驾驭。你想不想看看它上挽具？」

「实在想看，」我说。

===Original===
Upon this Layelah walked up to the monster and stroked his breast. The huge athaleb at once lay down upon his belly. Then she brought two long straps like reins, and fastened each to the tip of a projecting tip of each wing. Then she fastened a collar around his neck, to which there was attached a grappling-iron.

“We seat ourselves on his back,” said Layelah. “I guide with these reins. When we land anywhere I fasten him with the grapple. He looks dull now, but if I were to open the gate and remove his muzzle he would be off like the wind.”

“But can he carry both of us?” I asked.

“Easily,” said Layelah. “He can carry three persons without fatigue.”

===Chinese===
于是莱拉走到巨怪跟前，抚了抚它的胸口。庞大的阿塔莱布随即伏地而卧。接着她取来两条缰绳似的长皮带，各系在两边伸出的翼梢上。随后她又给它颈间系上一道颈圈，圈上拴着一只抓钩。

「我们骑坐在它背上，」莱拉说，「我用这两条缰驾驭。在哪里降落，我就用抓钩把它拴住。它此刻看着呆钝，可我若开了门、解了它的口络，它便一阵风似的去了。」

「可它驮得动我们两个么？」我问。

「轻而易举，」莱拉说，「驮上三人也不觉吃力。」

===Original===
“Could you mount on his back now, and show me how you sit?”

Layelah readily assented, and mounted with the greatest ease, seating herself on the broadest part of the back between the wings.

“Here,” said she, “is room for you. Will you not come?”

For an instant I hesitated; but then the sight of her, seated there as coolly as though she were on a chair reassured me, and I climbed up also, though not without a shudder. The touch of the fearful monster was abhorrent but I conquered my disgust and seated myself close behind Layelah. Here she sat, holding the reins in her hands, with the grapple just in front of her; and, seated in this position, she went on to explain the whole process by which the mighty monster was guided through the air.

===Chinese===
「你可否此刻骑上它背，让我看看你是怎样坐的？」

莱拉欣然应允，极其轻捷地骑了上去，坐在两翼之间背部最宽处。

「这儿，」她说，「有你的位置。你不来么？」

我踌躇了一瞬；但见她坐在那里安然自若，仿佛坐在一把椅子上，我便宽了心，也攀了上去——尽管并非不打着寒噤。挨着那可怖巨怪的躯体令人生厌，但我压下了憎恶，紧挨在莱拉身后坐定。她便这样坐着，双手执缰，抓钩正擎在她面前；她以这个坐姿，继续讲解驾驭这庞然巨物凌空飞行的全部法门。

===Original===
No sooner had I found myself actually on the back of the athaleb than all fear left me. I perceived fully how completely tame he was, and how docile. The reins attached to his wings could be pulled with the greatest ease, Just as one would pull the tiller-ropes of a boat. “Familiarity breeds contempt;” and now, since the first terror had passed away, I felt perfect confidence, and under the encouragement of Layelah I had become like some rustic in a menagerie, who at first is terrified by the sight of the elephant, but soon gains courage enough to mount upon his back. With my newfound courage and presence of mind I listened most attentively to all of Layelah’s explanations, and watched most closely the construction and fastening of the harness; for the thought had occurred to me that this athaleb might be of avail in another way﻿—that if I did not fly with Layelah, I might fly with Almah. This thought was only of a vague and shadowy character﻿—a dim suggestion, the carrying out of which I scarce dared to think possible; still, it was in my mind, and had sufficient power over me to make me very curious as to the plans of Layelah. I determined to find out where she proposed to go, and how far; to ask her about the dangers of the way and the means of sustenance. It seemed, I confess, rather unfair to Layelah to find out her plans and use them for another purpose; but then that other purpose was Almah, and to me at that time every device which was for her safety seemed fair and honorable.

===Chinese===
待到当真骑上阿塔莱布的背，恐惧便一齐离我而去。我充分觉出它是何等彻底的驯熟、何等的听话。系在它双翼上的缰绳，拉动起来毫不费力，正如人拉动小船的舵索。「亲昵生轻慢」；如今最初的惊怖既过，我便信心十足；在莱拉的鼓励之下，我已变得像兽展里的乡愚——初见大象吓得魂飞魄散，不多时便攒起足够的胆气骑上象背。我凭着新生的胆气与沉着，凝神谛听莱拉的每一句讲解，细察挽具的构造与系法；因为我心头已转过一念：这头阿塔莱布或许另有用处——倘若我不与莱拉同飞，我还可以与阿尔玛同飞。这念头起初只是一种朦胧虚幻的东西——一个隐约的暗示，能否见诸实行，我几乎不敢妄想；但它终究萦绕于心，其力量足以使我对莱拉的计划生出极强的好奇。我决意探明她打算前往何处、路途多远；要向她问清一路的凶险与口粮的来源。我承认，探得人家的计划而移作别用，于莱拉未免有欠公道；但那别用便是阿尔玛——在那时的我看来，凡有利于她安全的计谋，无不是光明正大之事。

===Original===
“Here,” said Layelah﻿—“here, Atam-or, you see the way of escape. The athaleb can carry us both far away to a land where you need never fear that they will put you to death﻿—a land where the people love light and life. Whenever you are ready to go, tell me; if you are ready to go now, say so, and at once I will open the door, and we shall soon be far away.”

She laid her hand on mine and looked at me earnestly; but I was not to be beguiled into any hasty committal of myself, and so I turned her proposal away with a question:

===Chinese===
「这儿，」莱拉说——「这儿，阿塔莫尔，你看见逃走的路径了。这阿塔莱布能驮着我俩远走高飞，飞到一片国土——在那里你永远不必担心他们要将你处死——那是一片人人热爱光明与生命的国土。你何时想走，只管告诉我；若你此刻便想走，就说一声，我立刻开门，转眼我们便可远在天外。」

她把一只手覆在我手上，恳切地望着我；但我不肯被诱得轻率许身，便拿一个问题把她的提议支开了：

===Original===
“How far is it,” I asked, “to that land?”

“It is too far for one flight,” said Layelah. “We go first over the sea till we come to a great island, which is called Magones, where there are mountains of fire; there we must rest, and feed the athaleb on fish, which are to be found on the shore. The athaleb knows his way there well, for he goes there once every season for a certain sacred ceremony. He has done this for fifty or sixty seasons, and knows his way there and back perfectly well. The difficulty will be, when we leave Magones, in reaching the land of the Orin.”

“The Orin?” I repeated. “Who are they?”

“They are a people among the Gojin who love life and light. It is their land that I wish to reach, if possible.”

“Where is it?” I asked, eagerly.

“I cannot explain,” said Layelah. “I can only trust to my own skill, and hope to find the place. We may have to pass over different lands of the Gojin, and if so we may be in danger.”

===Chinese===
「那地方，」我问，「有多远？」

「一程飞不到，」莱拉说，「我们先渡海，抵达一座大岛，名叫马戈内斯，那里有火焰之山；我们必须在彼歇息，用岸上捕得的鱼喂阿塔莱布。这阿塔莱布熟识去那里的路——它每一季都要去一趟，行某一场神圣的仪式。这差事它已当有五六十个季，去路归途烂熟于心。难就难在离开马戈内斯之后，如何抵达奥林人（Orin）的国土。」

「奥林人？」我重述了一遍，「他们是什么人？」

「他们是戈金人当中热爱生命与光明的一族。但愿能够抵达的，正是他们的国土。」

「在哪里？」我急切地问。

「我说不清，」莱拉道，「唯有倚仗我自己的本事，但愿寻得那地方。我们或许不得不飞越戈金人的几处国土，倘若如此，便可能有凶险。」

===Original===
“What is the reason why the athaleb goes to Magones every season?” I asked.

“To take there the chief pauper of the season, who has won the prize of death by starvation. It is one of the greatest honors among the Kosekin.”

“Is Magones barren?”

“It is an island of fire, without anything on it but craggy mountains and wild rocks and flowing rivers of fire. It stands almost in the middle of the sea.”

===Chinese===
「阿塔莱布每季去马戈内斯，是何缘故？」我问。

「为的是把当季的首席赤贫者送过去——此人赢得的奖赏，便是饥饿而死。这是科塞金人中间至高的荣誉之一。」

「马戈内斯荒芜么？」

「那是一座火岛，岛上除了嶙峋的山、狰狞的岩石与奔流的火河之外，一无所有。它几乎端立于大海的正中。」

===Original===
“How can we get away from here?” I asked, after some silence.

“From here?﻿—why, I open the gates, and the athaleb flies away; that is all.”

“But shall we not be prevented?”

“Oh no. No one here ever prevents anyone from doing anything. Everyone is eager to help his neighbor.”

===Chinese===
「我们从这里如何走得脱？」沉默半晌之后，我问。

「从这里？——咳，我开了门，阿塔莱布飞出去，仅此而已。」

「可不会有人拦阻么？」

「哦，不会。这里从没有人拦阻旁人做任何事。人人都巴不得帮邻人一把。」

===Original===
“But if they saw me deliberately mounting the athaleb and preparing for flight, would they not stop me?”

“No.”

I was amazed at this.

“But,” said I, “am I not a victim﻿—preserved for the great sacrifice?”

“You are; but you are free to go where you like, and do what you like. Your character of victim makes you most distinguished. It is the highest honor and dignity. All believe that you rejoice in your high dignity, and no one dreams that you are anxious to escape.”

===Chinese===
「可他们若见我从容不迫地骑上阿塔莱布、整装待飞，还不来拦我么？」

「不会。」

我闻言大为惊异。

「可是，」我说，「我岂不是一件祭牲——留着供那场大献祭的么？」

「正是；但你想去哪里便去哪里，想做什么便做什么。你这祭牲的身份，使你成为至为尊显的人物。那是最高的荣誉与尊贵。人人都相信你为自己崇高的尊荣而欢欣，谁也梦想不到你竟急于逃走。」

===Original===
“But if I did escape, would they not pursue me?”

“Certainly not.”

“What would they do for a victim?”

“They would wonder at your unaccountable flight, and then choose some distinguished pauper.”

===Chinese===
「可我若当真逃了，他们不来追么？」

「断然不会。」

「那他们上哪里再觅祭牲？」

「他们会对你这无从解释的飞遁啧啧称奇，然后再选出某位显赫的赤贫者来。」

===Original===
“But if I were to stay here, would they not save me from death at my entreaty?”

“Oh, certainly not; they would never understand such an entreaty. That’s a question of death, the supreme blessing. No one is capable of such a base act as saving his fellow-man from death. All are eager to help each other to such a fate.”

“But if I were to fly they would not prevent me, and they would not pursue me?”

“Oh no.”

===Chinese===
「可我若是留在此地，我苦苦哀求，他们也不肯救我脱离死亡么？」

「哦，断然不肯；这样的哀求他们永远无从理解。那可是有关死亡——至高福祉——的事。谁也做不出把同胞从死亡中救出这等卑鄙的行径。人人都热切地盼着帮彼此求得这样一个下场。」

「可我若飞走，他们既不拦我，也不追我？」

「哦，不会。」

===Original===
“Are there any in the land who are exempt from the sacrifice?”

“Oh yes; the Athons, Meleks, and Kohens﻿—these are not worthy of the honor. The artisans and tradesmen are sometimes permitted to attain to this honor; the laborers in greater numbers; but it is the paupers who are chiefly favored. And this is a matter of complaint among the rich and powerful, that they cannot be sacrificed.”

“Well, why couldn’t I be made an Athon or a Kohen, and be exempted in that way?”

“Oh, that would be too great a dishonor; it would be impossible. On the contrary, the whole people are anxious to honor you to the very uttermost, and to bestow upon you the greatest privileges and blessings which can possibly be given. Oh no, it would be impossible for them to allow you to become an Athon or a Kohen. As for me, I am Malca, and therefore the lowest in the land﻿—pitied and commiserated by the haughty pauper class, who shake their heads at the thought of one like me. All the people shower upon me incessantly new gifts and new offices. If my present love of light and life were generally known, they would punish me by giving me new contributions of wealth and new offices and powers, which I do not want.”

===Chinese===
「这国中可有人豁免于献祭？」

「哦，有；阿通、美勒克与科恩——这些人不配享受这份荣誉。工匠与商贾偶尔获准企及这份荣誉；劳力者获准的更多；但最受眷顾的终是赤贫者。富人豪门之间因此颇多怨言——怨自己得不到献祭。」

「那么，为何不能封我为阿通或科恩，如此便得豁免？」

「哦，那便是莫大的耻辱，万万不能。恰恰相反，举国上下正巴不得把你尊崇到无以复加，把人间所能赐予的至高特权与福祉尽数加于你身。哦不，他们绝不容你沦为阿通或科恩。至于我，我是玛尔卡，因而是全国至低之人——高傲的赤贫者阶级怜悯我、哀矜我，一想到我这样的人便直摇头。举国之人不停地将新的馈赠、新的官职倾泻到我身上。我如今这热爱光明与生命的心思若传扬开去，他们便要惩罚我——给我添上新的财富摊派、新的官职权柄，全是我不要的东西。」

===Original===
“But you love riches, do you not? and you must want them still?”

“No,” said Layelah, “I do not want them now.”

“Why, what do you want?” I asked.

“You!” said she, with a sweet smile.

I said nothing, but tried desperately to think of something that might divert the conversation.

===Chinese===
「可你不是爱财么？如今想必还贪想吧？」

「不，」莱拉说，「我如今不要了。」

「咦，那你要什么？」我问。

「要你！」她说，嫣然一笑。

我一言不发，只拼命想寻一句话来岔开话头。

===Original===
Layelah was silent for a few moments, and then went on in a musing tone:

“As I was saying, I love you, Atam-or, and I hate Almah because you love her. I think Almah is the only human being in all the world that I ever really hated; and yet, though I hate her, still, strange to say, I feel as though I should like to give her the immense blessing of death, and that is a very strange feeling, indeed, for one of the Kosekin. Do you understand, Atam-or, what such can possibly be?”

I did not answer, but turned away the conversation by a violent effort.

===Chinese===
莱拉默然片刻，而后以若有所思的声调继续说道：

「方才我说过，我爱你，阿塔莫尔；我恨阿尔玛，因为你爱她。我想，普天之下，我真正恨过的人，怕也只有阿尔玛一个；然而说来奇怪，我虽恨她，却仍觉得自己仿佛情愿把死亡这份浩大的福祉赐予她——对一个科塞金人而言，这实在是一种万分古怪的心情。你可明白，阿塔莫尔，这怎么会这样？」

我没有作答，只费尽气力将话头硬生生岔开。

===Original===
“Are there any other athalebs here?”

“Oh yes.”

“How many?”

“Four.”

===Chinese===
「此处还有别的阿塔莱布么？」

「哦，有。」

「几头？」

「四头。」

===Original===
“Are they all as tame as this?”

“Oh yes, all quite as tame; there is no difference whatever.”

Upon this I left the back of the athaleb, and Layelah also descended, after which she proceeded to show me the other monsters. At length she unharnessed the athaleb and we left the cavern.

===Chinese===
「都同这一头一样驯么？」

「哦，都一样驯，毫无二致。」

说罢，我便下了阿塔莱布的背，莱拉也随之下来；随后她领我去看了其余几头巨怪。末了她卸下阿塔莱布的挽具，我们便离了洞窟。

## XXII Escape / 二十二、出逃

===Original===
On the following *jom* I told everything to Almah. I told her that Layelah was urging me to fly with her, and that I had found out all about her plans. I described the athalebs, informed her about the direction which we were to take, the island of fire, and the country of the Orin. At this intelligence Almah was filled with delight, and for the first time since we had come to the *amir* there were smiles of joy upon her face. She needed no persuasion. She was ready to set forth whenever it was fitting, and to risk everything upon this enterprise. She felt as I did, and thought that the wildest attempt was better than this dull inaction.

Death was before us here, and every *jom* as it passed only brought it nearer. True, we were treated with the utmost kindness, we lived in royal splendor, we had enormous retinues; but all this was a miserable mockery, since it all served as the prelude to our inevitable doom. For that doom it was hard indeed to wait. Anything was better. Far better would it be to risk all the dangers of this unusual and amazing flight, to brave the terrors of that drear isle of fire, Magones; better to perish there of starvation, or to be killed by the hands of hostile Gojin, than to wait here and be destroyed at last by the sacrificial knife of these smiling, generous, kindhearted, self-sacrificing fiends; to be killed﻿—ay, and afterward borne to the tremendous Mista Kosek.

===Chinese===
到了下一个约姆，我把一应情由向阿尔玛和盘托出。我告诉她，莱拉正怂恿我随她远走高飞，又说她的种种谋划我已尽数探明。我把那些阿塔莱布描绘了一番，又将我们此行的路线告知于她：那火焰之岛，还有奥林（Orin）人的国度。听到这个消息，阿尔玛喜不自胜；自从来到了阿米尔，她脸上第一次绽出快乐的笑容。她无须劝导，只等时机相宜便随时可以启程，甘愿为这桩事业把一切都押上去。她与我心意相通，也觉得哪怕最狂乱的举动，也强似这般沉闷的无所作为。

死亡就在此地候着我们，过去一个约姆，便把它催近一分。不错，我们受着无微不至的善待，过着王室般的豪奢日子，身边仆从成群；可这一切都是一场凄凉的嘲弄，因为它们统统不过是我们那无可逃免的厄运的前奏。要坐等这厄运降临，实在是难乎为怀。什么都比这强。宁去冒这场破天荒、骇人听闻的飞行的种种危险，去领教那座荒凉的火焰之岛——马戈内斯（Magones）——的种种可怖；宁肯死在那里，饿死也好，死在怀敌意的戈金人（Gojin）手里也好，也强似在这里坐等，到头来丧命于这一班笑吟吟、慷慨、好心肠、舍己为人的魔鬼的献祭刀下——被杀——是啊，随后还要被抬去赴那盛大的米斯塔·科塞克。

===Original===
There was a difficulty with Layelah that had to be guarded against: in the first place that she might not suspect, and again that we might choose our time of escape when she would not be at all likely to find us out. We resolved to make our attempt without any further delay. Layelah was with us for the greater part of that *jom*, and the Kohen Gadol also gave us much of his company. Layelah did not seem to have any suspicions whatever of my secret purpose; for she was as bright, as amiable, and as devoted to me as ever, while the Kohen Gadol sought as before to make himself agreeable to Almah. I did not think fit to tell her about Layelah’s proposal, and therefore she was quite ignorant of the secret plans of the Kohen Gadol, evidently attributing his attention to the unfailing amiability of the Kosekin.

Layelah came again after Almah had retired, and spent the time in trying to persuade me to fly with her. The beautiful girl was certainly never more engaging, nor was she ever more tender. Had it not been for Almah it would have been impossible to resist such sweet persuasions; but as it was I did resist. Layelah, however, was not at all discouraged, nor did she lose any of her amiability; but when she took leave it was with a smile and sweet words of forgiveness on her lips for what she called my cruelty. After she left I remained for a time with a painful sense of helplessness. The fact is my European training did not fit me for encountering such a state of things as existed among the Kosekin. It’s very easy to be faithful to one’s own truelove in England, when other fair ladies hold aloof and wait to be sought; but here among the Kosekin, women have as much liberty in making love as men, and there is no law or custom about it. If a woman chooses she can pay the most desperate attentions, and play the part of a distracted lover to her heart’s content. In most cases the women actually take the initiative, as they are more impressible and impulsive than men; and so it was that Layelah made me the object of her persistent assault﻿—acting all the time, too, in accordance with the custom of the country, and thus having no thought whatever of indelicacy, since, according to the Kosekin, she was acting simply in accordance with the rights of every woman. Now, where a woman is urged by one ardent lover to dismiss her other lover, she may sometimes find it difficult to play her part satisfactorily; but in my case I did not play my part satisfactorily at all; the ordeal was too hard, and I was utterly unable to show to Layelah that firmness and decision of character which the occasion demanded.

===Chinese===
莱拉这里却有一重难关，不能不防：一是别让她起疑，二是要挑一个她万难察觉我们的时机动身。我们决意不再迁延，即刻举事。那个约姆的大半时光，莱拉都在我们这里度过，大祭司也来相陪了许久。莱拉对我心底的秘密似乎毫无觉察；她一如既往地明艳照人、和婉可亲、倾心于我，大祭司也照旧向阿尔玛曲意殷勤。我觉得不宜把莱拉的提议告诉阿尔玛，因此她对于大祭司的种种密谋一无所知，显然只把他的照拂，归之于科塞金人那从不缺席的和蔼可亲。

阿尔玛退下之后，莱拉又来了，把时间尽数用在劝我随她同飞上。这位美丽的姑娘实在从未如此惹人动心，也从未如此温存。倘若没有阿尔玛，这样甜蜜的劝说是断断抗拒不了的；但我终究还是抗拒了。莱拉却毫不气馁，和婉也不减分毫；临别之际，她面带微笑，唇边吐着宽恕的甜言，宽恕她所谓的我的残忍。她去后，我独自待了一阵，心头压着一种痛楚的无助之感。实情是，我这一副欧式的教养，全然不足以应付科塞金人中间的这般世道。在英国，要对自家的心上人忠贞是极容易的事，别位佳人自会矜持远引，静候人来相求；可是在这科塞金人中间，女子求爱与男子一样自由，此间于礼法风俗两无拘管。女子只要愿意，尽可以发起最不顾一切的殷勤，痛痛快快扮一个神魂颠倒的恋人。大多数时候，反倒是女子主动，因为她们比男子更易动情、更易冲动；莱拉正是如此，把我当作她穷追不舍的进攻目标——而且她一举一动又全依此地的风习，心里更无半点有失体面的自觉，因为在科塞金人看来，她不过是行使着每个女子都有的权利。当一位女子被一个热烈的情人怂恿着、要她打发掉另一位情人时，她有时难免觉得难以把这出戏演得圆满；至于我，我这出戏是演得全然不圆满；这场考验太严酷了，我全然无法向莱拉显出那场合所要求的坚定与决断。

===Original===
Yet, after all, the ordeal at last ended. Layelah left, as I have said, with sweet words of forgiveness on her lips, and I after a time succeeded in regaining my presence of mind.

Almah was waiting, and she soon joined me. We gathered a few articles for the journey, the chief of which were my rifle and pistol, which I had not used here, and then we set forth. Leaving our apartments we traversed the long passages, and at length came to the cavern of the athalebs. We met several people on the way, who looked at us with smiles, but made no other sign. It was evident that they had no commission to watch us, and that thus far Layelah’s information was correct.

Upon entering the cavern of the athalebs my first feeling was one of helplessness; for I had no confidence whatever in my own powers of managing these awful monsters, nor did I feel sure that I could harness them; but the emergency was a pressing one, and there was no help for it. I had seen where Layelah had left the harness, and now my chief desire was to secure one of the athalebs. The faint light served to disclose nothing but gloom; and I waited for a while, hoping that one of them would come forward as before. But waiting did no good, for no movement was made, and I had to try what I could do myself to rouse them. So I walked farther in toward the back part of the cavern, peering through the gloom, while Almah remained near the entrance.

===Chinese===
然而，这场磨难终于到了头。莱拉临去时唇边带着宽恕的甜言，前面已经说过；隔了一阵，我总算定下神来。

阿尔玛正在等候，随即来到我身边。我们拾掇了几件路上用的物件，最要紧的是我的步枪和手枪——在此地我一直不曾动用——随即启程。出了居所，我们穿过长长的甬道，终于来到阿塔莱布的窟穴。一路遇见几个人，都含笑望我们一眼，却无别的表示。看得出他们并无监视我们的差遣；莱拉所报的消息，至此一丝不差。

一进阿塔莱布的窟穴，我的头一个感觉便是手足无措；要驾驭这些骇人的巨怪，我对自己这点能耐毫无信心，也不确信自己套得上挽具；可是情势急迫，别无他法。莱拉把挽具留在何处，我已看在眼里；此刻头一件大事，便是先弄到手一头阿塔莱布。微光所照，唯余幽暗；我等了片刻，指望其中一头会像先前那样自行走上前来。但等待毫无用处，里头纹丝不动，我只得亲自试试能否把它们惊动。于是我朝窟穴深处继续走去，在幽暗里睁眼细辨，阿尔玛则留在洞口近旁。

===Original===
As I advanced I heard a slight noise, as of someone moving. I thought it was one of the athalebs, and walked on farther, peering through the gloom, when suddenly I came full upon a man who was busy at some work which I could not make out. For a moment I stood in amazement and despair, for it seemed as though all was lost, and as if this man would at once divine my intent. While I stood thus he turned and gave me a very courteous greeting, after which, in the usual manner of the Kosekin, he asked me with much amiability what he could do for me. I muttered something about seeing the athalebs, upon which he informed me that he would show them to me with pleasure.

He went on to say that he had recently been raised from the low position of Athon to that of Feeder of the Athalebs, a post involving duties like those of ostlers or grooms among us, but which here indicated high rank and honor. He was proud of his title of *Epet*, which means servant, and more than usually obliging. I at once took advantage of his complaisance, and requested him to show me the athalebs. Upon this he led the way farther on, where I could see through the gloom the shadowy outlines of four monsters, all of which were resting in an upright posture against the wall, with their claws fixed on a shelf of rock. They looked more than ever like dragons, or rather like enormous bats, for their wings were disclosed hanging in loose leathern folds.

===Chinese===
正往里走，我听得一阵轻微的响动，像有人走动。我以为是哪头阿塔莱布，便再往前去，在幽暗里张望，冷不防迎面撞见一个人，正埋头忙着什么我看不真切的活计。一时间，我立在惊愕与绝望之中，仿佛大势已去，这人一眼便要窥破我的来意。正僵立间，他转过身来，向我彬彬见礼，随即又以科塞金人惯常的样子，满脸和气地问我有何吩咐。我含含糊糊说了句想看看阿塔莱布；他闻言便道，他乐意领我去观览。

他接着又说，自己新近从低贱的阿通之位，擢升为阿塔莱布饲喂官（Feeder of the Athalebs）；论职分，这差事同我们那儿的马夫厩童相仿，在此地却标志着高贵的身份与荣光。他为「埃佩特（Epet）」这个头衔沾沾自喜——这词的意思便是仆役——人也格外殷勤。我当即趁他这份随和，请他把阿塔莱布指给我看。于是他在前引路，往更深处去；透过幽暗，我望见四头巨怪幢幢的轮廓，全都直挺挺倚壁而立，爪子抠定在一道岩架上。它们比往常更像龙了——或者说，更像硕大无朋的蝙蝠，因为那双翼看得分明，正松垮垮地垂叠下来，一褶一褶如皮革。

===Original===
“Can they be roused,” I asked, “and made to move?”

“Oh yes,” said the Epet, and without waiting for any further request he proceeded to pull at the loose fan-like wing of the nearest one. The monster drew himself together, gave a flutter with his wings, and then moved back from the wall.

“Make him walk,” said I, eagerly.

The Epet at this pulled upon his wing once more, and the athaleb moved forward.

“Bring him to the portal, so that I may see him,” said I.

The Epet, still holding the athaleb’s wing, pulled at him, and thus guided him toward the portal. I was amazed at the docility of this terrific monster; yet, after all, I thought that it was no more astonishing than the docility of the elephant, which in like manner allows itself to be guided by the slightest pressure. A child may lead a vast elephant with ease, and here with equal ease the Epet led the athaleb. He led him up near to the portal, where the aurora light beamed through far brighter than the brightest moon, and disclosed all the vast proportions of the monster. I stood and looked on for some time in silence, quite at a loss what to do next.

===Chinese===
「能把它们惊动么，」我问，「叫它们挪动挪动？」

「哦，能。」埃佩特说；不等再有什么请求，他便动手去拉最近那头松垂如扇的翼。巨怪把身子一拢，双翼扑扇了一下，随即从壁边退开。

「叫它走起来。」我急切地说。

埃佩特便又拉了拉它的翼，阿塔莱布向前挪动了。

「把它带到门口来，好让我看看它。」我说。

埃佩特仍攥着阿塔莱布的翼，拉它一把，就这样把它引向门口。这骇人的巨怪竟如此驯顺，真叫我称奇；不过转念一想，这其实不比大象的驯顺更可惊——大象也是这样，只消最轻的一点牵引，便听人导引。小孩子也能轻而易举地牵走一头巨象；埃佩特牵这头阿塔莱布，也是同样的轻而易举。他把它一直领到洞口近旁；极光从那里照进来，比最亮的月光还要亮得多，把巨怪那庞大的形骸照了个一览无余。我默默立在旁边看了半晌，全然不知下一步该当如何。

===Original===
And now Layelah’s words occurred to me as to the perfect willingness of the Kosekin to do anything which one might wish. She had insisted on it that they would not prevent our flight, and had given me to understand that they would even assist me if I should ask them. This is what now occurred to me, and I determined to make a trial. So I said:

“I should like to fly in the air on the athaleb. Will you harness him?”

I confess it was with some trepidation that I said this, but the feeling was soon dissipated. The Epet heard my words with perfect coolness, as though they conveyed the most natural request in the world, and then proceeded to obey me, just as at home a servant might hear and obey his master, who might say, “I should like to take a ride; will you harness the bay mare?”

===Chinese===
这时我想起莱拉说过的话——科塞金人是何等心甘情愿，人想望什么，他们便做什么。她一口咬定，他们绝不会阻拦我们出逃，还叫我知道，只消开口，他们甚至肯出力相助。此刻浮上心头的正是这话，于是我决计试它一试。我便说：

「我想骑着这头阿塔莱布到空中飞一飞。你肯替它套上挽具么？」

我承认，说这话时心里不无忐忑，但这感觉随即烟消云散。埃佩特听着，神色从容之至，仿佛这是天下再自然不过的请求，接着便动手照办——就像在家里，仆人听着主人的话便去应命，那主人或许会说：「我想出去骑一趟；你去把那匹枣红母马套上好么？」

===Original===
So the Epet proceeded to harness the athaleb, and I watched him in silence; but it was the silence of deep suspense, and my heart throbbed painfully. There was yet much to be risked. The gates had to be opened. Others might interfere. Layelah might come. All these thoughts occurred to me as I watched the Epet; and though the labor of harnessing the athaleb was simple and soon performed, still the time seemed long. So the collar was secured around the neck of the athaleb, with the grapple attached, and the lines were fastened to the wings, and then Almah and I mounted.

The Epet now stood waiting for further orders.

“Open the gates,” said I.

The Epet did so.

Almah was seated on the back of the athaleb before me, holding on to the coarse mane; I, just behind, held the reins in my hand. The gates were opened wide. A few people outside, roused by the noise of the opening gates, stood and looked on. They had evidently no other feeling but curiosity.

===Chinese===
于是埃佩特动手给阿塔莱布上挽具，我默默在旁看着；但那是悬心吊胆之下的默然，我的心怦怦痛跳。要冒的险还多着呢。大门总得打开；旁人或许要来过问；莱拉说不定会来。我望着埃佩特，这些念头纷至沓来；给阿塔莱布上挽具的活计虽简单，转眼便毕，我却觉得这段时间长得难熬。就这样，项圈牢牢套上了阿塔莱布的颈子，抓钩连缀停当，缰绳系牢在双翼上，随后阿尔玛与我便跨了上去。

埃佩特随即立在一旁，听候下一步吩咐。

「把大门打开。」我说。

埃佩特依言开了。

阿尔玛坐在我前头的阿塔莱布背上，攀着那粗硬的鬃毛；我就在她身后，手里攥着缰绳。大门敞得大开。门外有几个人，被开门的声响惊动，立在那里观望。他们显然除了好奇，别无他念。

===Original===
All was now ready and the way was open, but there was an unexpected difficulty﻿—the athaleb would not start, and I did not know how to make him. I had once more to apply for help to the Epet.

“How am I to make him start?” I asked.

“Pull at the collar to make him start, and pull at both reins to make him stop,” said the Epet.

Upon this I pulled at the collar.

===Chinese===
万事俱备，去路已通，不想却出了个意料之外的难处——阿塔莱布不肯起步，我又不知怎样叫它动。我只得再度向埃佩特求助。

「我该怎么叫它起步？」我问。

「拉项圈，它便起步；双缰齐拉，它便停下。」埃佩特说。

我闻言便去拉项圈。

===Original===
The athaleb obeyed at once. He rose almost erect, and moved out through the gate. It was difficult to hold on, but we did so. On reaching the terrace outside, the athaleb expanded his vast wings, which spread out over a space of full fifty feet, and then with vigorous motions raised himself in the air.

It was a moment full of terror to both of us; the strange sensation of rising in the air, the quivering muscles of the athaleb at the working of the enormous pinions, the tremendous display of strength, all combined to overwhelm me with a sense of utter helplessness. With one hand I clung to the stiff mane of the monster; with the other I held Almah, who was also grasping the athaleb’s hair; and thus for some time all thought was taken up in the one purpose of holding on. But at length the athaleb lay in the air in a perfectly horizontal position; the beat of the wings grew more slow and even, the muscular exertion more steady and sustained. We both began to regain some degree of confidence, and at length I raised myself up and looked around.

===Chinese===
阿塔莱布应声便动。它几乎直立起身子，出了大门。攀牢不易，但我们攀住了。一到外面的梯台，阿塔莱布便张开那双巨翼——一铺开来，足有五十英尺的幅面——随即鼓起遒劲的动作，腾身跃入空中。

那一刻，我们两个都满怀恐怖；凌空而起的奇异感觉，巨翼鼓荡时阿塔莱布颤动不休的筋肉，那排山倒海的力气，交织在一处，把我彻底压倒在全然无助之感里。我一手攀牢巨怪刚硬的鬃毛，一手挽住阿尔玛，她也正紧抓着阿塔莱布的毛；就这样好一阵子，全部心思都用在「抓牢」这唯一一件事上。但阿塔莱布终于把身子平平地横卧在空中；双翼的搏动渐趋迟缓匀停，筋肉的发力也愈发沉稳绵长。我们两个开始找回了几分胆气，我终于直起身来，四下环顾。

===Original===
It did not seem long since we had left; but already the city was far behind, rising with its long, crescent terraces, sparkling and twinkling with innumerable lights. We had passed beyond the bay; the harbor was behind us, the open sea before us, the deep water beneath. The athaleb flew low, not more than a hundred feet above the water, and maintained that distance all the time. It seemed, indeed, as if he might drop into the water at any time; but this was only fancy, for he was perfect master of all his movement and his flight was swift and well sustained.

Overhead the sky was filled with the glory of the aurora beams, which spread everywhere, flashing out from the zenith and illuminating the earth with a glow brighter than that of the brightest moon; beneath, the dark waters of the sea extended, with the waves breaking into foam, and traversed by galleys, by merchant-ships, and by the navies of the Kosekin. Far away the surface of the sea spread, with that marvellous appearance of an endless ascent, as though for a thousand miles, rising thus until it terminated halfway up the sky; and so it rose up on every side, so that I seemed to be at the bottom of a basin-shaped world﻿—an immense and immeasurable hollow﻿—a world unparalleled and unintelligible. Far away, at almost infinite distances, arose the long lines of mountains, which, crowned with ice, gleamed in the aurora light, and seemed like a barrier that made forever impossible all ingress and egress.

===Chinese===
动身仿佛还在顷刻之前，那座城却已远远落在身后——长长的新月形梯台层层耸起，闪烁着、明灭着数不尽的灯火。我们已飞出湾外；海港在身后，汪洋在眼前，深水在脚下。阿塔莱布飞得低，离水面不过一百英尺，而且始终保持着这个高度。看那样子，它当真随时都会一头栽进水里；但这只是妄念，它对自己的每一个动作都驾驭纯熟，飞得迅捷而又稳当。

头顶上，满天都是极光的光华，四下弥漫，自天顶迸射而出，把大地照得比最亮的月色还亮；脚底下，黑沉沉的海面铺展而去，浪头碎作白沫，海上有桨帆船往来、商船穿行，还有科塞金人的舰队巡弋。远远地，海面漫延开去，带着那种永无止境、冉冉上升的奇观，仿佛一路爬升上千英里，直到半空方才收束；四面八方无不如是，使我恍如置身一个盆形世界的盆底——一片浩瀚无涯的凹空——一个举世无匹、无从索解的世界。远处，在几乎无穷远的距离上，耸起一行行绵长的山脉，峰顶戴冰，在极光里莹莹发亮，仿佛一道壁垒，把一切出入之路永远隔绝。

===Original===
On and on we sped. At length we grew perfectly accustomed to the situation, the motion was so easy and our seats were so secure. There were no obstacles in our way, no roughness along our path; for that pathway was the smooth air, and in such a path there could be no interruption, no jerk or jar. After the first terror had passed there remained no longer any necessity for holding on﻿—we could sit and look around with perfect freedom; and at length I rose to my feet, and Almah stood beside me, and thus we stood for a long time, with all our souls kindled into glowing enthusiasm by the excitement of that adventurous flight, and the splendors of that unequalled scene.

At length the aurora light grew dim. Then came forth the stars, glowing and burning in the black sky. Beneath there was nothing visible but the darkness of the water, spotted with phosphorescent points, while all around a wall of gloom arose which shut out from view the distant shores.

===Chinese===
我们一路疾飞，向前，再向前。到后来，我们对这处境已是安之若素：行进是那样轻稳，坐处是那样牢靠。路上没有障碍，途间没有崎岖；因为我们走的是平滑的空气，这样的路途，谈不上中断，也谈不上颠簸震撞。最初的恐怖一过，便再没有必要死死抓住什么——我们可以安坐四顾，从容自如；我终于站起身来，阿尔玛立在我身旁，我们就这样站了许久，任那场冒险飞行的激越、任那无可比拟的景象的壮丽，把整个灵魂燃成一片灼灼的热忱。

终于，极光黯淡了下去。随后星星出来了，在黑沉沉的天幕里灼灼燃烧。脚下别无所见，唯有海水的幽暗，点缀着星星点点的磷光；四面则升起一堵昏暗的高墙，把远处的海岸一概遮断。

===Original===
Suddenly I was aware of a noise like the beat of vast wings, and these wings were not those of our athaleb. At first I thought it was the fluttering of a sail, but it was too regular and too long continued for that. At length I saw through the gloom a vast shadowy form in the air behind us, and at once the knowledge of the truth flashed upon me. It was another monster flying in pursuit!

Were we pursued? Were there men on his back? Should I resist? I held my rifle poised, and was resolved to resist at all hazards. Almah saw it all, and said nothing. She perceived the danger, and in her eyes I saw that she, like me, would prefer death to surrender. The monster came nearer and nearer, until at last I could see that he was alone, and that none were on his back. But now another fear arose. He might attack our athaleb, and in that way endanger us. He must be prevented from coming nearer; yet to fire the rifle was a serious matter. I had once before I learned the danger of firing under such circumstances, when my *opmahera* had fled in terror at the report, and did not wish to experience the danger which might arise from a panic-stricken athaleb; and so as I stood there I waved my arms and gesticulated violently. The pursuing athaleb seemed frightened at such an unusual occurrence, for he veered off, and soon was lost in the darkness.

===Chinese===
蓦地，我察觉到一阵声响，像巨翼的搏动，而这双翼并不是我们这头阿塔莱布的。起初我当是船帆的拍动，但那声音太匀、太长久，帆决计不会如此。终于，我透过幽暗，看见我们身后的空中有一个庞大的黑影；真相登时在我心头一亮——那是另一头巨怪，正振翼追来！

我们是被追了么？它背上有人么？我要不要抵抗？我端平了步枪，决意不惜一切抵抗到底。阿尔玛把这一切看在眼里，一言不发。她看出了危险；而从她的眼里我看出，她同我一样，宁死也不肯就缚。那巨怪越追越近，直到最后我总算看清：它是独自一个，背上空无一人。可是这时又生出另一重恐惧。它也许会袭击我们的阿塔莱布，那样便要连累我们。断不能容它再逼近；然而开枪又非同小可。我从前已经领教过在这种情势之下开枪的危险——枪声一响，我的奥普玛赫拉曾惊惶狂奔而逃——我不愿再尝一头魂飞魄散的阿塔莱布会惹出的险；于是我立在那里，挥舞双臂，狂乱地打着手势。那追来的阿塔莱布似乎被这异常的景象吓住了，只见它一拐弯，不多时便没入黑暗之中。

## XXIII The Island of Fire / 二十三、火焰之岛

===Original===
At last there appeared before us what seemed like a long line of dull-red fires, and as we looked we could see bursts of flame at fitful intervals, which shone out for a few moments and then died away. Upon this now our whole attention was fixed; for it seemed as though we were approaching our destination, and that this place was the Island of Fire﻿—a name which, from present appearances, was fully justified. As we went on and drew steadily nearer, the mass of glowing fire grew larger and brighter, and what at first had seemed a line was broken up into different parts, one of which far surpassed the others. This was higher in the air, and its shape was that of a long, thin, sloping line, with a burning, glowing globule at each end. It seemed like lava running down from the crater of a volcano, and this appearance was made certainty on a nearer approach; for we saw at the upper point, which seemed the crater, an outburst of flame, followed by a new flow of the fiery stream. In other places there were similar fires, but they were less bright, either because they were smaller or more remote.

At length we heard beneath us the roar of breakers, and saw long white lines of surf beating upon the shore. Our athaleb now descended and alighted; we clambered to the ground, and I, taking the grapple, fixed it securely between two sharp rocks. We were at last on Magones, the Island of Fire.

===Chinese===
终于，我们前方浮现出一道长长的暗红色火光，望去宛如一线；凝望之间，但见烈焰阵阵迸发，时作时歇，亮得片刻，随即熄灭。此刻我们的全副心神都贯注在这上面：看来我们正在步步逼近目的地，而这地方便是火焰之岛——单看眼前的景象，这名字可谓名实相副。我们继续前行，愈逼愈近，那团炽燃的火光愈见阔大、愈见明亮；起初貌似一线的，此刻已分作数段，其中一段远远压过其余。它悬在半空更高处，形状是一条细长的斜线，两端各顶着一团灼亮耀眼的火球。那模样像是熔岩正从火山口流淌而下；待行得更近，这观感便成了定论——因为我们望见上端那处像是火山口的地方迸出一阵烈焰，紧接着便有一股新的火流随之泻下。别处也有几处相仿的火，只是光色较暗，或因其规模较小，或因其距离更远。

终于，我们听见脚下传来碎浪的轰响，望见一道道长长的白色浪线拍击着海岸。我们的阿塔莱布随即降落着地；我们攀下地来，我取过抓钩，把它牢牢地卡进两块尖岩之间。我们终于踏上了马戈内斯——这火焰之岛。

===Original===
The brightness of the aurora light had left us, but it needed not this to show us the dismal nature of the land to which we had come. It was a land of horror, where there was nothing but the abomination of desolation﻿—a land overstrewn with blasted fragments of fractured lava-blocks, intermixed with sand, from which there arose black precipices and giant mountains that poured forth rivers of fire and showers of ashes and sheets of flame. A tremendous peak arose before us, with a crest of fire and sides streaked with red torrents of molten lava; between us and it there spread away a vast expanse of impassable rocks﻿—a scene of ruin and savage wildness which cannot be described, and all around was the same drear and appalling prospect. Here in the night-season﻿—the season of darkness and of awful gloom﻿—we stood in this land of woe; and not one single sign appeared of life save the life that we had brought with us. As for food, it was vain to think of it. To search after it would be useless. It seemed, indeed, impossible to move from the spot where we were. Every moment presented some new discovery which added to the horror of Magones.

But Almah was weary, for our flight had been long, and she wished to rest. So I found a place for her where there was some sand between two rocks, and here she lay down and went to sleep. I sat at a little distance off on a shelf of the rock, with my back against it, and here, after a little time, I also went to sleep.

===Chinese===
南极光的皎亮光华已离我们而去，但无需它来照见这片土地的阴惨本性。这是一片恐怖之地，触目惟有荒凉败坏的可憎之物——遍地铺满碎裂熔岩块的焦黑残砾，间杂着沙土；其间拔地而起的，是黑色的峭壁与巍巍的群山，山中倾泻出火的河流、灰烬的骤雨，以及一幅幅烈焰的帷幔。一座骇人的奇峰矗立在我们面前，峰顶冠着一环火，两侧被一道道赤红的熔岩激流划出条条纹路；在我们与它之间，铺展开一大片辽阔无边、无法逾越的乱石——一派毁灭与蛮荒的景象，非笔墨所能形容；环顾四周，处处是同样凄凉骇人的光景。在这黑夜的时节——黑暗与可怕阴霾的时节——我们伫立在这片苦厄之地；除了我们随身携来的生命，不见一丝生灵的踪迹。至于食物，想也不必想；寻觅也是徒劳。说实在的，连挪动脚步离开所立之处，似乎都无从做到。时时刻刻都有新的发现，为马戈内斯的恐怖再添一重。

但阿尔玛已困乏不堪——我们的逃亡航程实在太长了，她渴望歇息。于是我替她寻了一处地方，两块岩石之间正好有些沙，她便在那里躺下睡去。我在稍远处一块岩台上坐下，背倚着岩石，过了不多时，也睡着了。

===Original===
At length we awoke. But what a waking! There was no morning dawn, no blessed returning light to greet our eyes. We opened our eyes to the same scenes upon which we had closed them, and the darkness was still deep and dense around us. Over us both there was a sense of utter depression, and I was so deeply plunged into it that I found it impossible to rouse myself, even for the sake of saying words of cheer to Almah. I had brought a few fragments of food, and upon these we made our breakfast; but there was the athaleb to feed, and for him I found nothing, nor could I think of anything﻿—unless he could feed upon rocks and sand. Yet food for him was a matter of the highest consequence, for he was all our support and stay and hope; and if the monster were deprived of food he might turn upon us and satisfy upon us his ravenous appetite. These thoughts were painful indeed, and added to my despondency.

Suddenly I heard the sound of running water. I started away toward the place from which the sound came, and found, only a little distance off, a small brook trickling along on its way to the shore. I called Almah, and we both drank and were refreshed.

This showed an easy way to get to the shore, and I determined to go there to see if there were any fish to be found. Shellfish might be there, or the carcasses of dead fish thrown up by the sea, upon which the athaleb might feed. I left my pistol with Almah, telling her to fire it if she heard me fire, for I was afraid of losing my way, and therefore took this precaution. I left it lying on the rock full-cocked, and directed her to point it in the air and pull the trigger. It was necessary to take these precautions, as of course she was quite ignorant of its nature. After this I left her and tried to follow the torrent.

===Chinese===
我们终于醒了。可这是怎样的一种醒来啊！没有破晓的晨光，没有蒙福重临的光明来迎接我们的眼睛。我们睁开眼，看见的仍是合眼时所见的那些景象，四周依旧是深沉浓重的黑暗。我们两人心头都压着一种全然的消沉，而我沉溺其中之深，竟至无力振作，连为阿尔玛说几句宽心话也办不到。我随身带了些零碎的食物，我们便以此当了早餐；可是还有阿塔莱布要喂，我替它寻不到一点吃食，也想不出它能吃什么——除非它能拿岩石和沙土果腹。然而它的吃食却是天大的要事，因为它是我们全部的倚靠、支柱与希望；这巨怪若是断了粮，说不定会掉头向我们扑来，拿我们两个去填它那贪婪无厌的胃口。这些念头着实苦楚，愈发添了我的沮丧。

忽然，我听见了流水的声音。我循声赶去，发现就在不远处，一条小溪正潺潺流向岸边。我唤来阿尔玛，我们俩喝了个痛快，精神为之一爽。

这便指给我们一条去海边的便道，我决意到那里去看看能否寻到什么鱼。那里或许有贝类，或许有海水抛上岸的死鱼残尸，阿塔莱布或可以此为食。我把手枪留给阿尔玛，嘱咐她若听见我放枪，便也照着放一枪；我怕迷失路径，故而作了这一防备。我把枪搁在岩石上，扳起击锤，置成待发之势，又指点她朝天举枪、扣动扳机。这些嘱咐是少不得的，因为她对这物件的本性自然一无所知。此后我便撇下她，试着沿溪而行。

===Original===
This, however, I soon found to be impossible, for the brook on reaching a huge rock plunged underneath it and became lost to view. I then went toward the shore as well as I could﻿—now climbing over sharp rocks, now going round them, until at length after immense labor I succeeded in reaching the water. Here the scene was almost as wild as the one I had left. There was no beach whatever﻿—nothing but a vast extent of wild fragments of fractured lava-blocks, which were evidently the result of some comparatively recent convulsion of nature, for their edges were still sharp, and the water had not worn even those which were within its grasp to anything like roundness, or to anything else than the jagged and shattered outlines which had originally belonged to them. All the shore thus consisted of vast rocky blocks, over which the sea beat in foam.

Eager to find something, I toiled along this rocky shore for a long distance, but without seeing any change. I was unwilling to go back baffled, yet I was at length compelled to do so. But the necessity of feeding the athaleb was pressing, and I saw that our only course now would be to mount him again, leave this place, and seek some other. But where could we go? That I could not imagine, and could only conclude to trust altogether to the instinct of the athaleb, which might guide him to places where he might obtain food. Such a course would involve great risk for we might be carried into the midst of vast flocks of these monsters; yet there was nothing else to be done.

===Chinese===
然而我很快便发现这行不通，因为那小溪流到一块巨岩跟前，便一头钻进岩下，从此不见踪影。于是我只好竭尽所能朝海边行去——时而攀过尖利的岩石，时而绕岩而走，直到费了偌大的气力，终于抵达水边。这里的景象，几乎同我离开的那一处同样蛮荒。这里根本没有海滩可言——惟余一片茫茫无际的碎裂熔岩残块，狼藉满目；这些岩块显然是较近某次天崩地裂的产物，因为它们的棱边犹自锋利，连那些落在海水掌握之内的，也未被磨蚀成什么浑圆的模样，仍是它们生成之初那副参差破碎的轮廓。整条海岸便由这些巨大的岩块构成，海涛在其上迸碎成雪沫。

我一心想寻出点什么，便沿着这岩石海岸辛苦跋涉了老远，却不见任何变化。我不情愿铩羽而归，可到头来也只得如此。但喂饱阿塔莱布乃燃眉之急，我明白如今唯一的出路，便是再度骑上它，离开此地，另觅他处。可我们能去哪里呢？这我无从想象，只能决计全然听凭阿塔莱布的本能，它或能引我们到它觅得食物的地方。这样行事风险极大，因为我们可能被载进这些巨怪成群结队的渊薮之中；然而除此之外，也别无他法。

===Original===
