I now retraced my steps, and went for a long time near the sea. At length I found a place where the walking was somewhat easier, and went in this way up into the island and away from the sea. It seemed to lead in the direction where I wished to go. At length it seemed as if I had walked far enough, yet I could see no signs of Almah. I shouted, but there was no answer. I shouted again and again, but with the like result. Then I fired my rifle and listened. In response there came the report of the pistol far away behind me. It was evident that in coming back along the shore I had passed by the place where Almah was. There was nothing now left but to retrace my steps and this I accordingly did. I went back to the shore, and returned on my steps, shouting all the time, until at length I was rejoiced to hear the answering shout of Almah. After this it was easy to reach her.

We now took up the grapple and once more mounted. The athaleb, eager to be off, raised himself quickly in the air, and soon our late resting-place was far behind. His flight was now different from what it was before. Then he stood off in one straight line for a certain fixed destination, as though under some guidance; for though I did not direct him, still his long training had taught him to fly to Magones. But now training and guidance were both wanting, and the athaleb was left to the impulse of his hunger and the guidance of his instinct; so he flew no longer in one undeviating straight line, but rose high, and bent his head down low, and flew and soared in vast circles, even as I have seen a vulture or a condor sweep about while searching for food. All the while we were drawing farther and farther away from the spot which we had left.

===Chinese===
我这才循原路折返，沿着海边走了许久。终于寻到一处步履稍易的地方，便由着它走进岛内，离海而去。这路似乎通向我要去的方向。走了许久，自觉路程已足，却仍不见阿尔玛的踪影。我高声呼喊，无人应答；我喊了一遍又一遍，结果依然。于是我便开了步枪，侧耳倾听。作为回应，远远地在我身后传来一声手枪的响声。显然，我沿岸折返时，已走过了阿尔玛所在的地方。如今别无他法，唯有再折返一遭，我也就照办了。我回到岸边，一路呼喊着循来路走回，直到终于欢欢喜喜地听见阿尔玛应答的呼喊。此后要寻到她，便容易了。

我们收起抓钩，再度上骑。阿塔莱布急欲启程，倏地腾入空中，不多时，方才歇脚之处便远远落在后面。它此刻的飞法与先前判然不同。先前，它朝着某个固定的目的地循一条直线进发，仿佛冥冥中有所指引；因为虽然并非由我驾驭，它多年的调教已教会它直飞马戈内斯。可如今调教与指引两皆阙如，阿塔莱布只能任凭饥饿的驱迫、本能的导航；于是它不再循一条不偏不倚的直线飞行，而是扶摇直上，俯垂着头，盘旋回翔，兜出一个又一个巨大的圆圈，恰如我见过的秃鹫或神鹰觅食时回翔的样子。这一路，我们离那弃置之处愈行愈远。

===Original===
We passed the lofty volcano; we saw more plainly the rivers of molten lava; we passed vast cliffs and bleak mountains, all of which were more terrific than all that we had left behind. Now the darkness lessened, for the aurora was brightening in the sky, and gathering up swiftly and gloriously all its innumerable beams, and flashing forth its lustrous glow upon the world. To us this was equal to the return of day; it was like a blessed dawn. Light had come, and we rejoiced and were exceeding glad.

Now we saw before us, far beyond the black precipices, a broad bay with sloping shores, and a wide beach which seemed like a beach of sand. The surf broke here, but beyond the surf was the gentle sandy declivity, and beyond this there appeared the shores, still rocky and barren and desolate, but far preferable to what we had left behind. Far away in the interior arose lofty mountains and volcanoes, while behind us flamed the burning peak which we had passed.

Here the athaleb wheeled in long, circuitous flights, which grew lower and lower, until at length he descended upon the sandy beach, where I saw a vast sea-monster lying dead. It had evidently been thrown up here by the sea. It was like one of those monsters which I had seen from the galley of the Kohen at the time of the sacred hunt. By this the athaleb descended, and at once began to devour it, tearing out vast masses of flesh, and exhibiting such voracity and strength of jaw that I could scarcely bear to look upon the sight. I fastened the grapple securely to the head of the dead monster, and leaving the athaleb to feed upon it, Almah and I went up the beach.

===Chinese===
我们掠过高峻的火山，把熔岩的河流看得愈加分明；我们掠过森然巨崖与荒寒群山——这一切，比我们撇在身后的一切更加可怖。此时黑暗渐渐收敛，因为南极光正在天空中亮起，迅疾而壮丽地聚拢它无数的光束，向这世界闪射出灿然的光华。在我们看来，这无异于白昼重临；恰似一场蒙福的黎明。光明来了，我们欢欣鼓舞，喜不自胜。

此刻我们望见前方，在黑色峭壁远远以外，有一片坡岸徐缓的宽阔海湾，还有一道宽广的海滩，望去似是沙岸。浪涛在那里拍碎；浪花之外是平缓的沙坡，再往外便是海岸，依旧岩石嶙峋、荒瘠不毛，却远胜我们撇在身后的那一切。腹地深处远远耸起高山与火山，而我们身后，那座方才掠过的燃烧的峰峦犹自烈焰熊熊。

在这里，阿塔莱布兜着又长又绕的大圈子盘旋下降，越飞越低，终于落在那片沙岸上；岸上横卧着一具硕大无朋的海怪死尸，显系被海浪抛掷上岸。它同我在神圣狩猎之际从科恩的桨帆船上望见的那些巨怪正是同类。阿塔莱布在它旁边落定，随即开始撕吞大嚼，一块块巨肉被生生扯出，那副饕餮的凶相与双颚的蛮力，令我几乎不忍卒睹。我把抓钩牢牢拴在那死怪的头上，任阿塔莱布就地进食，我同阿尔玛便朝海滩上方走去。

===Original===
On our way we found rocks covered with seaweed, and here we sought after shellfish. Our search was at length rewarded, for suddenly I stumbled upon a place where I found some lobsters. I grasped two of these, but the others escaped. Here at last I had found signs of life, but they were of the sea rather than of the shore. Delighted with my prey, I hastened to Almah to show them to her. She recognized them at once, and I saw that they were familiar to her. I then spoke of eating them, but at this proposal she recoiled in horror. She could not give any reason for her repugnance, but merely said that among her people they were regarded as something equivalent to vermin, and I found that she would no more think of eating one than I would think of eating a rat. Upon this I had to throw them away, and we once more resumed our search.

At last we came to a place where numbers of dead fish lay on the sand. Nearer the water they were more fresh, and not at all objectionable. I picked up a few which looked like our common smelt, and found that Almah had no objection to these. But now the question arose how to cook them; neither of us could eat them raw. A fire was necessary, yet a fire was impossible; for on the whole island there was probably not one single combustible thing. Our discovery, therefore, seemed to have done us but little good, and we seemed destined to starvation, when fortunately a happy thought suggested itself. In walking along I saw far away the glow of some lava which had flowed to the shore at the end of the sandy beach, and was probably cooling down at the water’s edge. Here, then, was a natural fire, which might serve us better than any contrivance of our own, and toward this we at once proceeded. It was about two miles away; but the beach was smooth, and we reached the place without any difficulty.

===Chinese===
一路行去，我们看见岩石上覆满海藻，便在这里搜寻起贝类来。搜寻终于得了回报——蓦然间，我撞见一处有龙虾的地方。我一把抓住两只，其余的都逃掉了。至此我总算寻到了生命的迹象，只是那生命属于海，而非属于岸。得了这猎物，我喜不自胜，忙赶到阿尔玛那里给她看。她一眼便认了出来，我看出这是她素来熟识之物。于是我提起把它们吃掉；可一听这话，她骇然退缩。她说不出这憎恶的所以然，只说在她族人那里，这东西被视同虫豸害物一般；我发现，她之断不肯吃它，恰如我之断不肯吃一只老鼠。无可奈何，我只得把它们抛掉，我们便重新继续搜寻。

终于，我们来到一处所在，沙上横陈着许多死鱼。离水越近，鱼越新鲜，全无可嫌。我拾起几尾，模样像我家乡寻常的胡瓜鱼，阿尔玛对这些倒并无异议。可这时问题来了：怎样把它们弄熟？生吃是我们俩谁也吃不下的。必须有火，而生火又断无可能；因为整座岛上，恐怕找不出一样可燃之物。如此说来，我们的发现似乎于事无补，我们似已命定要忍饥挨饿——幸好这时灵光一现。原来一路行来，我望见远处有熔岩的微光，那熔岩流到了沙岸尽处的水边，多半正在水缘冷却。这不正是天然的火么？它比我们自己想出的任何法子都更济事；我们当即朝那里进发。那地方约在两英里开外；好在沙岸平坦，我们一路顺遂，便到了。

===Original===
Here we found the edge of that lava flood which seemed eternally descending from the crater beyond. The edge which was nearest the water was black; and the liquid fire, as it rolled down, curled over this in a fantastic shape, cooling and hardening into the form which it thus assumed. Here, after some search, I found a crevice where I could approach the fire, and I laid the fish upon a crimson rock, which was cooling and hardening into the shape of a vast ledge of lava. In this way, by the aid of nature, the fish were broiled, and we made our repast.

There was nothing here to invite a longer stay, and we soon returned to the athaleb. We found the monster, gorged with food, asleep, resting upon his hind legs, with his breast supported against the vast carcass. Almah called it a *jantannin*. It was about sixty feet in length and twenty in thickness, with a vast horny head, ponderous jaws, and back covered with scales. Its eyes were of prodigious size, and it had the appearance of a crocodile, with the vast size of a whale. It was unlike a crocodile, however; for it had fins rather than paws, and must have been as clumsy on the land as a seal or a walrus. It lay on its side, and the athaleb had fed itself from the uncovered flesh of its belly.

===Chinese===
在这里，我们寻到了那股熔岩洪流的边缘——它仿佛正从彼方的火山口亘古不息地泻下。离水最近的边缘已转作黑色；液态的火滚落下来，在这黑缘上翻卷出奇形怪状，一面冷却，一面凝定成它所形成的模样。我在这里寻了片刻，找到一道可以凑近火边的裂罅，便把鱼搁上一块殷红的岩石——那岩石也正在冷却硬化，凝成一大片熔岩台地之形。就这样，借大自然之力，鱼烤熟了，我们进了一餐。

这里没有什么引人流连之处，我们不久便回到阿塔莱布那里。只见这巨怪吃得腹饱肠满，已然睡去，后腿支地，胸膛倚在那庞大的尸身上。阿尔玛说，这死怪名叫詹坦宁（jantannin）。它长约六十英尺，粗约二十英尺，一颗庞大的角质头颅，双颚沉重，背上覆满鳞甲。它的眼睛大得骇人，形貌似鳄鱼，身躯却庞然如鲸。不过它又与鳄鱼不同：它长的不是脚爪而是鳍，在陆上想必同海豹或海象一般笨拙。它侧身躺着，阿塔莱布便是从它袒露的腹部下口饱餐的。

===Original===
There was nothing here to induce us to stay, and so we wandered along the beach in the other direction. On our right was the bay; on our left the rocky shore, which, beginning at the beach, ran back into the country, a waste of impassable rocks, where not a tree or plant or blade of grass relieved the appalling desolation. Once or twice we made an attempt to penetrate into the country, where openings appeared. These openings seemed like the beds of dried-up torrents. We were able to walk but a few paces for invariably we would come to some immense blocks of rock, which barred all farther progress. In this way we explored the beach for miles, until it terminated in a savage promontory that rose abruptly from the sea against which the huge billows broke in thunder.

Then we retraced our steps, and again reached the spot where the athaleb was asleep by the *jantannin*. Almah was now too weary to walk any farther, nor was it desirable to do so; for, indeed, we had traversed all that could be visited. On one side of the beach was the sea, on the other the impassable rocks; at one end the promontory, at the other the lava fires. There was nothing more for us to do but to wait here until the athaleb should awake, and then our actions would depend upon what we might now decide.

===Chinese===
这里没有什么让我们留下的理由，于是我们沿着海滩朝另一头信步走去。右边是海湾，左边是岩石的海岸——它自海滩起便折入内陆，成了一片无法逾越的乱石荒原，没有一株树、一丛草木、一茎草叶，来稍稍纾解那骇人的荒凉。有一两回，我们试着从显露的缺口处深入内陆。这些缺口看去像是干涸的溪床。可行不出几步，总不免撞上巨大的岩块，把去路堵得严严实实。就这样，我们把这片海滩踏勘了数英里，直到它尽处，一座蛮荒的海岬拔海突起，巨浪扑击其上，訇然如雷。

于是我们循原路折回，又到了阿塔莱布在詹坦宁旁酣睡的地方。这时阿尔玛已倦极，再走不动了，其实也不必再走；因为凡是能踏勘的地方，我们都已走遍。海滩一侧是海，另一侧是无法逾越的乱石；一头是海岬，另一头是熔岩之火。我们再无别事可做，惟有在此守候阿塔莱布醒来；而醒来之后何去何从，便要依我们此刻如何定夺了。

===Original===
This was the question that was now before us, and this we began to consider. We both felt the most unspeakable aversion for the island, and to remain here any longer was impossible. We would once more have to mount the athaleb, and proceed to some other shore. But where? Ah! there was the question! Not on the island, for it did not seem possible that in all its extent there could be one single spot capable of affording a resting-place. Layelah’s information with regard to Magones had made that much plain. I had not taken in her full meaning, but now mine eyes had seen it. Yet where else could we go? Almah could not tell where under the sky lay that land which she loved; I could not guess where to go to find the land of the Orin. Even if I did know, I did not feel able to guide the course of the athaleb; and I felt sure that if we were to mount again, the mighty monster would wing his flight back to the very place from which we had escaped﻿—the *amir*. These thoughts weighed down our spirits. We felt that we had gained nothing by our flight, and that our future was dark indeed. The only hope left us was that we might be able to guide the course of the athaleb in some different direction altogether, so that we should not be carried back to the Kosekin.

And now, worn out by the long fatigues of this *jom*, we thought of sleep. Almah lay down upon the sand, and I seated myself, leaning against a rock, a little distance off, having first reloaded my rifle and pistol.

===Chinese===
这便是摆在我们面前的问题，我们开始细细筹议。我们两人对这座岛都怀有说不出的憎厌，再在此地逗留片刻也是断然不能的了。我们只得再一次骑上阿塔莱布，往别的海岸去。可是，去哪里呢？唉！症结正在于此！岛上是不成的，因为偌大一片幅员，似乎断乎寻不出一处可供栖身之地。莱拉关于马戈内斯的一番话，早把这一点讲得明明白白。她话中的全部深意，我当时未能尽解，如今却是我亲眼看见了。可除此之外，我们又能往哪里去呢？阿尔玛说不出，她所爱的那片土地究竟在天底下哪一方；我猜不出，该往何处去寻奥林人之地。纵然我知道，我也自觉无力驾驭阿塔莱布的航向；而且我深信，我们若再度升空，这庞然巨怪多半会振翅飞回我们逃出的那个地方——阿米尔。这些念头压得我们意气消沉。我们觉得这场逃亡一无所获，而我们的前途实在黯淡。留给我们的唯一指望，是或许能引着阿塔莱布朝一个全然不同的方向飞去，使我们不致被带回科塞金人那里。

这时，这一约姆的漫长劳顿已使我们筋疲力竭，我们便想到了睡眠。阿尔玛在沙上躺下；我先给步枪和手枪重新装好了弹药，然后在稍远处拣一块岩石倚着坐下。

## XXIV Recapture / 二十四、再度被俘

===Original===
How long I slept I do not know; but in the midst of my sleep there sounded voices, which at first intermingled themselves with my dreams, but gradually became separate and sounded from without, rousing me from my slumbers. I opened my eyes drowsily, but the sight that I saw was so amazing that in an instant all sleep left me. I started to my feet, and gazed in utter bewilderment upon the scene before me.

The aurora light was shining with unusual brilliancy, and disclosed everything﻿—the sea, the shore, the athaleb, the *jantannin*, the promontory, all﻿—more plainly and more luminously than before; but it was not any of these things that now excited my attention and rendered me dumb. I saw Almah standing there at a little distance, with despairing face, surrounded by a band of armed Kosekin; while immediately before me, regarding me with a keen glance and an air of triumph, was Layelah.

===Chinese===
睡了多少时辰，我无从知晓；酣睡正浓之际，忽听得人声阵阵，起初还同梦境纠缠一处，渐渐便分离开来，自外面传来，将我从沉睡中唤醒。我睡眼惺忪地睁开双眼，可眼前所见实在太骇人，霎时间睡意荡然无存。我一跃而起，怔怔地望着面前的景象，全然摸不着头脑。

极光正大放光明，亮得出奇，把一切——海面、海岸、阿塔莱布、詹坦宁、那道海岬，一概——照得比先前更清楚、更透亮；然而此刻教我屏息噤声的，却不是这些东西。我看见阿尔玛立在不远处，满面绝望，被一队持械的科塞金人围在当中；而就在我眼前，莱拉正目光灼灼地打量着我，一脸得胜的神气。

===Original===
“*Atæsmzori alonla*,” said she, with a sweet smile, giving me the usual salutation of the Kosekin.

I was too bewildered to say a word, and stood mute as before, looking first at her and then at Almah.

===Chinese===
“阿特斯姆佐里·阿隆拉（Atæsmzori alonla）。”她面带甜甜的微笑说道，向我致了科塞金人平日的见面问候。

我心神恍惚，一句话也说不出，只像先前一样哑然立着，看看她，又看看阿尔玛。

===Original===
The sight of Almah a prisoner once more, surrounded by the Kosekin, excited me to madness. I seized my rifle, and raised it as if to take aim; but Almah, who understood the movement, cried to me:

“Put down your *sepet-ram*, Atam-or! you can do nothing for me. The Kosekin are too numerous.”

“*Sepet-ram!*” said Layelah; “what do you mean by that? If your *sepet-ram* has any power, do not try to use it, Atam-or, or else I shall have to order my followers to give to Almah the blessing of death.”

===Chinese===
眼见阿尔玛再度沦为囚徒，被科塞金人团团围住，我登时怒不可遏。我抓起步枪，举起来作势瞄准；阿尔玛明白这一举动的意思，朝我喊道：

“放下你的塞佩特-拉姆，阿塔莫尔！你救不了我。科塞金人太多了。”

“塞佩特-拉姆！”莱拉道，“你说这话是什么意思？倘若你的塞佩特-拉姆当真有什么威力，也千万别动用它，阿塔莫尔，不然我只得吩咐随从们，把死亡的福分赐给阿尔玛了。”

===Original===
At this my rifle was lowered: the whole truth flashed upon me, and I saw, too, the madness of resistance. I might kill one or two, but the rest would do as Layelah said, and I should speedily be disarmed. Well I knew how powerless were the thunders of my firearms to terrify these Kosekin; for the prospect of death would only rouse them to a mad enthusiasm, and they would all rush upon me as they would rush upon a *jantannin*﻿—to slay and be slain. The odds were too great. A crowd of Europeans could be held in check far more easily than these death-loving Kosekin. The whole truth was thus plain: we were prisoners, and were at their mercy.

Layelah showed no excitement or anger whatever. She looked and spoke in her usual gracious and amiable fashion, with a sweet smile on her face.

===Chinese===
听到这话，我的枪口垂了下来：全部真相在心头一闪而过，我也看出了负隅顽抗是何等疯狂。我或许能打死一两个，可余下的必定照莱拉的话行事，转眼间我就会被缴了械。我心里明白得很，我这几杆火器的雷霆，吓不住这些科塞金人；因为一想到死，他们反倒会被煽起一阵疯狂的狂热，个个朝我扑来，如同扑向一头詹坦宁一般——去杀，也去送死。众寡悬殊，太悬殊了。要震住一群欧洲人，也远比震住这些恋死的科塞金人容易。事情就此昭然：我们已是阶下之囚，生死全操在人家手里。

莱拉却分毫不见激动，也不见恼怒。她的神色口吻，一如平日的雍容和婉，脸上还挂着甜甜的微笑。

===Original===
“We knew,” said she, “that you would be in distress in this desolate place, and that you would not know where to go from Magones; and so we have come, full of the most eager desire to relieve your wants. We have brought with us food and drink, and are ready to do everything for you that you may desire. We have had great trouble in finding you, and have coursed over the shores for vast distances, and far over the interior, but our athalebs found you at last by their scent. And we rejoice to have found you in time, and that you are both so well, for we have been afraid that you had been suffering. Nay, Atam-or, do not thank us; thanks are distasteful to the Kosekin: these brave followers of mine will all be amply rewarded for this, for they will all be made paupers; but as for myself, I want no higher reward than the delightful thought that I have saved you from suffering.”

===Chinese===
“我们早知道，”她说道，“你们在这荒凉地方必定困顿不堪，离开马戈内斯之后也不知道该往哪里去；所以我们便来了，怀着最热切的心愿，要解你们的匮乏。我们带来了吃的喝的，你们想要什么，我们都乐于照办。寻找你们着实费了事，沿海岸来回搜了不知多少路，内地的远处也搜遍了，最后还是我们的阿塔莱布循着气味把你们寻着了。能及时寻到你们，见你们二位安然无恙，我们都欢喜得很，先前还只怕你们受了苦呢。不必谢我，阿塔莫尔，千万别谢；谢意是科塞金人最不受用的东西：我这班勇敢的随从，这一趟人人都要得一份厚赏，个个都会被封为赤贫者；至于我自己，一想到我把你们从苦难里解救了出来，这份快意便是给我多高的赏格我也不换。”

===Original===
The beautiful, smiling Layelah, who addressed me in this way with her sweet voice, was certainly not to be treated as an enemy. Against her a rifle could not be levelled; she would have looked at me with the same sweet smile, and that smile would have melted all my resolution. Nor could I even persist in my determination to remain. Remain! For what? For utter despair! And yet where else could we go?

“You do not know where lie the lands of the Orin,” said Layelah. “The athaleb does not know. You could not guide him if you did know. You are helpless on his back. The art of driving an athaleb is difficult, and cannot be learned without long and severe practice. My fear was that the athaleb might break away from you and return, leaving you to perish here. Had you tried to leave this place he would have brought you back to the *amir*.”

===Chinese===
这位美丽的、笑吟吟的莱拉，用这样甜蜜的声音对我说话，实在没法把她当作敌人看待。枪口是不能对准她的；她只会照样朝我甜甜一笑，而那一笑就会把我全部的决心都融化掉。我连留在此地的决心也坚持不下去了。留下！为什么留下？为了彻底的绝望么！可除此之外，我们还能到哪里去呢？

“你并不知道奥林人的国度在哪里，”莱拉道，“阿塔莱布也不知道。就算你知道，你也驾驭不了它。你在它背上全然无能为力。驾驭阿塔莱布是一门难功夫，不经年深日久的苦练是学不会的。我先前担心的，正是阿塔莱布挣脱你们自己飞回去，撇下你们死在这里。你们若真想离开此地，它也只会把你们驮回阿米尔去。”

===Original===
To this I said nothing﻿—partly because it was so true that I had no answer to make, and partly also out of deep mortification and dejection. My pride was wounded at being thus so easily baffled by a girl like Layelah, and all my grief was stirred by the sadness of Almah. In her eyes there seemed even now the look of one who sees death inevitable, and the glance she gave to me was like an eternal farewell.

Almah now spoke, addressing herself to Layelah.

“Death,” said she, in a voice of indescribable mournfulness, “is better here than with you. We would rather die here than go back. Let us, I pray you, receive the blessing of death here. Let us be paupers and exiles, and die on Magones.”

===Chinese===
这话我无言以对——一半因为它太真了，真得叫我无从分辩；一半也是出于深重的羞愤与沮丧。竟被莱拉这样一个女子如此轻巧地挫败，我的自尊大受创伤；而阿尔玛的哀伤，又把我满腔悲戚尽数搅起。她的眼中，此刻仿佛仍带着那种眼见死期已定的人才有的神色；她投向我的那一瞥，竟像是一场永诀。

这时阿尔玛开口了，向着莱拉说道。

“死，”她说，声音里有一种难以言传的悲怆，“在这里死，也强似回去死在你们中间。我们宁死在这里，也不回去。求求你，就让我们在此地领受死亡的福分吧。让我们做赤贫者、做流亡者，死在马戈内斯。”

===Original===
Layelah heard this, and stood for a moment in deep thought.

“No one but a stranger,” said she at length, “would ask such a favor as that. Do you not know that what you ask is among the very highest honors of the Kosekin? Who am I that I can venture to grant such a request as that? Ask for anything in my power, and I will be glad to grant it. I have already arranged that you shall be separated from Atam-or; and that, surely, is a high privilege. I might consent to bind you hand and foot, after the manner of the more distinguished *Asirin*; you may also be blindfolded if you wish it. I might even promise, after we return to the *amir*, to keep you confined in utter darkness, with barely sufficient food to keep you alive until the time of the sacrifice; in short, there is no blessing known among the Kosekin that I will not give so long as it is in my power. And so, beloved Almah,” continued Layelah, “you have every reason for happiness; you have all the highest blessings known among the Kosekin: separation from your lover, poverty, want, darkness; and, finally, the prospect of inevitable death ever before you as the crowning glory of your lot.”

===Chinese===
莱拉听了这话，伫立片刻，深深思索着。

“除了外乡人，”她终于开口，“谁也不会讨这样的恩典。你难道不知道，你所求的正是科塞金人至高的荣典之一么？我算什么人，怎敢贸然应允这样的请求？凡是我力所能及的，你只管求，我都乐意应允。我已经安排定了，要把你同阿塔莫尔分开；这不用说，是一重极高的恩宠。我还可以答应把你手脚捆缚，比照那些更尊贵的阿西林（Asirin）的体例；你若愿意，还可以蒙上眼睛。我甚至可以许诺，回到阿米尔之后，把你囚在十足的黑暗里，只给一点苟延性命的口粮，一直养到献祭的日子；总而言之，但凡科塞金人知道的福分，只要在我权内，没有一样我不肯给你的。所以，亲爱的阿尔玛，”莱拉接着说道，“你满有欢喜的理由了；科塞金人知道的至高福分，你已样样齐备：与爱人分离、赤贫、匮乏、黑暗；末了还有那无可回避的死亡，永远悬在眼前，作你这命数加冕的荣光。”

===Original===
These words seemed to the Kosekin the very excess of magnanimity, and involuntary murmurs of admiration escaped them; although it is just possible that they murmured at the greatness of the favor that was offered. But to me it sounded like fiendish mockery, and to Almah it sounded the same; for a groan escaped her, her fortitude gave way, she sank on her knees, buried her head in her hands, and wept.

“Almah,” cried I, in a fury, “we will not go back﻿—we will not be separated! I will destroy all the athalebs, and we shall all perish here together. At least, you and I will not be separated.”

At this Almah started up.

“No, no,” said she﻿—“no; let us go back. Here we have nothing but death.”

“But we have death also at the *amir*, and a more terrible one,” said I.

===Chinese===
这些话在科塞金人听来，实在是宽宏大量的极致，不由得啧啧称羡；不过他们的啧啧之声，或许也正为着这份恩典太过浩大。可在我听来，这无异于魔鬼的嘲弄，在阿尔玛听来也是一样；只听她一声呜咽，强撑的坚毅溃散了，双膝跪倒，把头埋进手里，哭了。

“阿尔玛！”我狂怒地喊道，“我们不回去——我们决不分开！我要把这些阿塔莱布统统杀死，大家一起死在这里。至少，你我是决不分开了。”

阿尔玛闻声跳了起来。

“不，不，”她说，“不；我们回去吧。在这里，我们只有死路一条。”

“可是回到阿米尔，等着我们的也是死，而且是更可怕的死。”我说。

===Original===
“If you kill the athalebs,” said Layelah, “I will give Almah the blessing of death.”

At this I recoiled in horror, and my resolution again gave way.

“You have some mysterious power of conferring death,” continued Layelah, “with what Almah calls your *sepet-ram*; but do not kill the athalebs, for it will do you no good. Almah would then receive the blessing of death. My followers, these noble Kosekin, would rejoice in thus gaining exile and death on Magones. As for myself, it would be my highest happiness to be here alone with you. With you I should live for a few sweet *joms*, and with you I should die; so go on﻿—kill the athalebs if you wish.”

“Do not!” cried Almah﻿—“do not! There is no hope. We are their prisoners, and our only hope is in submission.”

===Chinese===
“你若杀死阿塔莱布，”莱拉道，“我就把死亡的福分赐给阿尔玛。”

我吓得倒退一步，决心又动摇了。

“你手里确有一种致人死命的神秘力量，”莱拉接着说，“就是阿尔玛所谓的你的塞佩特-拉姆；可别杀阿塔莱布，杀了于你并无好处。阿尔玛立时便会领受死亡的福分。我的随从们，这些高贵的科塞金人，正可欢欢喜喜得一场流放，死在马戈内斯。至于我自己，能同你单独留在这里，便是我至高的幸福了。同你在一起，我还可以活上几个甜美的约姆，然后同你一道死；所以动手吧——你若愿意，就把阿塔莱布杀掉。”

“别动手！”阿尔玛喊道，“别动手！没有指望了。我们已是他们的囚徒，唯一的指望就是顺从。”

===Original===
Upon this all further thought of resistance left me, and I stood in silence, stolidly waiting for their action. As I looked around I noticed a movement near the *jantannin*, and saw several athalebs there, which were devouring its flesh. I now went over to Almah and spoke with her. We were both full of despair. It seemed as though we might never meet again. We were to be separated now; but who could say whether we should be permitted to see each other after leaving this place? We had but little to say. I held her in my arms, regardless of the presence of others; and these, seeing our emotion, at once moved away, with the usual delicacy of the Kosekin, and followed Layelah to the *jantannin* to see about the athalebs.

===Chinese===
至此，顽抗的念头尽数离我而去，我默默立着，木然听凭他们发落。环顾之际，我瞥见詹坦宁旁边有些动静，原来那里还伏着几头阿塔莱布，正在啃食它的血肉。我走到阿尔玛身边，同她说话。我们两人都满怀绝望。这一别，怕是再无相见之期了。眼下就要分开了；可离开此地之后，还许不许我们相见，又有谁说得准呢？我们彼此几乎无话可说。我不顾旁人在场，把她搂在怀里；那些人见了我们的情状，便都循着科塞金人惯有的体贴退开了，随莱拉到詹坦宁那边照看阿塔莱布去了。

===Original===
At last our interview was terminated. Layelah came and informed us that all was ready for our departure. We walked sadly to the place, and found the athalebs crouched to receive their riders. There were four beside ours. Layelah informed me that I was to go with her, and Almah was to go on another athaleb. I entreated her to let Almah go with me; but she declined, saying that our athaleb could only carry two, as he seemed fatigued, and it would not be safe to overload him for so long a flight. I told her that Almah and I could go together on the same athaleb; but she objected on the ground of my ignorance of driving. And so, remonstrances and objections being alike useless, I was compelled to yield to the arrangements that had been made. Almah mounted on another athaleb. I mounted with Layelah, and then the great monsters expanded their mighty wings, rose into the air, and soon were speeding over the waters.

===Chinese===
我们的诀别终于到了尽头。莱拉过来告知，启程的一切都已齐备。我们黯然走到那里，只见那些阿塔莱布蹲伏停当，只等骑手上去。除我们原来那头之外，还有四头。莱拉告诉我，我须与她同行，阿尔玛则另乘一头。我恳求她让阿尔玛与我同乘；她却不允，说我们这头只能载两人，它看来已经疲乏，长途飞行再超载便不安全了。我说我与阿尔玛可以共乘一头；她又以我不谙驾驭为辞驳回。于是，恳求也罢，抗议也罢，一概无用，我只好听凭他们既定的安排。阿尔玛上了另一头阿塔莱布，我随莱拉上了我们那头；随后，这些巨怪张开浩大的双翼，腾入空中，不多时便掠过海面疾飞而去了。

===Original===
We went on in silence for some time. I was too despondent to say a word, and all my thoughts turned toward Almah, who was now separated from me﻿—perhaps forever. The other athalebs went ahead, at long intervals apart, flying in a straight line, while ours was last. Layelah said nothing. She sat in front of me; her back was turned toward me; she held in her hands the reins, which hung quite loose at first, but after a while she drew them up, and seemed to be directing our course. For some time I did not notice anything in particular, for my eyes were fixed upon the athaleb immediately before us, upon which was seated the loved form of Almah, which I could easily recognize. But our athaleb flew slowly, and I noticed that we were falling behind. I said this to Layelah, but she only remarked that it was fatigued with its long journey. To this I objected that the others had made as long a journey, and insisted that she should draw nearer. This she at first refused to do; but at length, as I grew persistent, she complied, or pretended to do so. In spite of this, however, we again fell behind, and I noticed that this always happened when the reins were drawn tight. On making this discovery I suddenly seized both reins and let them trail loose, whereupon the athaleb at once showed a perceptible increase of speed, which proved that there was no fatigue in him whatever. This I said to Layelah.

===Chinese===
我们默默飞了一程。我沮丧得一句话也说不出，满心惦念的只有阿尔玛——她如今已与我分离，也许此生不复相见。其余的阿塔莱布在前，彼此隔着老远，排成一线直飞，我们这头坠在最后。莱拉一言不发。她坐在我前面，背对着我，手里握着缰绳；起初缰绳松松垂着，过了一阵，她把它们收紧了，似乎在拨转我们的航向。有一阵子我什么也没留意，因为我的眼睛只顾盯着紧前方的那头阿塔莱布——阿尔玛心爱的身影就骑在上面，我一眼便认得出来。但我们这头飞得慢，我发觉我们渐渐落在了后面。我把这话告诉莱拉，她只说它长途跋涉，乏了。我说别的阿塔莱布飞的路程一样长，执意要她赶上前去。她起初不肯；后来拗不过我一再坚持，便照办了，或者是装作照办了。尽管如此，我们还是又落了后，而且我发觉，每逢缰绳收紧，这情形便要出现。一经发现，我突然一把夺过两根缰绳，任它们松松垂着；那阿塔莱布顿时明显加快了速度——足见它身上哪有半点疲乏。我把这话对莱拉说了。

===Original===
She acquiesced with a sweet smile, and taking the reins again, she sat around so as to face me, and said:

“You are very quick. It is no use to try to deceive you, Atam-or: I wish to fall behind.”

“Why?”

“To save you.”

“To save me?”

“Yes. I can take you to the land of the Orin. Now is the time to escape from death. If you go back you must surely die; but now, if you will be guided by me, I can take you to the land of the Orin. There they all hate death, they love life, they live in the light. There you will find those who are like yourself; there you can love and be happy.”

===Chinese===
她带着甜甜的微笑默认了，重新接过缰绳，转过身来面对着我，说道：

“你真机灵。想瞒你是瞒不住了，阿塔莫尔：我是故意要落后的。”

“为什么？”

“为救你。”

“救我？”

“是的。我能带你到奥林人的国度去。眼下正是逃出死路的时机。你若回去，必死无疑；可现在，你只要听我引导，我便能把你带到奥林人的国度。那里的人个个厌弃死亡，热爱生命，生活在光明之中。在那里你会遇到与你同族同类的人；在那里你可以去爱，可以得到幸福。”

===Original===
“But what of Almah?” I asked.

Layelah made a pretty gesture of despair.

“You are always talking of Almah,” said she. “What is Almah to you? She is cold, dull, sad! She never will speak. Let her go.”

“Never!” said I. “Almah is worth more than all the world to me.”

Layelah sighed.

“I can never, never, never,” said she, “get from you the least little bit of a kind word﻿—even after all that I have done for you, and when you know that I would lie down and let you trample me under your feet if it gave you any pleasure.”

===Chinese===
“那阿尔玛怎么办？”我问。

莱拉作了一个楚楚动人的绝望手势。

“你开口闭口总是阿尔玛，”她说，“阿尔玛于你算得了什么？她冷冰冰、闷沉沉、愁眉苦脸！她连话都不肯多说一句。随她去吧。”

“绝不！”我说，“阿尔玛在我心里，比整个世界还贵重。”

莱拉叹了口气。

“我从你这里，永远、永远、永远，”她说，“得不到半句温存的话——哪怕我为你做了这一切，哪怕你明知道，只要能让你高兴，我情愿躺下来，让你从我身上踩过去。”

===Original===
“Oh, that is not the question at all,” said I. “You are asking me to leave Almah﻿—to be false to her﻿—and I cannot.”

“Among the Kosekin,” said Layelah, “it is the highest happiness for lovers to give one another up.”

“I am not one of the Kosekin,” said I. “I cannot let her go away﻿—I cannot let her go back to the *amir*﻿—to meet death alone. If she dies she shall see me by her side, ready to die with her.”

At this Layelah laughed merrily.

“Is it possible,” said she, “that you believe that? Do you not know that if Almah goes back alone she will not die?”

“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“唉，问题根本不在这里，”我说，“你要我离开阿尔玛——要我对她负心——这我做不到。”

“在科塞金人中间，”莱拉道，“情人们彼此相让，才是至高的幸福。”

“我不是科塞金人，”我说，“我不能让她走——不能让她独自回阿米尔去——独自赴死。她若是死，也必看见我在她身边，陪她一同去死。”

听了这话，莱拉快活地笑起来。

“难道说，”她道，“你真信这个？你不知道么，阿尔玛若是独自回去，她反倒死不成？”

“你这话是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Why, she can only die when you are in her company. She has lived for years among us, and we have waited for someone to appear whom she might love, so that we might give them both the blessing of death. If that one should leave her, Almah could not receive the blessing. She would be compelled to live longer, until some other lover should appear. Now, by going with me to the land of the Orin, you will save Almah’s life﻿—and as for Almah, why, she will be happy﻿—and dear papa is quite willing to marry her. You must see, therefore, dear Atam-or, that my plan is the very best that can be thought of for all of us, and above all for Almah.”

===Chinese===
“这还不明白么？她只有同你在一起，才死得成。她在我们中间住了这许多年，我们一直在等一个她能爱上的人出现，好把死亡的福分一并赐给你们两个。那人若是离了她，阿尔玛便领受不着这份福分了。她就不得不继续活下去，直等到另一个情人出现。所以，你随我到奥林人的国度去，便是救了阿尔玛的性命——至于阿尔玛么，她呀，她会快活的——亲爱的爸爸还满心乐意娶她呢。所以你总该看出来了，亲爱的阿塔莫尔，我这个打算，对我们大家、尤其是对阿尔玛，实在是再好不过的了。”

===Original===
This, however, was intolerable; and I could not consent to desert Almah, even if by doing so I should save her life. My own nature revolted from it. Still it was not a thing which I could dismiss on the instant. The safety of Almah’s life, indeed, required consideration; but then the thought came of her wonder at my desertion. Would she not think me false? Would not the thought of my falsity be worse than death?

“No,” said I, “I will not leave her﻿—not even to save her life. Even among us there are things worse than death. Almah would rather die by the sacrificial knife than linger on with a broken heart.”

“Oh no,” said Layelah, sweetly; “she will rejoice that you are safe. Do you not see that while you are together death is inevitable, but if you separate you may both live and be happy?”

“But she will think me dead,” said I, as a new idea occurred. “She will think that some accident has befallen me.”

“Oh no, she won’t,” said Layelah; “she will think that you have gone off with me.”

“Then that will be worse, and I would rather die, and have her die with me, than live and have her think me false.”

“You are very, very obstinate,” said Layelah, sweetly.

===Chinese===
然而，这实在是忍无可忍；纵然抛下阿尔玛便能救她的性命，我也不能应允背弃她。我的天性对此深恶痛绝。但这又不是可以转念撇开的事。阿尔玛的性命安危，确实要掂量；可继而又想到，她见我弃她而去，会作何感想。她岂不会当我负心？念及我的负心，岂不比死还难受？

“不行，”我说，“我不离开她——哪怕能救她的命也不离开。即便在我们那里，也有比死更可怕的事。阿尔玛宁可死在献祭刀下，也不愿抱着破碎的心苟延残喘。”

“哦，不会的，”莱拉甜甜地说，“她见你安然脱险，只会欢喜。你不明白么？你们在一起，死亡便无可逃避；你们一旦分开，倒可以双双活命，各自幸福。”

“可她会当我死了，”我说，这时心头生出一个新的念头，“她会当我遭了什么不测。”

“哦，不会的，”莱拉道，“她只会当你是随我跑掉了。”

“那可就更糟了。我宁可死，宁可她同我一道死，也不愿独自活着，让她当我是个负心人。”

“你呀，真是固执得不能再固执了。”莱拉甜甜地说。

===Original===
I made no reply. During this conversation I had been too intent upon Layelah’s words to notice the athalebs before me; but now as I looked up I saw that we had fallen far behind, and that Layelah had headed our athaleb in a new direction. Upon this I once more snatched the reins from her, and tried to return to our former course. This, however, I was utterly unable to do.

Layelah laughed.

“You will have to let me guide our course,” said she. “You can do nothing. The athaleb will now go in a straight line to the land of the Orin.”

===Chinese===
我没有作声。方才那番对答，我全神贯注在莱拉的话上，无暇顾及前面的阿塔莱布；此刻抬头一看，我们已远远落在后头，而莱拉早把我们这头拨上了一条新的航路。我登时又从她手里夺过缰绳，想折回原先的方向。可是我全然办不到。

莱拉笑了。

“你只好让我来掌管航向了，”她说，“你什么也做不成。这头阿塔莱布如今要笔直飞往奥林人的国度了。”

===Original===
Upon this I started up in wild excitement.

“Never, never, never!” I cried, in a fury. “I will not; I will destroy this athaleb and perish in the water!”

As I said this I raised my rifle.

“What are you going to do?” cried Layelah, in accents of fear.

“Turn back,” I cried, “or I will kill this athaleb!”

Upon this Layelah dropped the reins, stood up, and looked at me with a smile.

===Chinese===
一听这话，我狂乱地跳起身来。

“绝不，绝不，绝不！”我狂怒地喊道，“我不去；我要杀了这头阿塔莱布，一同葬身大海！”

说话间，我端起了步枪。

“你要干什么？”莱拉喊道，声音里透着恐惧。

“掉头回去，”我喊道，“不然我就杀了这头阿塔莱布！”

莱拉闻言丢开缰绳，站起身来，含笑望着我。

===Original===
“Oh, Atam-or,” said she, “what a thing to ask! How can I go back now, when we have started for the land of the Orin?”

“We shall never reach the land of the Orin,” I cried; “we shall perish in the sea!”

“Oh no,” said Layelah; “you cannot kill the athaleb. You are no more than an insect; your rod is a weak thing, and will break on his iron frame.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，阿塔莫尔，”她说，“你这要求多么为难人！我们已经朝奥林人的国度起飞了，如今教我怎么回头？”

“我们永远到不了什么奥林人的国度，”我喊道，“我们要葬身大海了！”

“哦，不会的，”莱拉道，“你杀不了阿塔莱布。你不过是一只小虫子；你那根杖子软弱无力，砸在他铁打的骨架上，只会自己折断。”

===Original===
It was evident that Layelah had not the slightest idea of the powers of my rifle. There was no hesitation on my part. I took aim with the rifle. At that moment I was desperate. I thought of nothing but the swift flight of the athaleb, which was bearing me away forever from Almah. I could not endure that thought, and still less could I endure the thought that she should believe me false. It was therefore in a wild passion of rage and despair that I levelled my rifle, taking aim as well as I could at what seemed a vital part under the wing. The motion of the wing rendered this difficult, however, and I hesitated a moment, so as to make sure. All this time Layelah stood looking at me with a smile on her rosy lips and a merry twinkle in her eyes﻿—evidently regarding my words as empty threats and my act as a vain pretence, and utterly unprepared for what was to follow.

===Chinese===
莱拉对我这支步枪的威力，显然连一丁点概念也没有。我却再无半分迟疑。我举枪瞄准。那一刻我已横下了心。心里只容得下一个念头：阿塔莱布正疾飞着，把我从阿尔玛身边永远带走。这个念头我受不住，而一想到她还要当我负心，就更受不住。于是我怀着狂怒与绝望的激情，端平了步枪，尽力瞄准翅膀底下那处像是致命的地方。只是翅膀不住扇动，瞄准颇难，我迟疑了片刻，务求一击必中。这期间，莱拉始终站在那里望着我，玫瑰色的唇边带笑，眼里闪着顽皮的光——显然把我这话当作空口恫吓，把我这举动当作虚张声势，对接下来要发生的事，全然没有防备。

===Original===
Suddenly I fired both barrels in quick succession. The reports rang out in thunder over the sea. The athaleb gave a wild, appalling shriek, and fell straight down into the water, fluttering vainly with one wing, while the other hung down useless. A shriek of horror burst from Layelah. She started back, and fell from her standing-place into the waves beneath. The next instant we were all in the water together﻿—the athaleb writhing and lashing the water into foam, while I involuntarily clung to his coarse mane, and expected death every moment.

===Chinese===
蓦地，我双筒连发，一枪紧接着一枪。枪声如雷，在海面上空回荡。那阿塔莱布发出一声凄厉骇人的尖唳，径直栽进水里，一只翅膀徒劳地扑腾，另一只垂着，全然废了。莱拉迸出一声恐怖的尖叫，向后一仰，从她立脚的地方跌进下面的波涛里。转眼间，我们全都落到了水里——阿塔莱布翻腾扭动，把海水搅成一片白沫，我则不由自主地死死抓住它粗糙的鬃毛，时时刻刻都以为死期到了。

===Original===
But death did not come; for the athaleb did not sink, but floated with his back out of the water, the right pinion being sunk underneath and useless, and the left struggling vainly with the sea. But after a time he folded up the left wing and drew it close in to his side, and propelled himself with his long hind-legs. His right wing was broken, but he did not seem to have suffered any other injury.

Suddenly I heard a cry behind me:

“Atam-or! oh, Atam-or!”

===Chinese===
但死亡并没有来；因为阿塔莱布并没有下沉，而是背脊露出水面浮着：右翼压在身下，已然无用，左翼还在徒劳地同大海挣扎。过了一阵，它把左翼收拢，紧贴在身边，用长长的后腿划水前行。它的右翼断了，除此之外，似乎并没有受别的伤。

忽然，我听见身后一声呼喊：

“阿塔莫尔！啊，阿塔莫尔！”

===Original===
I looked around and saw Layelah. She was swimming in the water, and seemed exhausted. In the agitation of the past few moments I had lost sight of her, and had thought that she was drowned; but now the sight of her roused me from my stupor and brought me back to myself. She was swimming, yet her strokes were weak and her face was full of despair. In an instant I had flung off my coat, rolled up the rifle and pistol in its folds, and sprung into the water. A few strokes brought me to Layelah. A moment more and I should have been too late. I held her head out of water, told her not to struggle, and then struck out to go back. It would have been impossible for me to do this, encumbered with such a load, had I not fortunately perceived the floating wing of the athaleb close beside me. This I seized, and by means of it drew myself with Layelah alongside; after which I succeeded in putting her on the back of the animal, and soon followed myself.

===Chinese===
我回头一看，是莱拉。她正在水里游着，看样子已筋疲力尽。方才那阵慌乱之中，我把她丢了，只当她已经溺死；此刻一见她，我便从怔忡中惊醒，回过神来。她还在游，可划水已软弱无力，满脸绝望。一转眼间，我甩脱外衣，把步枪和手枪卷在衣里，纵身跳入水中。几把水便游到了莱拉身边。再迟一刻，就来不及了。我托住她的头露出水面，叫她不要挣扎，然后转身往回游。带着这样一副重负，这本是办不到的事，幸而我一眼瞧见身边漂浮着阿塔莱布的那只翅膀。我一把抓住，借着它，拖着莱拉一路靠了过去；随后把她弄上那巨兽的背，我自己也很快跟着上去了。

===Original===
The terror of the rifle had overwhelmed her, and the suddenness of the catastrophe had almost killed her. She had struggled in the water for a long time, and had called to me in vain. Now she was quite exhausted, and lay in my arms trembling and sobbing. I spoke to her encouragingly, and wrapped her in my coat, and rubbed her hands and feet, until at last she began to recover. Then she wept quietly for a long time; then the weeping fit passed away. She looked up with a smile, and in her face there was unutterable gratitude.

“Atam-or,” said she, “I never loved death like the rest of the Kosekin; but now﻿—but now﻿—I feel that death with you would be sweet.”

Then tears came to her eyes, and I found tears coming to my own, so that I had to stoop down and kiss away the tears of Layelah. As I did so she twined both her arms around my neck, held me close to her, and sighed.

“Oh, Atam-or, death with you is sweet! And now you cannot reproach me﻿—You have done this yourself, with your terrible power; and you have saved my life to let me die with you. You do not hate me, then, Atam-or, do you? Just speak once to a poor little girl, and say that you do not hate her!”

===Chinese===
步枪的恐怖把她吓破了胆，这场大祸又来得如此骤然，几乎要了她的命。她在水里挣扎了许久，呼喊我也无回应。这时她已气力全无，躺在我怀里瑟瑟发抖，呜呜咽咽。我温言宽慰她，拿外衣把她裹住，揉搓她的手脚，直到她渐渐缓过来。然后她低声哭了很久；哭劲过去了，她抬眼一笑，脸上写满了难以言宣的感激。

“阿塔莫尔，”她说，“我从来不曾像其余科塞金人那样爱慕死亡；可是现在——可是现在——我觉得，同你一道死，会是甜蜜的。”

说着她眼中涌出泪来，我发觉自己的眼眶也湿了，只得俯下身去，吻去莱拉的泪。我俯身之际，她双臂缠住我的脖颈，把我紧紧搂向她，轻轻叹了一口气。

“啊，阿塔莫尔，同你一道死是甜蜜的！这下你可再不能怪我了——这一切是你自己亲手做下的，用着你那可怕的威力；你救了我的性命，正是要让我同你一道死。这么说，你并不恨我，是么，阿塔莫尔？只求你对一个可怜的小姑娘开一次口，说你并不恨她！”

===Original===
All this was very pitiable. What man that had a heart in his breast could listen unmoved to words like these, or look without emotion upon one so beautiful, so gentle, and so tender? It was no longer Layelah in triumph with whom I had to do, but Layelah in distress: the light banter, the teasing, mocking smile, the kindling eye, the ready laugh﻿—all were gone. There was nothing now but mournful tenderness﻿—the timid appeal of one who dreaded a repulse, the glance of deep affection, the abandonment of love.

I held Layelah in my arms, and I thought of nothing now but words of consolation for her. Life seemed over; death seemed inevitable; and there, on the back of the athaleb, we floated on the waters and waited for our doom.

===Chinese===
这一切实在可怜。凡胸中还有一颗心的男人，听了这样的话，谁能无动于衷？见了这样美丽、这样温婉、这样缠绵的人，谁能不生怜惜？此刻同我相对的，已不是那得胜扬扬的莱拉，而是落难伤心的莱拉：轻巧的调侃、逗弄揶揄的笑、发亮的眼睛、动不动的一声轻笑——统统不见了。眼下只有一片哀婉的温柔——那是唯恐遭到拒绝的人怯生生的恳求，是深情的一瞥，是爱意的全然倾付。

我把莱拉搂在怀里，此刻心里只想着安慰她的话。生命似乎已经到了尽头，死亡似乎无可逃免；我们就这样伏在阿塔莱布的背上，随波漂浮，静候厄运降临。

## XXV Falling, Like Icarus, Into the Sea / 二十五、如伊卡洛斯之坠海

===Original===
The aurora light, which had flamed brightly, was now extinct, and darkness was upon the face of the deep, where we floated on the back of the monster. He swam, forcing himself onward with his hind-legs, with one broad wing folded up close. Had both been folded up the athaleb could have swum rapidly; but the broken wing lay expanded over the water, tossing with the waves, so that our progress was but slight. Had it not been for this, the athaleb’s own instinct might have served to guide him toward some shore which we might have hoped to reach before life was extinct; but as it was, all thought of reaching any shore was out of the question, and there arose before us only the prospect of death﻿—a death, too, which must be lingering and painful and cruel. Thus amid the darkness we floated, and the waves dashed around us, and the athaleb never ceased to struggle in the water, trying to force his way onward. It seemed sweet at that moment to have Layelah with me, for what could have been more horrible than loneliness amid those black waters? and Layelah’s mind was made up to meet death with joy, so that her mood conveyed itself to me. And I thought that since death was inevitable it were better to meet it thus, and in this way end my life﻿—not amid the horrors of the sacrifice and the Mista Kosek, but in a way which seemed natural to a seafaring man like myself, and with which I had long familiarized my thoughts. For I had fallen upon a world and among people which were all alien and unintelligible to me; and to live on would only open the way to new and worse calamities. There was peace also in the thought that my death would snatch the prospect of death from Almah. She would now be safe. It was only when we were together as lovers that death threatened her; but now since I was removed she could resume her former life, and she might remember me only as an episode in that life. That she would remember me I felt sure, and that she would weep for me and mourn after me was undeniable; but time as it passed would surely alleviate that grief, and Almah would live and be happy. Perhaps she might yet regain her native land and rejoin her loved kindred, whom she would tell of the stranger from an unknown shore who had loved her, and through whose death she had gained her life. Such were the thoughts that filled my mind as I floated over the black water with darkness all around, as I held Layelah in my arms, with my coat wrapped around her, and murmured in her ear tender words of consolation and sympathy.

===Chinese===
先前燃得一片明亮的极光此刻已然熄灭，渊面一片黑暗，我们便浮在巨怪的背上随波漂流。它游着水，靠两条后腿奋力向前划进，一侧的大翼紧紧收拢。倘若双翼尽收，这头阿塔莱布原可游得飞快；怎奈那只折断的翅膀摊在水面上，随浪颠簸翻腾，我们便行进得极缓。若不是这重拖累，阿塔莱布出于本能，也许能引着我们游向某处海岸，赶在性命熄灭之前登岸，也在人意之中；可是事到如今，一切登岸之念全然无从谈起，眼前升起的唯有死亡的前景——而且那死法，还必是迁延的、痛苦的、残忍的。我们就这样浮在黑暗之中，波涛在四周激荡，阿塔莱布在水中挣扎不休，只顾奋力向前。此刻有莱拉在身旁，竟令人觉出一种甜意；在那黑水之间，还有什么比孤独更可怖的呢？何况莱拉已打定主意，要欢欢喜喜迎向死亡，她那份心境也传给了我。我又想，既然死亡无可逃免，倒不如就这样迎上前去，就这样了却此生——不在献祭与米斯塔·科塞克的种种可怖之中，而以一种在像我这样的海员看来颇为自然的方式，一种我早已在思想上处惯了的方式。因为我沦落的这个世界、混迹的这族人民，于我全然隔膜、全然不可索解；活下去，也不过是给新的、更惨的灾祸开门铺路罢了。还有一层安宁：我一死，便把死亡的前景从阿尔玛手里夺去了。她从此可以安然无恙。唯有当我们以情侣之身两相依偎时，死亡才威胁到她；如今我这一去，她便可重回旧日的生活，而我，至多不过是那段生活里的一段插曲。她会记得我，这一点我深信不疑；她会为我流泪、为我哀恸，这也无可否认；然而时光流转，总归会将那份悲伤渐渐抚平，阿尔玛会活下去，还会活得快活。说不定她终究能重返故土，与亲爱的骨肉重聚，对他们讲起那个来自无名海岸的异乡人——他爱过她，又以自己的死，换来了她的生。我便是怀着这一腔心事，浮在四围漆黑的茫茫水面上；我把莱拉搂在怀中，外衣裹在她身上，在她耳畔低声吐着温存的安慰与怜惜的话。

===Original===
A long, long time had passed﻿—but how long I know not﻿—when suddenly Layelah gave a cry, and started up on her knees, with her head bent forward listening intently. I too listened, and I could distinctly hear the sound of breakers.

It was evident that we were approaching some shore; and, from what I remembered of the shore of Magones, such a shore meant death, and death alone. We stood up and tried to peer through the gloom. At length we saw a whole line of breakers, and beyond all was black. We waited anxiously in that position, and drew steadily nearer. It was evident that the athaleb was desirous of reaching that shore, and we could do nothing but await the result.

But the athaleb had his wits about him, and swam along on a line with the breakers for some distance, until at length an opening appeared, into which he directed his course. Passing through this we reached still water, which seemed like a lagoon surrounded by a coral reef. The athaleb swam on farther, and at length we saw before us an island with a broad, sandy beach, beyond which was the shadowy outline of a forest. Here the monster landed, and dragged himself wearily upon the sand, where he spread his vast bulk out, and lay panting heavily. We dismounted﻿—I first, so as to assist Layelah; and then it seemed as if death were postponed for a time, since we had reached this place where the rich and rank vegetation spoke of nothing but vigorous life.

===Chinese===
过了许久许久——究竟多久，我无从知晓——莱拉忽然叫了一声，一跃跪起，俯着身子凝神谛听。我也侧耳去听，清清楚楚听见了碎浪之声。

看来我们正在向某处海岸靠近；而凭我对马戈内斯海岸的记忆，这样的海岸意味着死亡，且唯有死亡。我们站起身来，竭力想望穿那片幽暗。终于，我们望见一长列碎浪，浪外尽是漆黑。我们焦灼地那样伫立着，一路被稳稳带得越来越近。看得出阿塔莱布一心要奔赴那道海岸，我们别无办法，只有静候结局。

好在阿塔莱布心窍灵明，沿着与碎浪线平行的水路游了一程，终于现出一道豁口，它便拨转方向直入其间。穿过豁口，我们到了一片静水，看去像是一座珊瑚礁环围的潟湖。阿塔莱布再往前游，末了我们望见面前有一座岛，岛前是一片开阔的沙滩，沙滩背后是森林朦胧的轮廓。巨怪便在这里登了岸，拖着一身疲惫爬上沙地，摊开它庞大的躯体，趴伏在那里大口喘息。我们从它背上下来——我先下来，好去搀扶莱拉——这时死亡仿佛被暂且搁下了，因为我们抵达的这地方，那繁茂滋蔓的草木，道出的无一不是旺盛的生机。

===Original===
Fortune had indeed dealt strangely with me. I had fled with Almah, and with her had reached one desolate shore, and now I found myself with Layelah upon another shore, desolate also, but not a savage wilderness. This lonely island, ringed with the black ocean waters, was the abode of a life of its own, and there was nothing here to crush the soul into a horror of despair like that which was caused by the tremendous scenes on Magones.

In an instant Layelah revived from her gloom. She looked around, clapped her little hands, laughed aloud, and danced for joy.

“Oh, Atam-or,” she cried, “see﻿—see the trees, see the grass, the bushes! This is a land of wonder. As for food, you can call it down from the sky with your *sepet-ram*, or we can find it on the rocks. Oh, Atam-or! life is better than death, and we can live here, and we can be happy. This shall be better to us than the lands of the Orin, for we shall be alone, and we shall be all in all to one another.”

===Chinese===
命运待我委实离奇。我偕阿尔玛出逃，同她抵过一片荒凉的海岸；如今却与莱拉一起，置身于另一处海岸——同样荒凉，却并非蛮荒的绝域。这座孤岛四面环着黑沉沉的洋面，自有它一番生机气象；此地再没有什么，能像马戈内斯那些骇人景象一般，把人的灵魂碾成恐怖的绝望。

莱拉霎时间便从愁云惨雾里活了过来。她四下张望，拍着一双小手，放声大笑，欢喜得手舞足蹈。

“哦，阿塔莫尔，”她喊道，“看哪——看这些树，这青草，这些灌木！这是一片奇境。至于吃食，你尽可以用你的塞佩特-拉姆把它从天上召下来，不然我们在礁石上也寻得着。哦，阿塔莫尔！活着终究比死了好，我们可以在这里过活，可以快快活活的。这地方于我们，比奥林人的国度还要好，因为只有你我两个，你我便是彼此的一切。”

===Original===
I could not help laughing, and I said:

“Layelah, this is not the language of the Kosekin. You should at once go to the other side of this island, and sit down and wait for death.”

“Never,” said Layelah; “you are mine, Atam-or, and I never will leave you. If you wish me to die for you, I will gladly lay down my life; but I will not leave you. I love you, Atam-or; and now, whether it be life or death, it is all the same so long as I have you.”

===Chinese===
我不由得笑了，说道：

“莱拉，这可不是科塞金人的言语。你该即刻到岛的那一头去，坐下来等死才是。”

“决不，”莱拉说，“你是我的，阿塔莫尔，我决不离开你。你若要我为你死，我甘愿舍了这条性命；可我不离开你。我爱你，阿塔莫尔；如今是生是死，全都一样，只要我有你。”

===Original===
Our submersion in the sea and our long exposure afterward had chilled both of us, but Layelah felt it most. She was shivering in her wet clothes in spite of my coat which I insisted on her wearing, and I determined, if possible, to kindle a fire. Fortunately my powder was dry, for I had thrown off my flask with my coat before jumping into the sea, and thus I had the means of creating fire. I rubbed wet powder over my handkerchief, and then gathered some dried sticks and moss. After this I found some dead trees, the boughs of which were dry and brittle, and in the exercise I soon grew warm, and had the satisfaction of seeing a great heap of fagots accumulating. I fired my pistol into the handkerchief, which, being saturated with powder, caught the fire, and this I blew into a flame among the dried moss. A bright fire now sprang up and blazed high in the air; while I, in order to have an ample supply of fuel, continued to gather it for a long time. At length, as I came back, I saw Layelah lying on the sand in front of the fire, sound asleep. I was glad of this, for she was weary, and had seemed so weak and tremulous that I had felt anxious; so now I arranged my coat over her carefully, and then sat down for a time to think over this new turn which my fortune had taken.

===Chinese===
我们在海中一浸、随后又长久暴露于风寒之下，两人都冻透了，莱拉尤甚。她穿着一身湿衣，虽披上我硬要她披的外衣，仍瑟瑟发抖，我便打定主意，但有可能，定要生起一堆火。所幸我的火药还是干的，因为跳海之前，我把火药筒连同外衣一并解了下来，于是生火的手段有了。我把湿火药抹在手帕上，随后拾了些干枝和苔藓。这之后我又寻到几株枯树，枝条干脆易折；这般劳作下来，我很快便暖和了，眼见一大堆柴薪渐渐积起，心中颇为受用。我朝手帕开了一枪，手帕浸透了火药，登时燃着，我便将它吹成明火，引进干苔藓中。明亮的火焰腾地蹿起，在半空里熊熊烧着；我为了备下充裕的柴火，又继续拾掇了好一阵。末了我抱着柴回来，只见莱拉躺在火前的沙地上，睡得正酣。我见了欢喜，因为她早已困乏不堪，先前又显得那样虚弱发颤，令人担忧；于是我把外衣细心替她盖好，然后坐下来，将我命运这一番新的转折思量了一番。

===Original===
This island was certainly very unlike Magones, yet I had no surety but that it might be equally destitute of food. This was the first question, and I could not think of sleep until I had found out more about the place. The aurora light, which constantly brightens and lessens in this strange world, was now shining gloriously, and I set forth to explore the island. The beach was of fine sand all the way. The water was smooth, and shut in on every side by an outer reef against which the sea-waves broke incessantly. As I walked I soon perceived what the island was; for I had often seen such places before in the South Pacific. It was, in fact, a coral islet, with a reef of rocks encircling it on every side. The vegetation, however, was unlike anything in the world beyond; for it consisted of many varieties of tree-ferns, that looked like palms, and giant grasses, and bamboo. The island was but small, and the entire circuit was not over a mile. I saw nothing that looked like food, nor did it seem likely that in so small a place there could be enough sustenance for us. Our only hope would be from the sea, yet even here I could see no signs of any sort of shellfish. On the whole the prospect was discouraging, and I returned to the starting-point with a feeling of dejection; but this feeling did not trouble me much at that time: my chief thought was of rest, and I flung myself down on the sand and fell asleep.

===Chinese===
这座岛固然与马戈内斯大不相同，但它是否同样绝无吃食，我却拿不准。这是头一个问题，不把这地方探个明白，我实难想到睡觉。极光在这个奇异世界里明明灭灭、变幻不定，此刻正照得一片辉煌，我便动身去勘察全岛。滩头一路尽是细沙。水面平滑，四面为一道外礁围拢，海浪在那礁上拍击不休。走着走着，我便看出这岛的底细了；南太平洋一带这类地方，我从前是见惯的。它其实是一座珊瑚小岛，四周围着一圈礁石。只是岛上的草木，与外面世界的任何物事都迥然不同：尽是诸般种类的树蕨，望去宛如棕榈，又有巨草和竹子。岛甚小，绕行一周不过一英里光景。我看不到任何像是吃食的东西，这样小的一块地方，似乎也不大可能供养得起我们两个。我们唯一的指望只在海里，可就连海里，我也望不见任何贝类的踪影。总而言之，前景是叫人丧气的，我怀着满心颓唐回到动身之处；不过那种情绪当时并不甚搅扰我：我心里头一件大事是歇息，我便往沙滩上一倒，睡着了。

===Original===
I was awakened by a cry from Layelah. Starting up, I saw her standing and looking into the sky. She was intensely excited. As soon as she saw me she rushed toward me and burst into tears, while I, full of wonder, could only stare upward.

“Oh!” cried Layelah, “they’ve turned back﻿—they’ve found us! We shall have to leave our dear, lovely island. Oh, Atam-or, I shall lose you now; for never, never, never again will you have one thought of love for your poor Layelah!”

With these words she clung sobbing to me. For my part I do not remember what I said to soothe her, for the sight above was so amazing that it took up all my attention. The aurora shone bright, and in the sky I saw two vast objects wheeling and circling, as if about to descend. I recognized them at once as athalebs; but as their backs were hid from view by their immense wings, I could not make out whether they were wanderers about to alight of their own accord, or guided here by riders﻿—perhaps by the Kosekin from whom we had been parted.

===Chinese===
莱拉的一声喊叫把我惊醒。我一跃而起，见她立在那里仰望天空，激动已极。她一见我，便扑奔过来，泪如泉涌；我则满心诧异，只能呆呆望着上空。

“哦！”莱拉喊道，“他们折回来了——他们找着我们了！我们就要离开这亲爱的、可爱的岛了。哦，阿塔莫尔，我这就要失去你了；因为你对你的可怜的莱拉，永远、永远、永远不会再有一丝一毫爱的念头了！”

说着，她便偎在我身上呜呜咽咽哭个不住。至于我，已记不得当时说了些什么话去宽慰她，因为头顶的景象太过惊人，把我整个的心神都占了去。极光明亮，我望见天上有两个庞然巨物盘旋回转，像是要降落。我一眼便认出那是两头阿塔莱布；只是它们的脊背被巨大的翅膀遮蔽不见，我分辨不出它们是自行择地而降的游荡者，还是被骑者驱策至此——兴许正是那班与我们离散了的科塞金人。

===Original===
This much at least I remember. I said to Layelah that these athalebs were wild ones, which had come here because they saw or scented our wounded one; but Layelah shook her head with mournful meaning.

“Oh no,” said she; “Almah has come back for you. This firelight has guided them. If you had not made the fire they never, never, never could have found us; but now all is lost.”

There was no time for conversation or discussion. The athalebs drew swiftly nearer and nearer, descending in long circuits, until at length they touched the ground not far away on the wide sandy beach. Then we saw people on their backs, and among them was Almah. We hurried toward them, and Almah rushed into my arms, to the great disgust of Layelah, for she was close beside me and saw it all. She gave an exclamation of grief and despair, and hurried away.

===Chinese===
至少这一点我记得。我对莱拉说，这几头阿塔莱布是野的，因为望见或嗅着我们这头受伤的，才寻到这里来；莱拉却摇了摇头，那神情凄然，大有深意。

“哦，不，”她说，“是阿尔玛回来寻你了。是这火光引着他们来的。你若不生这火，他们永远、永远、永远也寻不着我们；可如今什么都完了。”

已没有工夫攀谈细论。那两头阿塔莱布盘着长长的圈子急遽下降，越降越近，末了在不远处宽阔的沙滩上落了地。这时我们看见它们背上有人，其中就有阿尔玛。我们急忙迎上前去，阿尔玛一头扑进我的怀抱，惹得莱拉怨愤已极——她就在我身旁，把这一切尽收眼底。她发出一声悲苦绝望的呼喊，匆匆走开了。

===Original===
From Almah I learned that our disappearance had caused alarm; that two of the athalebs had come back in search of us; that they had been to Magones, and had searched over the seas, and were just about giving us up as lost, when the firelight had attracted their attention and drawn them here.

I said nothing at that time about the cause of our disappearance, but merely remarked that the athaleb had fallen into the sea and swam here. This was sufficient. They had to remain here for some time longer to rest their athalebs. At length we prepared to depart. Our wounded athaleb was left behind to take care of himself. I was taken with Almah, and Layelah went on the other. We were thus separated; and so we set forth upon our return, and at length arrived at the *amir*.

===Chinese===
我从阿尔玛口中得知：我们的失踪引起了惊慌；有两头阿塔莱布回头来寻我们；他们到过马戈内斯，把海面搜了个遍，正当他们死心放弃、认我们已葬身大海之际，那火光攫住了他们的注意，将他们引到这里。

当时我对我们失踪的缘由只字未提，只说那阿塔莱布坠了海，一路游到此间。这就尽够了。他们还须在此地再盘桓些时，好让阿塔莱布歇息。末了我们准备启程。我们那头受伤的阿塔莱布被留在身后，由它自己去照管自己。我同阿尔玛同乘一头，莱拉上了另一头。我们就这样分开了；于是我们踏上归程，终于回到了阿米尔。

## XXVI Grimm’s Law Again / 二十六、又是格林律

===Original===
Dinner was now announced, and Oxenden laid the manuscript aside; whereupon they adjourned to the cabin, where they proceeded to discuss both the repast and the manuscript.

“Well,” said Featherstone, “More’s story seems to be approaching a crisis. What do you think of it now, Melick? Do you still think it a sensational novel?”

“Partly so,” said Melick; “but it would be nearer the mark to call it a satirical romance.”

“Why not a scientific romance?”

“Because there’s precious little science in it, but a good deal of quiet satire.”

“Satire on what?” asked Featherstone. “I’ll be hanged if I can see it.”

===Chinese===
这时开饭的通报传来，奥克森登便把手稿搁在一旁；于是众人移步舱厅，把菜肴与手稿一并加以品评。

“那么，”费瑟斯通说，“莫尔的故事看样子是渐近紧要关头了。梅利克，你如今作何感想？还当它是一部轰动小说么？”

“算是，也不尽然，”梅利克道，“不过，叫它讽刺传奇倒更切题些。”

“为何不是科学传奇呢？”

“因为里头的科学少得可怜，不动声色的讽刺倒有的是。”

“讽刺什么？”费瑟斯通问，“我反正是死活瞧不出来。”

===Original===
“Oh, well,” said Melick, “on things in general. The satire is directed against the restlessness of humanity; its impulses, feelings, hopes, and fears﻿—all that men do and feel and suffer. It mocks us by exhibiting a new race of men, animated by passions and impulses which are directly the opposite of ours, and yet no nearer happiness than we are. It shows us a world where our evil is made a good, and our good an evil; there all that we consider a blessing is had in abundance﻿—prolonged and perpetual sunlight, riches, power, fame﻿—and yet these things are despised, and the people, turning away from them, imagine that they can find happiness in poverty, darkness, death, and unrequited love. The writer thus mocks at all our dearest passions and strongest desires; and his general aim is to show that the mere search for happiness per se is a vulgar thing, and must always result in utter nothingness. The writer also teaches the great lesson that the happiness of man consists not in external surroundings, but in the internal feelings, and that heaven itself is not a place, but a state. It is the old lesson which Milton extorted from Satan:

> “ ‘What matter where, if I be still the same﻿—’

“Or again:

> “ ‘The mind is its own place, and of itself
 

 Can make a heaven of hell, a hell of heaven﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
“哦，好吧，”梅利克说，“讽刺的是世间万般事物。这讽刺直指人类的躁动不息——他们的冲动、情感、希望与恐惧，人们的一切作为、感受与苦难。它向我们展示一族新的人类，其激情冲动与我们的恰恰相反，却也不见得比我们更接近幸福，以此嘲弄我们。它让我们看到一个世界：在那里，我们的恶成了善，我们的善成了恶；凡我们奉为福祉的东西，那里都充盈丰足——绵长而永驻不逝的阳光，财富、权力、声名——然而这些东西尽遭鄙弃，那里的人背转过身去，自以为能在贫穷、黑暗、死亡与单相思之中觅得幸福。作者就这样把我们最珍贵的激情、最强烈的欲望嘲弄了个遍；他的总旨，是要表明单单追求幸福本身乃是一桩俗事，到头来必定落得一场空。作者还教给我们一个大教训：人的幸福不在于外在的境遇，而在于内心的感受；天堂本身也并非一个处所，而是一种心境。这正是当年弥尔顿从撒但口中逼出来的旧训：

> “‘我若依然故我，身在何处又有何妨——’”

“又如：

> “‘心灵自有其境地，全凭它自身
 

 便能化地狱为天堂，化天堂为地狱——’”

===Original===
“That’s good too,” cried Oxenden. “That reminds me of the German commentators who find in the *Agamemnon* of Aeschylus or the *Oedipus* of Sophocles or the *Hamlet* of Shakespeare motives and purposes of which the authors could never have dreamed, and give us a metaphysical, beer-and-tobacco, High-Dutch Clytemnestra or Antigone or Lady Macbeth. No, my boy, More was a simple sailor, and had no idea of satirizing anything.”

“How, then, do you account for the perpetual undercurrent of meaning and innuendo that may be found in every line?”

“I deny that there is anything of the sort,” said Oxenden. “It is a plain narrative of facts; but the facts are themselves such that they give a new coloring to the facts of our own life. They are in such profound antithesis to European ways that we consider them as being written merely to indicate that difference. It is like the *Germania* of Tacitus, which many critics still hold to be a satire on Roman ways, while as a matter of fact it is simply a narrative of German manners and customs.”

“I hope,” cried Melick, “that you do not mean to compare this awful rot and rubbish to the *Germania* of Tacitus?”

“By no means,” said Oxenden; “I merely asserted that in one respect they were analogous. You forced on the allusion to the *Germania* by calling this ‘rot and rubbish’ a satirical romance.”

===Chinese===
“这话说得倒也漂亮，”奥克森登嚷道，“这教我想起那些德国注释家来。他们在埃斯库罗斯的《阿伽门农》、索福克勒斯的《俄狄浦斯》或者莎士比亚的《哈姆雷特》里，挖掘出种种作者本人做梦也想不到的命意与宗旨，于是端给我们一个形而上学气、啤酒加烟草味的高地德语版的克吕泰涅斯特拉、安提戈涅或麦克白夫人。不，老兄，莫尔不过是个朴实的水手，压根没想到要讽刺什么。”

“那么，字里行间处处寻得见的那股绵延不断的言外之意与影射讥刺，你又作何解释？”

“我根本否认有那一类名堂，”奥克森登说，“这是一篇平铺直叙的纪事；只是这些事实本身，便给我们自己生活里的事实染上了一层新的色彩。它们与欧陆的风习背反得如此深切，以致我们只当它们是特意写来点明这种差异的。这就像塔西佗的《日耳曼尼亚志》，至今仍有许多批评家认定它是对罗马风俗的讽刺，而实则它不过是记叙日耳曼人的风气习俗罢了。”

“我倒要希望，”梅利克嚷道，“你总不是存心拿这堆可怕的烂糟废话，去比塔西佗的《日耳曼尼亚志》吧？”

“绝无此意，”奥克森登道，“我只是断言两者在某一点上相似。是你先管这‘烂糟废话’叫讽刺传奇，才逼得我抬出《日耳曼尼亚志》来。”

===Original===
“Oh, well,” said Melick, “I only referred to the intention of the writer. His plan is one thing and his execution quite another. His plan is not bad, but he fails utterly in his execution. The style is detestable. If he had written in the style of a plain seaman, and told a simple unvarnished tale, it would have been all right. In order to carry out properly such a plan as this the writer should take Defoe as his model, or, still better, Dean Swift. *Gulliver’s Travels* and *Robinson Crusoe* show what can be done in this way, and form a standard by which all other attempts must be judged. But this writer is tawdry; he has the worst vices of the sensational school﻿—he shows everywhere marks of haste, gross carelessness, and universal feebleness. When he gets hold of a good fancy, he lacks the patience that is necessary in order to work it up in an effective way. He is a gross plagiarist, and over and over again violates in the most glaring manner all the ordinary proprieties of style. What can be more absurd, for instance, than the language which he puts into the mouth of Layelah? Not content with making her talk like a sentimental boarding-school, bread-and-butter English miss, he actually forgets himself so far as to put in her mouth a threadbare joke, which everyone has heard since childhood.”

“What is that?”

“Oh, that silly speech about the athaleb swallowing its victuals whole.”

===Chinese===
“哦，好吧，”梅利克说，“我说的只是作者的用意。他的构思是一回事，落笔成文又是另一回事。构思不算坏，可一到笔头便一败涂地。文笔糟不可言。他若用朴实水手的笔调来写，讲一个不加雕饰的简单故事，那倒也罢了。要把这样一个构思做得妥帖，作者当以笛福为楷模，或者更上一层，以斯威夫特教长为楷模。《格列佛游记》与《鲁滨逊漂流记》昭示了这条路子所能抵达的境地，也立下了评判一切后来尝试的标尺。可是这一位俗艳得很，把轰动小说派的种种恶习集于一身——处处露出仓促的痕迹、粗疏的大意和通体的孱弱。他偶有一二妙想到手，又缺少把妙想经营见效所必需的耐心。他还是个恬不知耻的剽窃老手，一而再、再而三地以最刺眼的方式践踏一切行文的常法。譬如，还有比他塞进莱拉口中的言语更荒唐的么？先叫她满口多愁善感的寄宿学校腔、一副吃黄油面包长大的英国小妞儿做派犹嫌不足，居然忘形到往她嘴里塞了一个磨穿了嘴皮的旧笑话——人人自打童年便听熟了的那一个。”

“是哪一个？”

“哦，就是阿塔莱布把吃食囫囵个吞下去的那句蠢话呗。”

===Original===
“What’s the matter with that?” asked Oxenden. “It’s merely a chance resemblance. In translating her words into English they fell by accident into that shape. No one but you would find fault with them. Would it have been better if he had translated her words into the scientific phraseology which the doctor made use of with regard to the Ichthyosaurus? He might have made it this way: ‘Does it bite?’ ‘No; it swallows its food without mastication.’ Would that have been better? Besides, it’s all very well to talk of imitating Defoe and Swift; but suppose he couldn’t do it?”

“Then he shouldn’t have written the book.”

“In that case how could his father have heard about his adventures?”

“His father!” exclaimed Melick. “Do you mean to say that you still accept all this as bona fide?”

“Do you mean to say,” retorted Oxenden, “that you still have any doubt about the authenticity of this remarkable manuscript?”

At this each looked at the other; Melick elevated his eyebrows, and Oxenden shrugged his shoulders, but each seemed unable to find words to express his amazement at the other’s stupidity, and so they took refuge in silence.

===Chinese===
“那有什么不妥？”奥克森登问，“不过是碰巧相像罢了。把她的原话译成英文的时候，无巧不巧落进了那个模子。除了你，谁也不会去挑这种错。难道他把她的译成医生谈论鱼龙时用的那套科学术语，就更好了？他大可以这么写：‘它咬人么？’‘不；它吞食食物，不加咀嚼。’那样就更好么？再说，侈谈模仿笛福与斯威夫特固然轻巧，可假使他学不来呢？”

“那他就不该写这部书。”

“那样的话，他父亲又怎能听闻他的这些奇遇呢？”

“他父亲！”梅利克失声喊道，“难道你是说，你至今仍把这一切当作千真万确的实录？”

“难道你是说，”奥克森登反唇相讥，“你至今还对这部非同寻常的手稿的真伪心存半点怀疑么？”

这一来两人面面相觑：梅利克扬起了眉毛，奥克森登耸了耸肩膀；可两人似乎都寻不出话来，表达各自对对方愚不可及的惊诧，于是只好躲进沉默里去。

===Original===
“What do you understand by this athaleb, doctor?” asked Featherstone.

“The athaleb?” said the doctor. “Why, it is clearly the Pterodactyl.”

“By the by,” interrupted Oxenden, “do please take notice of that name. It affords another exemplification of ‘Grimm’s Law.’ The Hebrew word is *ataleph*, and means bat. The Kosekin word is *athaleb*. Here you see the thin letter of Hebrew represented by the aspirated letter of the Kosekin language, while the aspirated Hebrew is represented by the Kosekin medial.”

“Too true,” exclaimed Melick, in a tone of deep conviction; “and now, Oxenden, won’t you sing us a song?”

“Nonsense,” said Featherstone; “let the doctor tell us about the athaleb.”

===Chinese===
“医生，你对这阿塔莱布作何解？”费瑟斯通问。

“阿塔莱布？”医生道，“嗐，这分明就是翼手龙（Pterodactyl）嘛。”

“顺便说一句，”奥克森登插言道，“请务必留意这个名字。它又给‘格林律’添了一条例证。希伯来语的原词是阿塔莱夫（ataleph），义为蝙蝠；科塞金语的词是阿塔莱布（athaleb）。你们看，希伯来语的清音字母，在这里由科塞金语的送气音字母来充任；而希伯来语的送气音字母，则由科塞金语的浊音字母来充任。”

“千真万确，”梅利克高声叹道，那口气深信不疑，“那么，奥克森登，现在何不给我们唱支歌？”

“胡闹，”费瑟斯通道，“且让医生给我们讲讲阿塔莱布。”

===Original===
“Well,” resumed the doctor, “as I was saying, it must be undoubtedly the Pterodactyl. It is a most extraordinary animal, and is a species of flying lizard, although differing from the lizard in many respects. It has the head and neck of a bird, the trunk and tail of an ordinary mammal, the jaws and teeth of a reptile, and the wings of a bat. Owen describes one whose sweep of wings exceeded twenty feet, and many have been found of every gradation of size down to that of a bat. There is no reason why they should not be as large as More says; and I for my part do not suspect him of exaggeration. Some have supposed that a late, lingering individual may have suggested the idea of the fabulous dragon﻿—an idea which seems to be in the minds of nearly all the human race, for in the early records of many nations we find the destruction of dragons assigned to their gods and heroes. The figure of the Pterodactyl represents pretty closely that which is given to the dragons. It is not impossible that they may have existed into the period which we call prehistoric, and that monsters far larger than any which we have yet discovered may have lingered until the time when man began to increase upon the Earth, to spread over its surface, and to carve upon wood and stone representations of the most striking objects around him. When the living Pterodactyls had disappeared the memory of them was preserved; some new features were added, and the imagination went so far as to endow them with the power of belching forth smoke and flames. Thus the dragon idea pervaded the minds of men, and instead of a natural animal it became a fabulous one.

“The fingers of the forelegs were of the ordinary dimensions, and terminated with crooked nails, and these were probably used to suspend themselves from trees. When in repose it rested on its hind legs like a bird, and held its neck curving behind, so that its enormous head should not disturb its equilibrium. The size and form of the feet, of the leg, and of the thigh prove that they could hold themselves erect with firmness, their wings folded, and move about in this way like birds, just as More describes them as doing. Like birds they could also perch on trees, and could crawl like bats and lizards along the rocks and cliffs.

“Some think that they were covered with scales, but I am of the opinion that they had a horny hide, with a ridge of hair running down their backs﻿—in which opinion I am sustained by More’s account. The smaller kinds were undoubtedly insectivorous, but the larger ones must have been carnivorous, and probably fed largely on fish.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”医生接着讲下去，“方才我说到，它无疑必是翼手龙。这是一种极不寻常的动物，属会飞的蜥蜴一类，虽然在许多方面又与蜥蜴不同。它生着鸟的头颅与颈项，寻常兽类的躯干与尾巴，爬虫的颚与牙齿，还有蝙蝠的双翼。欧文描述过一只两翼展开逾二十英尺的；已经发现的标本大小俱全、等差相递，一路小到蝙蝠那么大。它们没有理由不能长得同莫尔说的那样大；至少我个人毫不疑心他夸张。有人推测，或许正是一只迟迟不肯绝灭、苟延残喘的零星个体，启发了传说巨龙的构想——这一构想似乎盘踞在几乎全体人类的心头，因为在许多民族的早期记载里，我们都见到屠龙之功被归在他们的神祇与英雄名下。翼手龙的形貌，与人们赋给巨龙的形貌颇为吻合。它们未必不可能一直存续到我们所谓的史前时期，而远比我们迄今所发现的任何一种更为巨大的怪物，也可能逗留到人类开始在地球上繁衍、散布到地表各处、并把周遭最触目的物象镌刻上木头与石头的时代。待到活的翼手龙绝迹之后，人们对它们的记忆仍得以保存；又添上若干新的特征，想象力索性走得更远，竟赋予它们喷吐烟焰的本领。于是巨龙的观念弥漫人心，一种自然的动物就此变成了传说的动物。”

“它们前肢的指头尺寸如常，末端生着弯钩般的爪，多半便是用来把自己悬吊在树上的。歇息时，它像鸟一样以两条后腿立地，把脖颈向后弯成弓形，好教那颗庞大的头颅不致坏了身体的均衡。足爪、小腿与大腿的大小形状表明，它们能够收拢双翼、稳稳地直立，并能以这副姿态像鸟一般四处走动——正与莫尔描写的情状一样。它们也能像鸟一般栖于树上，还能像蝙蝠与蜥蜴那样，沿着岩石与峭壁攀爬。”

“有人认为它们遍体覆鳞；我却以为它们长着角质的外皮，脊背上还贯着一条毛脊——在这一见解上，莫尔的记述正好给我作证。小型的种类无疑食虫，大型的则必属食肉，而且多半大量以鱼为食。”

===Original===
“Well, at any rate,” said Melick, gravely, “this athaleb solves the difficult question as to how the Troglodytes emigrated to the South Pole.”

“How?” asked the doctor.

“Why, they must have gone there on athalebs! Your friends the Pterodactyls probably lingered longest among the Troglodytes, who, seeing that they were rapidly dying out, concluded to depart to another and a better world. One beauty of this theory is that it cannot possibly be disproved; another is that it satisfies all the requirements of the case; a third is that it accounts for the disappearance of the Pterodactyls in our world, and their appearance at the South Pole; and there are forty or fifty other facts, all included in this theory, which I have not time just now to enumerate, but will try to do so after we have finished reading the manuscript. I will only add that the athaleb must be regarded as another link which binds the Kosekin to the Semitic race.”

===Chinese===
“也好，无论如何，”梅利克一本正经地说，“这阿塔莱布总算解决了特罗格洛迪特人如何移居南极这道难题。”

“怎么讲？”医生问。

“嗐，他们准是骑着阿塔莱布去的呗！你那些翼手龙朋友，多半就在特罗格洛迪特人中间存留得最久；后者眼见它们日渐凋零、眼看要死绝了，便拿定主意动身前往另一个更好的世界。这套理论有一桩妙处：它根本无从驳倒；第二桩妙处：它满足此案的一切要求；第三桩妙处：它既说通了翼手龙在我们这个世界的绝迹，又说通了它们在南极的现身；此外还有四五十条事实，尽收于此理论的涵盖之下，眼下我无暇一一列举，且等手稿读毕之后再设法补齐。我只想再添一句：这阿塔莱布，必须视为把科塞金人系于闪米特人种的又一条链环。”

===Original===
“Another link?” said Oxenden. “That I already have; and it is one that carries conviction with it.”

“All your arguments invariably do, my dear fellow.”

“What is it?” asked the doctor.

“The Kosekin alphabet,” said Oxenden.

“I can’t see how you can make anything out of that,” said the doctor.

===Chinese===
“又一条链环？”奥克森登说，“我手上早有一条了；而且那一条，是叫人不能不服的。”

“亲爱的伙计，你的论证向来条条如此。”

“是哪一条？”医生问。

“科塞金字母，”奥克森登说。

“我看不出你从那上头能弄出什么名堂来。”医生说。

===Original===
“Very well, I can easily explain,” replied Oxenden. “In the first place we must take the old Hebrew alphabet. I will write down the letters in their order first.”

Saying this he hastily jotted down some letters on a piece of paper, and showed to the doctor the following:

“That,” said he, “is substantially the order of the old Hebrew alphabet.”

“But,” said the doctor, “the Kosekin alphabet differs in its order altogether from that.”

“That very difference can be shown to be all the stronger proof of a connection between them,” said Oxenden.

“I should like to know how.”

===Chinese===
“好说，不难解释，”奥克森登答道，“首先，我们得取古希伯来字母。我先把字母按着次序写下来。”

说罢，他匆匆在一张纸上写下若干字母，递给医生看——写的是如下的东西：

“这，”他说，“大体上便是古希伯来字母的次序。”

“可是，”医生说，“科塞金字母的次序与它全然不同啊。”

“恰恰是这一差异，恰恰可以证明两者之间的关联愈发确凿。”奥克森登说。

“我倒要请教是如何证明的。”

===Original===
“The fact is,” said Oxenden, “these letters are represented differently in the two languages in exact accordance with Grimm’s Law.”

“By Jove!” cried Featherstone, “Grimm’s Law again!”

“According to that law,” continued Oxenden, “the letters of the alphabet ought to change their order. Now let us leave out the vowels and linguals, and deal only with the mutes. First, we have in the Hebrew alphabet the medials *B*, *G*, and *D*. Very well; in the Kosekin we have standing first the thin letters, or ‘tenues,’ according to Grimm’s Law, namely, *P*, *K*, *T*. Next we have in the Hebrew the aspirates *F*, *Ch*, *Dh*. In the Kosekin alphabet we have corresponding to them the medials *B*, *G*, *D*. Next we have in the Hebrew the tenues, or thin letters *P*, *K*, *T*. In the Kosekin we have the corresponding aspirates *F*, *Ch*, *Th*. The vowels, liquids, and sibilants need not be regarded just here, for the proof from the mutes is sufficient to satisfy any reasonable man.”

===Chinese===
“实情是，”奥克森登说，“这些字母在两种语言里的呈现各不相同，而其不同，恰恰严格遵照格林律。”

“天哪！”费瑟斯通喊道，“又是格林律！”

“依照那条定律，”奥克森登接着说，“字母的次序本就该发生变换。现在，且撇开元音与舌音，单论塞音。首先，希伯来字母里有浊音字母 B、G、D。很好；在科塞金字母里，依照格林律，居首的恰是清音字母，即所谓‘tenues’，亦即 P、K、T。其次，希伯来语里有送气音字母 F、Ch、Dh；科塞金字母里与之对应的，是浊音字母 B、G、D。再次，希伯来语里有清音字母、即薄音字母 P、K、T；科塞金语里与之对应的，便是送气音 F、Ch、Th。元音、流音与咝音在此不必挂怀，因为单凭塞音得来的证明，已足以折服任何一个讲道理的人。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Melick, “I for one am thoroughly satisfied, and don’t need another single word. The fact is, I never knew before the all-sufficient nature of Grimm’s Law. Why, it can unlock any mystery! When I get home I must buy one﻿—a tame one, if possible﻿—and keep him with me always. It is more useful to a literary man than to any other. It is said that with a knowledge of Grimm’s Law a man may wander through the world from Iceland to Ceylon, and converse pleasantly in all the Indo-European languages. More must have had Grimm’s Law stowed away somewhere about him; and that’s the reason why he escaped the icebergs, the volcanoes, the cannibals, the subterranean channel monster, and arrived at last safe and sound in the land of the Kosekin. What I want is Grimm’s Law﻿—a nice tidy one, well trained, in good working order, and kind in harness; and the moment I get one I intend to go to the land of the Kosekin myself.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”梅利克说，“我本人算是彻底心服了，再添半个字也不需要。实不相瞒，我从前来竟不知道格林律这万能的品性。嗐，天底下还有什么谜团它解不开！等回了家，我定要买上一条——最好买条驯熟的——永远随身带着。它于文人要比于任何其他人都更有用处。据说只要通晓格林律，一个人便可从冰岛一路游历到锡兰，用各种印欧语言与人谈笑风生。莫尔身上准在什么角落窝藏着一条格林律；所以他才逃过了冰山、火山、食人生番和地下海道里的巨怪，末了安然无恙地抵达科塞金人的国土。我所要的就是一条格林律——一条齐齐整整、干净利落的，调教纯熟，运转灵光，套上轭具也肯乖乖出力；一旦到手，我自己便要动身，到科塞金人的国土去。”

## XXVII Oxenden Preaches a Sermon / 二十七、奥克森登布道

===Original===
“Magones,” said the doctor, “is clearly a volcanic island, and, taken in connection with the other volcanoes around, shows how active must be the subterranean fires at the South Pole. It seems probable to me that the numerous caves of the Kosekin were originally fissures in the mountains, formed by convulsions of nature; and also that the places excavated by man must consist of soft volcanic rock, such as pumice-stone, or rather tufa, easily worked, and remaining permanently in any shape into which it may be fashioned. As to Magones, it seems another Iceland; for there are the same wild and hideous desolation, the same impassable wildernesses, and the same universal scenes of ruin, lighted up by the baleful and tremendous volcanic fires.”
“But what of that little island on which they landed?” asked Featherstone. “That, surely, was not volcanic.”
“No,” said the doctor; “that must have been a coral island.”
“By the by, is it really true,” asked Featherstone, “that these coral islands are the work of little insects?”
“Well, they may be called insects,” replied the doctor; “they are living zoophytes of most minute dimensions, which, however, compensate for their smallness of size by their inconceivable numbers. Small as these are they have accomplished infinitely more than all that ever was done by the Ichthyosaurus, the Plesiosaurus, the Pterodactyl, and the whole tribe of monsters that once filled the Earth. Immense districts and whole mountains have been built up by these minute creatures. They have been at work for ages, and are still at work. It is principally in the South Seas that their labors are carried on. Near the Maldive Islands they have formed a mass whose volume is equal to the Alps. Around New Caledonia they have built a barrier of reefs four hundred miles in length, and another along the northeast coast of Australia a thousand miles in length. In the Pacific Ocean, islands, reefs, and islets innumerable have been constructed by them, which extend for an immense distance.
“The coral islands are called ‘atolls.’ They are nearly always circular, with a depression in the centre. They are originally made ring-shaped, but the action of the ocean serves to throw fragments of rock into the inner depression, which thus fills up; firm land appears; the rock crumbles into soil; the winds and birds and currents bring seeds here, and soon the new island is covered with verdure. These little creatures have played a part in the past quite as important as in the present. All Germany rests upon a bank of coral; and they seem to have been most active during the Oolitic Period.”

===Chinese===
“马戈内斯，”医生说，“显然是一座火山岛；再联系四周其他的火山来看，足见南极地底的火该是何等活跃。依我看，科塞金人那许许多多的洞窟，原本多半是山中的裂隙，由大自然的剧变造成；而那些经人工开凿之处，想必尽是松软的火山岩，例如浮石，或者不如说凝灰岩，易于开凿，又能长久保持塑成的任何形状。至于马戈内斯，它简直是又一座冰岛：那里有同样荒蛮可憎的凄凉，同样不可通行的莽原，同样满目疮痍的景象，只是都被凶险而骇人的火山烈火映照得通明。”

“可他们落脚的那座小岛又怎么说呢？”费瑟斯通问，“那座总归不是火山岛吧。”

“不是，”医生说，“那想必是一座珊瑚岛。”

“对了，说起来，”费瑟斯通问，“这些珊瑚岛当真是小虫子们的造物吗？”

“嗯，把它们叫作虫子也未尝不可，”医生答道，“它们是身量极其细微的植虫（zoophyte），却以多得不可思议的数量弥补了形体的渺小。它们虽小，其成就却无穷无尽地超过鱼龙、蛇颈龙、翼手龙，以及昔日充塞大地的整个巨怪族类全部业绩的总和。辽阔的地带、整座整座的山峦，都是这些微末的生灵堆造起来的。它们已经营作了亿万斯年，如今仍在劳作不息。它们的工程主要在南太平洋诸海域进行。在马尔代夫群岛附近，它们堆起了一座体量堪与阿尔卑斯山相埒的庞大礁体。在新喀里多尼亚周围，它们筑起一道长四百英里的礁堤；沿澳大利亚东北海岸，又筑起另一道长一千英里的。在太平洋里，更有数不清的岛屿、礁脉与礁屿出自它们之手，绵延至极其辽远的地方。

“珊瑚岛称作‘环礁’（atoll）。它们几乎总呈圆形，中央洼陷一片。它们起初便生成环形，但海浪的作用把岩石碎块抛进中间的洼地里，洼地便渐渐填满；坚实的陆地出现了；岩石碎解为土壤；风、鸟与海流把种子带到此处，不久，新岛便披上一层青翠。这些小生灵在过去所扮演的角色，与今日同等重要。整个德意志都坐落在一片珊瑚礁盘之上；而它们最活跃的时代，似乎是在鲕状岩纪（Oolitic Period，即侏罗纪旧称）。

===Original===
“How do the creatures act?” asked Featherstone.
“Nobody knows,” replied the doctor.
A silence now followed, which was at last broken by Oxenden.
“After all,” said he, “these monsters and marvels of nature form the least interesting feature in the land of the Kosekin. To me the people themselves are the chief subject of interest. Where did they get that strange, all-pervading love of death, which is as strong in them as love of life is in us?”
“Why, they got it from the imagination of the writer of the manuscript,” interrupted Melick.
“Yes, it’s easy to answer it from your point of view; yet from my point of view it is more difficult. I sometimes think that it may be the strong spirituality of the Semitic race, carried out under exceptionally favorable circumstances to the ultimate results; for the Semitic race more than all others thought little of this life, and turned their affections to the life that lives beyond this. The Kosekin may thus have had a spiritual development of their own, which ended in this.

===Chinese===
“这些小生灵是怎么营作的？”费瑟斯通问。

“这没人知道。”医生答道。

随后是一阵沉默，末了还是奥克森登打破了它。

“说到底，”他说，“这些自然的巨怪与奇观，在科塞金人的国度里实在是最不足观的部分。在我看来，那里的人民本身才是最耐人寻味的题目。他们从哪里得来那种奇特的、弥漫一切的尚死之情？它在他们心中之强烈，一如爱生之情在我们心中。”

“嗐，那还不是从手稿作者的想象里得来的。”梅利克插嘴道。

“是呀，从你的立足点上作答，自然容易；从我的立足点上，可就难了。我有时想，那或许是闪米特种族强烈的灵性，在格外顺遂的境遇中推演到底的终极结果；因为闪米特种族比一切别的种族都更看轻今生，而把情爱转向今生之外的彼世。科塞金人也许正因如此，走过了一段他们自己的灵性发展，而以这般地步收场。”

===Original===
“Yet there may be another reason for it, and I sometimes think that the Kosekin may be nearer to the truth than we are. We have by nature a strong love of life﻿—it is our dominant feeling﻿—but yet there is in the minds of all men a deep underlying conviction of the vanity of life, and the worthlessness. In all ages and among all races the best, the purest, and the wisest have taught this truth﻿—that human life is not a blessing; that the evil predominates over the good; and that our best hope is to gain a spirit of acquiescence with its inevitable ills. All philosophy and all religions teach us this one solemn truth, that in this life the evil surpasses the good. It has always been so. Suffering has been the lot of all living things, from the giant of the primeval swamps down to the smallest zoophyte. It is far more so with man. Some favored classes in every age may furnish forth a few individuals who may perhaps lead lives of self-indulgence and luxury; but to the mass of mankind life has ever been, and must ever be, a prolonged scene of labor intermingled with suffering. The great Indian religions, whether Brahmanic or Buddhistic, teach as their cardinal doctrine that life is an evil. Buddhism is more pronounced in this, for it teaches more emphatically than even the Kosekin that the chief end of man is to get rid of the curse of life and gain the bliss of Nirvana, or annihilation. True, it does not take so practical a form as among the Kosekin, yet it is believed by one-third of the human race as the foundation of the religion in which they live and die. We need not go to the Kosekin, however, for such maxims as these. The intelligent Hindus, the Chinese, the Japanese, with many other nations, all cling firmly to this belief. Sakyamoum Gautama Buddha, the son and heir of a mighty monarch, penetrated with the conviction of the misery of life, left his throne, embraced a life of voluntary poverty, want, and misery, so that he might find his way to a better state﻿—the end before him being this, that he might ultimately escape from the curse of existence. He lived till old age, gained innumerable followers, and left to them as a solemn legacy the maxim that not to exist is better than to exist; that death is better than life. Since his day millions of his followers have upheld his principles and lived his life. Even among the joyous Greeks we find this feeling at times bursting forth it comes when we least expect it, and not even a Kosekin poet could express this view more forcibly than Sophocles in the *Oedipus at Colonus*:

===Chinese===
“然而，其中或许另有缘故；我有时想，科塞金人没准儿比我们更接近真理。我们天生怀有强烈的爱生之情——那是我们占支配地位的情感——然而在所有人的心底，都深藏着一个信念：人生虚妄，一无可取。历代各族之中，最优秀、最纯粹、最睿智的人士，无不宣示这一真理——人世并非福祉；恶盖过了善；我们最好的指望，便是修得一颗甘顺之心，去承受人生无可回避的诸般苦难。一切哲学与一切宗教，都教给我们这同一个庄严的真理：在此生之中，恶逾于善。从来如此。受苦是一切生灵的定命，从太古泽国中的巨物，直到最小的植虫，概莫能外；于人更何止百倍。每个时代，若干得天独厚的阶级或可供养出寥寥几个耽于逸乐、穷奢极侈的个体；可对人类的大众而言，生活从来是、也永远将是一幅劳苦与磨难交织不尽的长卷。印度的大宗教，无论婆罗门教还是佛教，皆以‘人生是恶’为根本教义。佛教在这一点上走得更为决绝，因为它比科塞金人还斩钉截铁地教导说：人的至高目的，便是摆脱生之诅咒，证得涅槃——亦即寂灭——的至福。诚然，它并不取科塞金人那样切实的形式，却为人类的三分之一所信奉，奉为他们生死以之的宗教根基。其实，这样的箴言我们不必远求于科塞金人。聪慧的印度人、中国人、日本人，连同其他许多民族，都坚守此信。释迦牟尼·乔达摩佛陀，一位强盛君主的子嗣与储君，满怀人生悲苦的信念，抛却王位，甘愿以身践履自愿的贫困、匮乏与苦难，只为觅得一条通往更好境界的路——他眼前的归宿便是：终得脱离存在的诅咒。他活到高龄，收得无数信众，并给他们留下一份庄严的遗训：不生胜于生，死胜于生。自他之后，千百万信众恪守其道、践履其生。即便在欢愉的希腊人中间，这种情感也时而迸发而出——它总在我们最料想不到的时候到来；而论表达之力，纵是科塞金的诗人，也不能胜过《俄狄浦斯在科洛诺斯》中的索福克勒斯：”

===Original===
> “ ‘Not to be born surpasses every lot;
 

 And the next best lot by far, when one is born
 

 Is to go back whence he came as soon as possible;
