 For while youth is present bringing vain follies,
 

 What woes does it not have, what ills does it not bear﻿—
 

 Murders, factions, strife, war, envy,
 

 But the extreme of misery is attained by loathsome old age﻿—
 

 Old age, strengthless, unsociable, friendless,
 

 Where all evils upon evils dwell together.’ ”

===Chinese===
> “‘不曾出生，胜过一切的命数；
 

 而既已出生，其次最好的命数，
 

 便是尽早回到他来的地方去；
 

 因为青春犹在之时，携着虚妄的痴愚，
 

 它又有什么苦楚未曾有、什么灾殃不背负——
 

 谋杀、党争、倾轧、战乱、嫉妒，
 

 然而苦难的极致，终归于可憎的老年——
 

 老年，乏力、孤僻、无友，
 

 万恶叠着万恶，都在那里聚居。’”

===Original===
“I’ll give you the words of a later poet,” said Melick, “who takes a different view of the case. I think I’ll sing them, with your permission.”
Melick swallowed a glass of wine and then sang the following:

> “ ‘They may rail at this life: from the hour I began it
 

 I found it a life full of kindness and bliss,
 

 And until they can show me some happier planet,
 

 More social and bright, I’ll content me with this.
 

 As long as the world has such lips and such eyes
 

 As before me this moment enraptured I see,
 

 They may say what they will of their orbs in the skies,
 

 But this Earth is the planet for you, love, and me.’

===Chinese===
“我来给你引一位后世诗人的词句吧，”梅利克说，“他对这桩公案持的是另一种见解。诸位若肯俯允，我想把它们唱出来。”

梅利克灌下一杯葡萄酒，随即唱道：

> “‘任凭世人贬斥此生：自我踏上它的时辰起，
 

 我便发觉此生满是温情与至乐；
 

 除非他们能指给我一颗更幸福的行星，
 

 更热闹、更明亮，否则我于此已知足。
 

 只要这世上还有这样的唇、这样的眼睛，
 

 像此刻在我面前令我心醉神迷的这般，
 

 随他们去夸赞天上的那些星辰，
 

 但这地球才是你、亲爱的、和我的行星。’”

===Original===
“What a pity it is,” continued Melick, “that the writer of this manuscript had not the philological, theological, sociological, geological, palaeological, ontological, ornithological, and all the other logical attainments of yourself and the doctor! He could then have given us a complete view of the nature of the Kosekin, morally and physically; he could have treated of the geology of the soil, the ethnology of the people, and could have unfolded before us a full and comprehensive view of their philosophy and religion, and could have crammed his manuscript with statistics. I wonder why he didn’t do it even as it was. It must have been a strong temptation.”
“More,” said Oxenden, with deep impressiveness, “was a simple-minded though somewhat emotional sailor, and merely wrote in the hope that his story might one day meet the eyes of his father. I certainly should like to find some more accurate statements about the science, philosophy, and religion of the Kosekin; yet, after all, such things could not be expected.”
“Why not?” said Melick; “it was easy enough for him.”
“How?” asked Oxenden.
“Why, he had only to step into the British Museum, and in a couple of hours he could have crammed up on all those points in science, philosophy, ethnology, and theology, about which you are so anxious to know.”

===Chinese===
“真遗憾，”梅利克接着说，“这位手稿的作者竟不曾具备您和医生两位于语言学、神学、社会学、地质学、古生物学、本体论、鸟类学，以及其余一切种种‘学’上的造诣！否则他本可以就科塞金人的本性，从道德到体格，给我们一个完整的概观；他本可以论述那片土地的地质、那个民族的人种源流，本可以在我们面前铺陈出一幅全备而宏富的图景，展现他们的哲学与宗教，还可以往手稿里塞满统计数字。我不明白，即便在当时的情形之下，他怎么竟没有这么做。那必定是个不小的诱惑。”

“莫尔，”奥克森登语气深沉恳切地说，“是个心思单纯、略带感伤的水手，他写作不过是存着一念之望：盼他的故事有朝一日能落入他父亲的眼中。我自然也希望能读到关于科塞金人的科学、哲学与宗教的更精确的陈述；可是说到底，这类东西本就不能指望。”

“为什么不能？”梅利克说，“这对他容易得很。”

“怎么讲？”奥克森登问。

“嗐，他只消迈进大英博物馆，花上几个钟头，就能把你急于知晓的科学、哲学、人种学、神学种种方面，统统突击个通透。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Featherstone, “suppose we continue our reading? I believe it is my turn now. I shan’t be able to hold out so long as you did, Oxenden, but I’ll do what I can.”
Saying this, Featherstone took the manuscript and went on to read.

===Chinese===
“好了，”费瑟斯通说，“我们接着往下读如何？我想现在该轮到我了。我撑不了你那么久，奥克森登，不过我尽力而为。”

说着，费瑟斯通取过手稿，继续读了下去。

## XXVIII In Prison / 二十八、狱中

===Original===
It was with hearts full of the gloomiest forebodings that we returned to the *amir*, and these we soon found to be fully justified. The athalebs descended at that point from which they had risen﻿—namely, on the terrace immediately in front of the cavern where they had been confined. We then dismounted, and Layelah with the Kosekin guards accompanied us to our former chambers. There she left us, saying that a communication would be sent to us.

We were now left to our own conjectures.

“I wonder what they will do to us?” said I.

“It is impossible to tell,” said Almah.

“I suppose,” said I, “they will punish us in some way; but then punishment among the Kosekin is what seems honor and reward to me. Perhaps they will spare our lives, for that in their eyes ought to be the severest punishment and the deepest disgrace imaginable.”

===Chinese===
我们怀着满心最阴郁的凶兆回到阿米尔，而且不久便发觉，这些凶兆果然完全应验。阿塔莱布在原先腾起的那处地方降落下来——就在囚禁它们的那座山洞正前方的梯台上。我们于是下了坐骑，莱拉带着科塞金卫兵，押送我们回到原先的居室，便在那里离开了我们，说稍后自有公文传达给我们。

这一下，只剩我们自己去胡乱揣度了。

“不知道他们会怎么处置我们？”我说。

“这无从料想。”阿尔玛说。

“我猜，”我说，“他们总要想法子惩罚我们；可在科塞金人中间算是惩罚的，在我看来无宁是荣耀与犒赏。说不定他们会饶下我们的性命，因为在他们眼里，这该是想得到的最重的刑罚、最深的耻辱了。”

===Original===
Almah sighed.

“The Kosekin do not always act in this matter as one would suppose,” said she. “It is quite likely that they may dread our escaping, and may conclude to sacrifice us at once.”

On the next *jom* I had a visit from the Kohen Gadol. He informed me that the paupers had held a Council of State, in which they had made a special examination of our late flight. He and Layelah had both been examined, as well as the Kosekin who had gone after us; but Layelah’s testimony was by far the most important.

The Council of State gathered from Layelah’s report that we had fled to Magones for the especial purpose of gaining the most blessed of deaths; that she pursued us in the interest of the State; and that we on her arrival had generously surrendered our own selfish desires, and had at once returned.

We learned that much gratification was felt by the council, and also expressed, at Layelah’s account and at our action.

===Chinese===
阿尔玛叹了口气。

“科塞金人在这上头行事，未必如人所想，”她说，“他们很可能唯恐我们再逃，索性议定即刻把我们献祭了。”

到了下一个约姆（jom），大祭司来探访我。他告知我，赤贫者们开了一回国务会议，专门查究了我们新近的这场出走。他和莱拉都受了盘问，追我们去的那些科塞金人也都受了盘问；只是莱拉的证词，分量远比谁都重。

国务会议从莱拉的呈报里得出结论：我们逃往马戈内斯，专是为求取那至为蒙福的死；她则是为着国家的利益追踪我们；而她一到，我们又慨然舍了一己的私愿，当即随她折返。

我们得知，议会方面对莱拉的陈述同我们的行事都深感快慰，而且形于言表。

===Original===
First, at our eager love of death, which was so natural in their eyes; secondly, at the skill which we had shown in selecting Magones; and finally, at our generosity in giving up so readily the blessed prospect of exile and want and death, so as to come back to the *amir*. Had we been Kosekin our acts would have been natural enough; but, being foreigners, it was considered more admirable in us, and it seemed to show that we were equal to the Kosekin themselves. It was felt, however, that in our eager rush after death we had been somewhat selfish; but as this probably arose from our ignorance of the law, it might be overlooked. On the whole it was decided that we ought to be rewarded, and that, too, with the greatest benefits that the Kosekin could bestow. What these benefits were the Kohen Gadol could not say; and thus we were left, as before, in the greatest possible anxiety. We still dreaded the worst. The highest honors of these men might well awaken apprehension; for they thought that the chief blessings were poverty and darkness and death.

Layelah next came to see me. She was as amiable as ever, and showed no resentment at all. She gave me an account of what had happened at the Council of State, which was the same as what I had heard from the Kohen Gadol.

===Chinese===
第一，快慰于我们对死亡的急切爱慕——这在他们眼里是再自然不过的性情；第二，快慰于我们拣选马戈内斯时显出的眼力；末了，快慰于我们的慷慨——竟能那样爽利地舍弃流放、匮乏与死亡这一份蒙福的前景，回到阿米尔来。我们若真是科塞金人，这般行止原属寻常；但身是外邦人，便愈发显得难能，似乎表明我们竟堪与科塞金人自身比肩。只是议会也觉得，我们这番奔赴死亡的急切里未免带几分自私；不过这想必出于我们不明律法，尚可宽宥。大要而言，议会议定我们理当受赏，而且要赏以科塞金人所能颁赐的至大恩福。这些恩福究竟是什么，大祭司也说不上来；于是我们一如既往，被留在无可再加的忧惧之中。我们仍旧惧怕最坏的结局。这些人的至高荣耀，尽够叫人心惊胆战；因为照他们想，头一等的福分，正是贫穷、黑暗与死亡。

随后来看我的是莱拉。她一如既往地和婉可亲，没有半点衔恨的样子。她把国务会议的经过对我讲了一遍，同我从大祭司那里听来的毫无二致。

===Original===
I asked her why she had made such a report of us.

“To conciliate their goodwill,” said Layelah. “For if they thought that you had really fled from death from a love of life, they would have felt such contempt for you that serious harm might have happened.”

“Yes,” said I; “but among the Kosekin what you call harm would probably have been just what I want. I should like to be viewed with contempt, and considered unworthy of death and the Mista Kosek, and other such honors.”

“Oh yes,” said Layelah; “but that doesn’t follow; for you see the paupers love death so intensely that they long to bestow it on all; and if they knew that you were afraid of it, they would be tempted to bestow it upon you immediately, just to show you how delightful a thing it is. And that was the very thing that I was trying to guard against.”

===Chinese===
我问她，为什么把我们的事报成那般。

“为讨他们的好感，”莱拉说，“他们若当真以为你因为贪恋生命而逃避死亡，就会鄙夷你到那个地步，只怕要闯出大祸来。”

“是啊，”我说，“可在科塞金人中间，你所称的祸事，多半恰恰是我求之不得的。我倒情愿受人鄙夷，被看作不配死亡、不配米斯塔·科塞克、不配这一类荣耀的人。”

“哦，是啊，”莱拉说，“可事情不会那样顺当；你要明白，赤贫者们爱死亡爱得太深切，巴不得把它赐给所有人；他们若知道你怕死，就会按捺不住，当即把死亡赐到你身上，好教你知道这是多么甘美的一件事。而我竭力要防的，恰恰就是这一条。”

===Original===
“Well,” said I, “and what is the result? Do you know what their decision is?”

“Yes,” said Layelah.

“What is it?” I asked, eagerly.

Layelah hesitated.

“What is it?” I cried again, full of impatience.

“I’m afraid it will not sound very pleasant to you,” said Layelah, “but at any rate your life is spared for the present. They have decided to give you what they call the greatest possible honors and distinctions.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”我说，“结果怎样？你可知道他们的裁决么？”

“知道。”莱拉说。

“是什么？”我急切地问。

莱拉迟疑着。

“到底是什么？”我又喊道，满心不耐。

“我怕说出口来，你听着不会痛快，”莱拉说，“不过无论如何，眼下你的性命是保住了。他们决意要授给你他们所谓的至高无上的尊荣与殊恩。”

===Original===
Layelah paused, and looked at me earnestly. For my part these words sounded ominous, and were full of the darkest meaning.

“Tell me all,” I said; “don’t keep me in suspense.”

“Well,” said Layelah, “I’m afraid you will think it hard; but I must tell you. I will tell it, therefore, as briefly and formally as possible.

===Chinese===
莱拉停了停，恳切地望着我。在我这方面，这些话听来字字凶险，浸透了最黑暗的意味。

“全都告诉我，”我说，“别叫我悬着心。”

“好吧，”莱拉说，“我怕你会觉得难以消受；可我必须告诉你。那么，我就尽量简括、尽量照着官样来讲。

===Original===
“First, then, they have decreed the blessing of separation. You and Almah must now be parted, since this is regarded as the highest bliss of lovers.

“Secondly, they have decreed the blessing of poverty. All these luxuries will be taken away, and you will be raised to an equality in this respect with the great paupers.

“Thirdly, you are to have the blessing of darkness. You are to be removed from this troublesome and vexatious light, which here is regarded as a curse, and henceforth live without it.

“Fourthly, the next decree is the high reward of imprisonment. You are to be delivered from the evils of liberty, and shut up in a dark cavern, from which it will be impossible to escape or to communicate with anyone outside.

“Fifthly, you are to associate with the greatest of the paupers, the class that is the most honored and influential. You will be present at all their highest councils, and will have the privilege of perpetual intercourse with those reverend men. They will tell you of the joys of poverty, the happiness of darkness, and the bliss of death.”

===Chinese===
“第一，他们颁下了分离的福分。你同阿尔玛从此必须分开，因为这在他们看来是情人们的至福。

“第二，他们颁下了贫穷的福分。这一切奢华都要撤去，你在这一节上要擢升到与头等赤贫者平等的地位。

“第三，你要领受黑暗的福分。你要从这道烦扰硌人的亮光里挪出去——亮光在此地被看作一种咒诅——从此生活在无光之中。

“第四，下一道敕令，是监禁的重赏。你要从自由的种种祸害里被解救出来，关进一处黑洞洞的窟穴，从那里既逃不脱，也休想同外面任何人通个消息。

“第五，你要同最尊贵的赤贫者们过从——那是最受尊崇、最有权势的一等人。他们最高的各项议事你都要列席，并且有幸同那些可敬的人永远周旋。他们会对你讲贫穷之乐、黑暗之福与死亡之至乐。”

===Original===
Layelah paused, and looked at me earnestly.

“Is there anything more?” I gasped.

“No,” said she. “Is not that enough? Some were in favor of bestowing immediate death, but they were outvoted by the others. You surely cannot regret that.”

Layelah’s words sounded like the words of a mocking demon. Yet she did not wish to distress me; she had merely stated my sentence in formal language, without any attempt to soften its tremendous import. As for me, I was overwhelmed with despair. There was but one thought in my mind﻿—it was not of myself, but of Almah.

===Chinese===
莱拉停了停，恳切地望着我。

“还有么？”我喘着气问。

“没有了，”她说，“这还不够么？有几位本主张当即赐死，却被其余的人投票压倒了。这个你总不至于懊悔吧。”

莱拉这些话，听来像是一个揶揄的恶魔所说的话。然而她并非有意折磨我；她不过用官样的言语宣示了我的判决，毫不设法缓及其可怕的分量。至于我，早已被绝望压倒。心里只剩一个念头——不是为我自己，而是为阿尔玛。

===Original===
“And Almah?” I cried.

“Almah,” said Layelah﻿—“she will have the same; you are both included in the same sentence.”

At this a groan burst from me. Horror overwhelmed me. I threw myself down upon the floor and covered my face with my hands. All was lost! Our fate﻿—Almah’s fate﻿—was darkness, imprisonment, and death. Could anything be imagined that might mitigate such woes as these? Could anything be conceived of as more horrible? Yes; there remained something more, and this was announced by Layelah.

===Chinese===
“那阿尔玛呢？”我喊道。

“阿尔玛，”莱拉说——“她的处分一模一样；你们两个同在这一纸判决之内。”

听了这话，我迸出一声呻吟。恐怖将我整个吞没。我扑倒在地，双手掩面。一切都完了！我们的命运——阿尔玛的命运——是黑暗、监禁与死亡。还能想出什么来纾解这样的苦难么？还能设想什么比这更可怕么？能的；还有更甚的在后头，而这正是莱拉接下来要宣布的。

===Original===
“Finally,” said she, “it has been decreed that you shall not only have the blessing of death, but that you shall have the rare honor of belonging to the chosen few who are reserved for the Mista Kosek. Thus far this had not been granted. It was esteemed too high an honor for strangers; but now, by an exercise of unparalleled liberality, the Grand Council of Paupers have added this, as the last and best, to the high honors and rewards which they have decreed for you and Almah.”

To this I had nothing to say; I was stupefied with horror. To such words what answer could be made? At that moment I could think of nothing but this tremendous sentence﻿—this infliction of appalling woes under the miserable name of blessings! I could not think of Layelah; nor did I try to conjecture what her motives might be in thus coming to me as the messenger of evil. I could not find space amid my despair for speculations as to her own part in this, or stop to consider whether she was acting the part of a mere messenger, or was influenced by resentment or revenge. All this was far away from my thoughts; for all my mind was filled with the dread sentence of the Council of Paupers and the baleful prospect of the woes that awaited us.

===Chinese===
“最后，”她说，“敕令已定：你不惟要领受死亡的福分，还要享有一项罕有的殊荣——跻身于那班蒙拣选、留待米斯塔·科塞克之用的少数人之中。这恩典此前从不曾颁给外人；那素来被看作外邦人受之有愧的过高尊荣；可是如今，赤贫者大议会施展了旷古未有的宽宏，把这一项作为末后的至善，追加进他们为你和阿尔玛颁定的种种尊荣与恩赏之中。”

对此我无话可说；我已被恐怖震得呆了。这样的话，还能拿什么作答？那一刻，我满心只有这道骇人的判决——这顶着福分这一凄惨名目施加的骇人祸殃！我顾不上想莱拉，也不去揣测她这样跑来充当报凶的信使，用意究竟何在。绝望之中，我已无余地推究她在里头的干系，也无心细辨她是纯然做一个传话的差役，还是出于怀恨与报复。这一切都远远离开我的心头；因为我全部的心神，都被赤贫者议会那可怕的判决，同那等候着我们的祸殃的不祥前景塞满了。

===Original===
On the next *jom* I saw Almah. She had already learned the awful tidings. She met me with a face of despair; for there was no longer any hope, and all that remained for us was a last farewell. After this we parted, and each of us was taken to our respective prison.

I was taken along dark passages until I came to a cavern with a low, dark portal. Upon entering I found the darkness deeper than usual, and there was only one solitary lamp, which diffused but a feeble ray through the gloom. The size of the place could not be made out. I saw here a group of human beings, and by the feeble ray of the lamp I perceived that they were wan and thin and emaciated, with scant clothing, all in rags, squalor, misery, and dirt; with coarse hair matted together, and long nails and shaggy beards. They reminded me in their personal appearance of the cannibals of the outer shore. These hideous beings all gathered around me, blinking at me with their bleary eyes and grinning with their abominable faces, and then each one embraced me. The filth, squalor, and unutterable foulness of these wretches all combined to fill my soul with loathing, and the inconceivable horror of that embrace well-nigh overwhelmed me. Yet, after all, it was surpassed by the horror of the thought that Almah might be at that very moment undergoing the same experience; and for her such a thing must be worse than for me.

===Chinese===
到了下一个约姆，我见到了阿尔玛。那可怕的消息她已听闻。她迎着我，一脸绝望；因为再无一丝指望，留给我们的只剩下最后的一别。别过之后，我们便分手，各自被押往各自的监狱。

我被押着走过一条条黑暗的甬道，来到一座窟室跟前，门洞低矮黢黑。一进门便觉得此处的黑暗比寻常更深，只有孤零零一盏灯，在幽暗里散出一缕微光。这地方有多大，全然辨不出来。我看见这里有一群人；就着那点灯光，我看清他们个个枯瘦羸弱、形销骨立，衣衫单薄，通身褴褛、腌臜、凄惨、污垢；头发粗硬纠结成毡，指甲长长的，胡须蓬乱如莽。他们那副形貌，叫我想起外海岸的食人生番。这些丑类一齐围上前来，睁着烂涩昏花的眼睛朝我眨动，咧着狰狞可憎的脸发笑，随后一个个同我拥抱。这班可怜虫的腌臜、污秽与说不出的龌龊，合起来激满了我灵魂的憎恶；而那拥抱之难以想象的恐怖，几乎将我整个压倒。然而到底还有更甚的：一想到阿尔玛此际说不定正受着同样的遭遇，这点恐怖便被盖过了；而且这样的事落在她身上，必定比落在我身上更难堪。

===Original===
I retreated as far as possible from them, deep into the thick darkness, and sat down. No convicted felon at the last hour of life, no prisoner in the dungeons of the Inquisition, ever could have suffered more mental agony than I did at that moment. The blessings, the awful blessings of the Kosekin were descending upon my miserable head﻿—separation from Almah, squalor and dirt, imprisonment, the society of these filthy creatures, darkness, the shadow of death, and beyond all the tremendous horrors of the Mista Kosek!

I do not know how the time passed, for at first I was almost stupefied with despair; nor could I ever grow reconciled to the society of these wretches, scarce human, who were with me. Some food was offered me﻿—filthy stuff, which I refused. My refusal excited warm commendation; but I was warned against starving myself, as that was against the law. In my despair I thought of my pistol and rifle, which I still kept with me﻿—of using these against my jailors, and bursting forth; but this wild impulse soon passed away, for its utter hopelessness was manifest. My only hope, if hope it was, lay in waiting, and it was not impossible that I might see Almah again, if only once.

===Chinese===
我从他们跟前退开，退得越远越好，退进浓黑的深处，坐了下来。便是死囚在生命的最后时辰，便是宗教裁判所地牢里的囚徒，其精神上的痛楚，也断乎不至于过于此刻的我。科塞金人的种种福分、种种骇人的福分，正纷纷落到我这不幸的头顶上——与阿尔玛的分离、腌臜与污秽、监禁、同这班肮脏东西为伍、黑暗、死亡的阴影，而在这一切之上，还有米斯塔·科塞克那惊心动魄的恐怖！

时光怎么过去的，我说不上来，因为起初我几乎被绝望弄呆了；同这班几乎不成其为人、与我共处一窟的可怜东西，我也终究处不惯。他们给我端来吃食——腌臜东西，我推开了。我这一推辞，倒激起一片热切的称许；可是又有人告诫我不可绝食饿死自己，因为那是干犯法律的。绝望之下，我想起仍带在身边的手枪和步枪——想拿它们对付看守，杀将出去；但这狂乱的冲动不久便消散了，因为它的全然无望是明摆着的。我唯一的指望——倘若这也算得指望——在于等待；再见阿尔玛一面，哪怕只得一见，也未必全无可能。

===Original===
*Joms* passed away, I know not how. The Chief Pauper, who is the greatest man in the land of the Kosekin, made several attempts to converse with me, and was evidently very condescending and magnanimous in his own eyes; but I did not meet his advances graciously﻿—he was too abhorrent. He was a hideous wretch, with eyes nearly closed and bleary, thick, matted hair, and fiendish expression﻿—in short, a devil incarnate in rags and squalor.

But as the *joms* passed I found it difficult to repel my associates. They were always inflicting their society upon me, and thrusting on me nasty little acts of kindness. The Chief Pauper was more persistent than all, with his chatter and his disgusting civilities. He was evidently glad to get hold of a fresh subject for his talkative genius; he was a very garrulous cannibal, and perhaps my being a foreigner made me more interesting in his eyes.

The chief topic of his discourse was death. He hated life, loved death, longed for it in all its forms, whether arising from disease or from violence. He was an amateur in corpses, and had a larger experience in dead bodies than any other man in the nation.

===Chinese===
约姆一个接一个过去，怎么过去的，我不知。首席赤贫者——他是科塞金人国土上最伟大的人物——几次三番想法子同我攀谈，而且显然自认为极其屈尊、极其大量；只是他那份殷勤我消受不起——他实在太可憎了。他是个面目狰狞的可怜东西：两眼几乎闭拢，烂涩昏花；头发又浓又厚，黏结成毡；一脸凶相——总而言之，是个裹在褴褛腌臜里的魔鬼化身。

可是随着约姆过去，我发现要推开我这班同伴着实不易。他们总把他们的相伴强加于我，硬塞给我种种龌龊的小殷勤。首席赤贫者比谁都锲而不舍，聒聒噪噪，那套令人作呕的客气没完没了。他显然很乐于抓住我这个新鲜题目，好供他那健谈的天才发挥；他是个极其饶舌的食人生番，何况我又是个外邦人，在他眼里恐怕格外有趣。

他言谈的主要题目就是死亡。他憎恶生命，热爱死亡，渴慕它的一切形态，无论得自疾病，还是得自暴力。他是死尸的行家，论经手的死人，全国再没有人比他阅历更深。

===Original===
I could not help asking him once why he did not kill himself, and be done with it.

“That,” said he, “is not allowed. The temptation to kill one’s self is one of the strongest that human nature can experience, but it is one that we must struggle against, of course, for it is against all law. The greatest blessing must not be seized. It must be given by nature or man. Those who violate the blessed mystery of death are infamous.”

He assured me that he had all his life cultivated the loftiest feelings of love to others. His greatest happiness consisted in doing good to others, especially in killing them. The blessing of death, being the greatest of all blessings, was the one which he loved best to bestow upon others; and the more he loved his fellow-creatures the more he wished to give them this blessing. “You,” said he, “are particularly dear to me, and I should rather give to you the blessing of death than to any other human being. I love you, Atam-or, and I long to kill you at this moment.”

===Chinese===
有一回，我忍不住问他，何不干脆自杀了事。

“那，”他说，“是不容许的。自杀的诱惑，是人性能经验的最强烈的诱惑之一，可我们当然必须同它搏斗，因为它违犯一切法律。至大的福分不可以强取。它必须由自然或者他人赐下。亵渎死亡这蒙福奥秘的人，声名扫地。”

他向我保证，他一生都在涵养对他人最崇高的仁爱之情。他最大的幸福在于为他人行善，尤其是杀死他们。死亡的福分既是万福之最，自然也是他最喜欢颁给旁人的一桩；他爱同类爱得越深，便越想把这份福分赐给他们。“你，”他说，“于我尤其亲爱；把死亡的福分赐给你，比赐给世上任何别人都更叫我情愿。我爱你，阿塔莫尔，此时此刻我就渴望杀死你。”

===Original===
“You had better not try it,” said I, grimly.

He shook his head despondingly.

“Oh no,” said he; “it is against the law. I must not do it till the time comes.”

“Do you kill many?” I asked.

“It is my pleasing and glorious office,” he replied, “to kill more than any other; for, you must know, I am the *Sar Tabakin*” (chief of the executioners).

===Chinese===
“那你最好别试。”我狠声说。

他沮丧地摇了摇头。

“哦，不，”他说，“那是犯法的。时辰不到，我千万动不得手。”

“你杀的人多么？”我问。

“我这桩差使，”他答道，“又可喜又光荣，杀的人比谁都多；因为，你必须知道，我就是萨尔·塔巴金（Sar Tabakin）”（刽子手之首）。

===Original===
The Chief Pauper’s love of death had grown to be an all-absorbing passion. He longed to give death to all. As with us there are certain philanthropists who have a mania for doing good, so here the pauper class had a mania for doing what they considered good in this way. The Chief Pauper was a sort of Kosekin Howard or Peabody, and was regarded by all with boundless reverence. To me, however, he was an object of never-ending hate, abhorrence, and loathing; and, added to this, was the thought that there might be here some equally hideous female﻿—someone like the nightmare hag of the outer sea﻿—a torment and a horror to Almah.

===Chinese===
首席赤贫者对死亡的爱，已经长成一种并吞一切的癖好。他渴望把死亡赐给所有人。正如我们那边有些慈善家，行善行成了癖，这里赤贫者阶级也拿他们心目中的行善之道养成了癖。首席赤贫者俨然是科塞金人的霍华德（Howard）或皮博迪（Peabody），受着众人无边的崇敬。然而在我眼里，他却是个永无了局地招恨、惹憎、令人作呕的东西；何况我又想到，此处说不定另有一个同样丑恶的女人——某个同外海那个梦魇老妪一路的人物——要成为阿尔玛的折磨与恐怖。

## XXIX The Ceremony of Separation / 二十九、分离仪式
===Original===
Separated from Almah, surrounded by foul fiends, in darkness and the shadow of death, with the baleful prospect of the Mista Kosek, it was mine to endure the bitterest anguish and despair; and in me these feelings were all the worse from the thought that Almah was in a similar state, and was enduring equal woes. All that I suffered in my present condition she too was suffering﻿—and from this there was no possibility of escape. Perhaps her surroundings were even worse, and her sufferings keener; for who could tell what these people might inflict in their strange and perverted impulses?

Many *joms* passed, and there was only one thing that sustained me﻿—the hope of seeing Almah yet again, though it were but for a moment. That hope, however, was but faint. There was no escape. The gate was barred without and within. I was surrounded by miscreants, who formed the chief class in the state and the ruling order. The Chief Pauper was the highest magistrate in the land, from whose opinion there was no appeal, and the other paupers here formed the Kosekin senate. Here, in imprisonment and darkness, they formed a secret tribunal and controlled everything. They were objects of envy to all. All looked forward to this position as the highest object of human ambition, and the friends and relatives of those here rejoiced in their honor. Their powers were not executive, but deliberative. To the Meleks and Athons was left the exercise of authority, but their acts were always in subordination to the will of the paupers.

===Chinese===
与阿尔玛分离了，四面围着腌臜的魔鬼，处在黑暗和死亡的阴影里，面前又悬着米斯塔·科塞克那凶险的前景——最苦的痛楚与绝望，如今要由我来受。而这些滋味落在我身上，又格外沉重，因为一想到阿尔玛也身处同样的境地，受着同等的苦难。我现状所受的一切，她也都在受——而这是万无脱逃之望的。或许她四周的情形比我的还要坏，她的苦楚还要更深；因为这班人在他们那些乖僻倒错的冲动之下会做出什么来，谁又说得准呢？

许多个约姆过去了，支撑着我的只剩一件事——巴望还能再见到阿尔玛，哪怕只有一瞬也好。然而这指望微弱得很。逃是逃不出去的。牢门里外都上了闩。围着我的是一班恶徒，他们正是这国中的头等阶级、统治的阶层。首席赤贫者是全境最高的裁断者，他的意旨之下再无上诉一说；其余的赤贫者便在此间组成科塞金的元老院。他们就在这监禁与黑暗之中结成一个秘密法庭，操持着一切。他们是众人艳羡的对象。人人都把这样的位置悬为人生至高的鹄的；此处诸人的亲友，无不为他们所享的荣耀而欢喜。他们的权柄不在施行，而在议决。权柄的行使留给美勒克们与阿通们，只是他们的举措，永远须听命于赤贫者之意。

===Original===
“I have everything that heart can wish,” said the Chief Pauper to me once. “Look at me, Atam-or, and see me as I stand here: I have poverty, squalor, cold, perpetual darkness, the privilege of killing others, the near prospect of death, and the certainty of the Mista Kosek﻿—all these I have, and yet, Atam-or, after all, I am not happy.”

To this strange speech I had nothing to say.

“Yes,” continued the Chief Pauper, in a pensive tone, “for twenty seasons I have reigned as chief of the Kosekin in this place. My cavern is the coldest, squalidest, and darkest in the land. My raiment is the coarsest rags. I have separated from all my friends. I have had much sickness. I have the closest captivity. Death, darkness, poverty, want, all that men most live and long for, are mine to satiety; and yet, as I look back and count the *joms* of my life to see in how many I have known happiness, I find that in all they amount to just seven! Oh, Atam-or, what a comment is this on the vanity of human life!”

To this I had no answer ready; but by way of saying something, I offered to kill him on the spot.

===Chinese===
“人心里想望的东西，我样样都有了，”首席赤贫者有一回对我说。“你看我，阿塔莫尔，瞧瞧我站在这里的这副样子：我有贫穷、腌臜、寒冷、无尽的黑暗，有杀人的特权，有近在眼前的死亡，还有米斯塔·科塞克的万无一失——这些我全有了，可是，阿塔莫尔，说到底，我并不快活。”

对这番怪话，我无话可答。

“是啊，”首席赤贫者接着说，语调郁郁若有所思，“二十个季度了，我在此地做科塞金人的首领。我的洞窟是全国最冷、最腌臜、最黑的。我的衣裳是最粗的破布。我同所有的亲友都分离了。我害过许多病。我受着最严密的拘禁。死亡、黑暗、贫穷、匮乏——世人最宝爱渴慕的一切——我都饱享到了餍足；可是，当我回首把一生的约姆逐一数过，要看看究竟有多少个约姆里我尝到过快活，我发现统统加起来，恰恰只有七个！唉，阿塔莫尔，人生之虚妄，这是何等的一则注脚啊！”

这话我一时也无从答起；但为了不至于哑口无言，我便提出，不妨当场就把他杀死。

===Original===
“Nay, nay, Atam-or,” said he, with a melancholy smile, “do not tempt me. Leave me to struggle with temptations by myself, and do not seek to make me falter in my duty. Yes, Atam-or, you behold in me a melancholy example of the folly of ambition; for I often think, as I look down from my lofty eminence, that after all it is as well to remain content in the humble sphere in which we are placed at birth; for perhaps, if the truth were known, there is quite as much real happiness among the rich and splendid﻿—among the Athons and Meleks.”

On this occasion I took advantage of the Chief Pauper’s softer mood to pour forth an earnest entreaty for him to save Almah’s life, or at least to mitigate her miseries. Alas! he was inexorable. It was like an appeal of some mad prisoner to some gentle-hearted governor in Christendom, entreating him to put some fellow-prisoner to death, or at least to make his confinement more severe.

The Chief Pauper stared at me in horror.

“You are a strange being, Atam-or,” said he, gently. “Sometimes I think you mad. I can only say that such a request is horrible to me beyond all words. Such degradation and cruelty to the gentle and virtuous Almah is outrageous and forever impossible; no, we will not deprive her of a single one of those blessings which she now enjoys.”

I turned away in despair.

===Chinese===
“不，不，阿塔莫尔，”他说，带着凄凉的微笑，“不要引诱我。由我独自去同诱惑搏斗，不要想法子叫我在职分上动摇。是啊，阿塔莫尔，你在我身上看见的，是野心之愚的一个凄凉的榜样；因为我从这高高的地位俯望下来时常常想，说到底，安分守着生来所处的卑微境地，倒也罢了；因为倘若把底细揭穿，只怕那班富贵华美之人——阿通们和美勒克们——中间，真正的快活也一样的多。”

这一回，我趁首席赤贫者心肠较软，便恳切地苦苦哀求他救阿尔玛的性命，或者至少减轻她的苦难。唉！他偏偏不肯通融。这就像基督教世界里某个疯癫的囚徒，向某位心慈的典狱长苦苦哀告，求他把某个狱友处死，或者至少把他的监禁弄得更严些。

首席赤贫者骇然瞪着我。

“你真是个奇怪的人，阿塔莫尔，”他温和地说，“有时我觉得你疯了。我只能说，这样的请求，在我看来可怕得无以复加。对温良贤淑的阿尔玛施加这样的糟践与残忍，是伤天害理，万世不能；不，她如今享有的那些福分，我们一样也不容她缺少。”

我绝望地掉过身去。

===Original===
At length one *jom* the Chief Pauper came to me with a smile and said,

“Atam-or, let me congratulate you on this joyous occasion.”

“What do you mean?” I asked.

“You are to have your ceremony of separation.”

“Separation!” I repeated.

“Yes,” said he. “Almah has given notice to us. She has announced her intention of giving you up, and separating from you. With us the woman always gives the announcement in such cases. We have fixed the ceremony for the third *jom* from this, and I hope you will not think it too soon.”

===Chinese===
末了，有一个约姆，首席赤贫者含着笑到我这里来，说道，

“阿塔莫尔，在这大喜的日子里，请容我向你道贺。”

“你这话是什么意思？”我问。

“你的分离仪式就要举行了。”

“分离！”我重复了一遍。

“是啊，”他说，“阿尔玛已经知会了我们。她宣布有意舍掉你，同你分离。在我们这里，这类事总由女方出面宣告。仪式已定在即日起第三个约姆举行，望你不要嫌太仓促。”

===Original===
This strange intelligence moved me greatly. I did not like the idea of a ceremony of separation; but behind this there rose the prospect of seeing Almah, and I felt convinced that she had devised this as a mode of holding communication with me, or at least of seeing me again. The thought of Layelah was the only thing that interfered with this belief, for it might be her doings after all; yet the fact remained that I was to see Almah, and in this I rejoiced with exceeding great joy.

The appointed *jom* came. A procession was formed of the paupers. The chief did not go, as he never left the cavern except on the great sacrifices and *Mista Koseks*. The door was opened, and I accompanied the procession. On our way all was dark, and after traversing many passages we came at length to the door of a cavern as gloomy as the one I had left. On entering this I found all dark and drear; and a little distance before me there was a light burning, around which was gathered a group of hags hideous beyond all expression. But these I scarcely noticed; for there amid them, all pale and wan, with her face now lighted up with joyous and eager expectation, I saw my darling﻿—my Almah! I caught her in my arms, and for a few moments neither of us spoke a word. She sobbed upon my breast, but I knew that the tears which she shed were tears of joy. Nor was our joy checked by the thought that it was to be so short-lived. It was enough at that moment that we saw one another﻿—enough that we were in one another’s arms; and so we mingled our tears, and shared one common rapture. And sweet it was﻿—sweet beyond all expression﻿—the sweetest moment in all my life; for it had come in the midst of the drear desolation of my heart and the black despair. It was like a flash of lightning in the intense darkness, short and sudden indeed, yet still intense while it lasted, and in an instant filling all with its glow.

===Chinese===
这古怪的消息令我心绪大为激荡。分离仪式这种事，我本不情愿；可是在它后头，却升起再见阿尔玛一面的前景。我深信这是她想出的法子，借以同我通一通消息，或者至少再见我一见。唯有对莱拉的念头碍着这份确信，因为说到底这也可能是她的作为；然而我要见到阿尔玛了，这是改不动的事实，为此我大大地欢喜，欢欣无以复加。

约定的那个约姆到了。赤贫者们排成一列仪仗。首领没有来，因为除非大献祭和米斯塔·科塞克，他从不离开洞窟。牢门开了，我随队而行。一路皆是黑暗，穿过许多条通道，末了来到一座洞窟门前，同我离开的那座一般阴森。进得窟来，但见四下黑暗凄凉；前面不远处有一盏灯燃着，灯的周围聚着一群老妪，丑陋得言语无从形容。可这些人我几乎不曾看见；因为在她们中间，脸色苍白憔悴、面庞正被欢欣急切的期待照亮着的，我望见了我的爱人——我的阿尔玛！我把她搂进怀里，有好一会儿，我们谁也没有说一句话。她伏在我胸前啜泣，可我知道她流的泪是欢喜的泪。我们的欢喜也不曾因想到它如此短促而稍减。此刻我们彼此得见，已经够了——彼此相拥在怀，已经够了；于是我们的眼泪流在一处，共尝同一场狂喜。那真是甜美——甜美得言语无从形容——我一生最甜美的时刻；因为它来临在我心中凄凉荒芜、漆黑绝望的正中央。它像沉沉黑暗里的一道闪电，固然短促而倏忽，可在它存续之际依然炽烈，霎时间便以它的光辉照彻了一切。

===Original===
“I did this,” murmured Almah, “to see you and to save you.”

“Save me!” I repeated.

“Yes,” said she. “I have seen Layelah. She told me that there is this chance and this one only to save you. I determined to try it. I cannot bear to think of you at the sacrifice﻿—and for love of me meeting your death﻿—for I would die to save you, Atam-or.”

I pressed her closer in my arms.

“Oh, Almah,” said I, “I would die to save you! and if this ceremony will save you I will go through with it, and accept my fate whatever it may be.”

We were now interrupted.

===Chinese===
“这件事是我做的，”阿尔玛低声说，“为的是见你，为的是救你。”

“救我！”我重复道。

“是啊，”她说，“我见到莱拉了。她告诉我，救你的机会唯有这一个，再无别路。我决意试上一试。一想到你在献祭上——为了对我的爱而赴死——我就受不住；因为为救你，我是甘愿赴死的，阿塔莫尔。”

我把她搂得更紧了些。

“唉，阿尔玛，”我说，“为救你，我也是甘愿赴死的！这仪式若能救你，我便把它行完，无论等着我的命运是什么，我都领受。”

这时我们被人打断了。

===Original===
The women﻿—the hags of horror﻿—the shriek-like ones, as I may call them, or the fiend-like, the female fiends, the foul ones﻿—they were all around us; and one there was who looked so exactly like the nightmare hag of the outer sea that I felt sure she must be the same, who by some strange chance had come here. Such, indeed, is quite likely, for there may have been a pass over the mountains to the land of the Kosekin; and those savage cannibals may all have been honored Kosekin exiles, dwelling in poverty, want, woe, and darkness, all of which may have been allotted to them as a reward for eminent virtues. And so here she was, the nightmare hag, and I saw that she recognized me.

A circle was now formed around us, and the light stood in the middle. The nightmare hag also stood within the circle on the other side of the light opposite us. The beams of the lamp flickered through the darkness, faintly illuminating the faces of the horrible creatures around, who, foul and repulsive as harpies, seemed like unclean beasts, ready to make us their prey. Their glances seemed to menace death; their blear eyes rested upon us with a horrid eager hunger. My worst fears at that moment seemed realized; for I saw that Almah’s associates were worse than mine, and her fate had been more bitter. And I wondered how it had been possible for her to live among such associates; or, even though she had lived thus far, whether it would be possible for her to endure it longer.

===Chinese===
这班女人——骇人的老妪——倘若容我这样称呼，也可叫她们尖声怪啸的一类，或曰魔魅似的东西、母魔、腌臜的一伙——已把我们团团围住；其中有一个，同外海那个梦魇老妪像得一模一样，我断定必是同一个，不知经了什么奇缘来到此地。这也实在大有可能，因为山里说不定有一条通路直抵科塞金人的国土；而那班凶蛮的食人生番，说不定全都是荣显的科塞金流人，居在贫穷、匮乏、苦难与黑暗之中，而这一切，或许正是颁赏给他们，以酬其殊勋懿德的。于是她就在这里，这梦魇老妪；我看出来，她也认出了我。

我们四周这时围成一个圆圈，灯立在正中。梦魇老妪也在圈内，立在灯的那一侧，正与我们相对。灯光在黑暗里摇曳，昏昏地照出四周那些可怕生灵的脸；她们腌臜可憎，宛如鸟身女妖（harpy），又似不洁的野兽，随时要把我们当作口中之食。她们的目光像在以死相胁；她们那烂涩的眼睛盯在我们身上，带着一种骇人的、饥渴的贪婪。我最大的疑惧此刻仿佛坐实了；因为我看出，阿尔玛的同伴比我的更坏，她的命分比我更苦。我不明白，她怎么可能同这样的同伴相处至今；或者，纵然相处到了今日，再往后她可还撑持得下去。

===Original===
And now there arose a melancholy chant from the old hags around﻿—a dreadful strain, that sounded like a funeral dirge, sung in shrill, discordant voices, led by the nightmare hag, who as she sang waved in her hand a kind of club. All the time I held Almah in my arms, regardless of those around us, thinking only of her from whom I must soon again be separated, and whom I must leave in this drear abode to meet her fearful fate alone. The chant continued for some time, and as long as it continued it was sweet to me; for it prolonged the meeting with Almah, and postponed by so much our separation.

At length the chant ceased. The nightmare hag looked fixedly at us, and spoke these words:

“You have embraced for the last time. Henceforth there is no more sorrow in your love. You may be happy now in being forever disunited, and in knowing the bliss of eternal separation. As darkness is better than light, as death is better than life, so may you find separation better than union.”

===Chinese===
这时，四周的老妪们起了哀哀的诵唱——一支骇人的调子，听去像一首葬仪的挽歌，用尖利乖刺的嗓音唱出，领唱的正是梦魇老妪，她一面唱，一面挥着手里一根棍棒似的东西。我始终把阿尔玛搂在怀里，全然不顾周围那些人，心里只有她——我不久便要再度与她分离的她，我必须撇在这凄凉居处、独自去面对可怖命运的她。诵唱持续了好一阵，只要它不停，于我便是甘美的；因为它延长了与阿尔玛的相会，也就把我们的分离推迟了这么久。

末了诵唱止息。梦魇老妪定定地望着我们，说出下面这些话：

“你们最后一回的相拥，已经拥过了。从此你们的爱情里再没有哀伤。如今你们可以快活了——永远地离散，并且确知永恒分离的至福。黑暗既胜于光明，死亡既胜于生命，愿你们也由此悟得：分离胜于结合。”

===Original===
She now gave a blow with her club at the lamp, which broke it to atoms and extinguished the flame. She continued:

“As the baleful light is succeeded by the blessed darkness, so may you find the light of union followed by the blessed darkness of separation.”

And now in the deep darkness we stood clasped in one another’s arms; while around us, from the horrible circle of hags, there arose another chant as harsh and discordant as the previous one, but which, nevertheless, like that, served at least to keep us together a little longer. For this reason it sounded sweeter than the sweetest music; and therefore, when at last the hideous noise ended, I felt a pang of grief, for I knew that I must now give up Almah forever.

I was right. The ceremony was over. We had to part, and we parted with tears of despair. I was led away, and as I went I heard Almah’s sobs. I broke away, and tried to return for one more embrace; but in the darkness I could not find her, and could only hear her sobs at a greater distance, which showed that she too was being led away. I called after her,

===Chinese===
说着她抡起棍棒朝灯打去，把灯击得粉碎，火焰应声而灭。她接着说道：

“凶险的光既被蒙福的黑暗接替，愿你们也见结合之光随之熄灭，没入分离的蒙福黑暗。”

于是，在深深的黑暗中，我们相拥而立；四周，从老妪们那可怕的一圈里，又起了另一支诵唱，同前一支一样粗厉乖刺；可是它同前一支一样，至少还能教我们多厮守片刻。为此，它听来竟比最甜美的音乐还甜美；也因此，当那可憎的聒噪终于停歇时，我心头一阵剧痛，因为我知道，如今我必须永远舍掉阿尔玛了。

我所料不差。仪式完了。我们必须分手，我们便含着绝望的泪分了手。我被人带走，走时听得见阿尔玛的呜咽。我挣脱出来，想回去再拥她一抱；可是黑暗里寻她不见，只听见她的呜咽已在更远处，可见她也在被人带走了。我在身后呼喊她，

===Original===
“Farewell, Almah!”

Her reply came back broken with sobs.

“Farewell forever, Atam-or!”

I was once more led away, and again traversed the dark passages, and again came back to my den, which now seemed dark with the blackness of despair.

===Chinese===
“别了，阿尔玛！”

她回答的声音传来，泣不成声。

“永别了，阿塔莫尔！”

我再度被带走，重新穿过那些黑暗的通道，回到我的巢穴；此刻它似乎黑得同绝望一个颜色了。

===Original===
On my return I was formally and solemnly congratulated by all the paupers. I should not have received their congratulations had I not expected that there would be something more. I expected that something would be said about the result of this act of separation; for Almah had believed that it would be the means of saving my life, and I believed that it would be the means of saving her life, and for this reason each of us had performed our part; although, of course, the joy of meeting with one another would of itself have been sufficient, and more than sufficient, to make that ceremony an object of desire. I thought, therefore, that some statement might now be made to the effect that by means of this ceremony my status among the Kosekin would be changed, and that both I and Almah, being no longer lovers, would be no longer fit for the sacrifice. To my intense disappointment, however, nothing whatever was said that had the remotest reference to this.

On the following *jom* I determined to ask the Chief Pauper himself directly; and accordingly, after a brief preamble, I put the question point-blank:

“Will our ceremony of separation make any difference as to our sacrifice?”

===Chinese===
我一回来，众赤贫者便郑重其事、一本正经地向我道贺。若不是我指望着还有下文，他们的道贺我原是消受不起的。我指望他们总会就这桩分离之举的结果交代些什么；因为阿尔玛原信这是救我性命的门径，我也信这是救她性命的门径，我们两个才各自尽了本分；固然，单是彼此相见的欢喜，本身便已尽够——且不止尽够——教这仪式成为心愿所系。因此我想，此刻总该有话说明，大意是说：经此仪式，我在科塞金人中间的身份已然更改，我与阿尔玛既是情人不再，便也不再相宜于献祭了。然而令我极度失望的是，无论什么话都没有说，连沾着一点边儿的都没有。

到了下一个约姆，我决计直接去问首席赤贫者本人；于是略作了几句引子之后，我便开门见山地问道：

“我们的分离仪式，会不会对我们的献祭有什么影响？”

===Original===
“What?” he asked, with a puzzled expression.

I repeated the question.

“I don’t understand,” said he, still looking puzzled.

Upon this I once more repeated it.

“How can that be?” said he at length; “how can the ceremony of separation have any effect upon your sacrifice? The ceremony of separation stands by itself as the sign and symbol of an additional blessing. This new happiness of separation is a great favor, and will make you the object of new envy and admiration; for few have been so fortunate as you in all the history of the Kosekin. But you are the favorite of the Kosekin now, and there is nothing that they will not do for you.”

===Chinese===
“什么？”他问，一脸茫然。

我把问题重复了一遍。

“我不明白。”他说，仍是满脸茫然。

于是我又重复了一遍。

“那怎么可能呢？”他终于说道；“分离仪式怎么能对你们的献祭有什么影响？分离仪式自成一事，是一项额外福分的表记与象征。这份分离的新幸福是莫大的恩宠，将使你成为新的艳羡与钦仰之的；因为在科塞金人的全部历史上，像你这般有福的寥寥无几。如今你是科塞金人的宠儿，只要是他们力所能及的，没有一样不肯为你做。”

===Original===
“But we were separate before,” said I, indignantly.

“That is true,” said he, “in point of fact; but this ceremony makes your separation a legal thing, and gives it the solemn sanction of law and of religion. Among the Kosekin one cannot be considered as a separate man until the ceremony of separation has been publicly performed.”

“I understood,” said I, “that we were chosen to suffer the sacrifice together because we were lovers, and now since you do not any longer regard us as lovers, why do you sacrifice us?”

At this question the Chief Pauper looked at me with one of those hungry glances of his, which showed how he thirsted for my blood, and he smiled the smile of an evil fiend.

===Chinese===
“可是我们本来就分开着呀。”我愤然说。

“那是实情，”他说，“就事实而言是如此；可这仪式使你们的分离成为合法之事，赋予它法律与宗教的庄严认可。在科塞金人中间，非到分离仪式公开行过，一个人不得算是已分离的人。”

“我原以为，”我说，“我们是因为是情人，才被拣选同受献祭的；如今你们既不再把我们看作情人了，为什么还要献祭我们？”

听了这一问，首席赤贫者拿他那饿狼般的一眼望着我——那一眼流露出他如何渴着我的血——并且咧开嘴，笑出一个邪恶魔鬼的笑。

===Original===
“Why do we sacrifice you, Atam-or?” he replied. “Why, because we honor you both, and love you both so dearly that we are eager to give you the greatest of all blessings, and to deny you nothing that is in our power to bestow.”

“Do you mean to sacrifice both of us?” I gasped.

“Of course.”

“What! Almah too?”

“Certainly. Why should we be so cruel to the dear child as to deprive her of so great a boon?”

At this I groaned aloud and turned away in despair.

===Chinese===
“我们为什么献祭你们，阿塔莫尔？”他答道，“嗨，因为我们敬重你们两位，深爱你们两位，爱得急切，恨不得把至大的福分赐给你们，只要我们力所能及，没有一样舍不得给的。”

“你是说，我们两个都要献祭？”我倒抽一口气。

“当然。”

“什么！阿尔玛也是？”

“自然是。这样亲爱的一个孩子，我们何忍那样狠心，剥夺她这么大的恩惠呢？”

听得这话，我不禁出声呻吟，绝望地掉过身去。

===Original===
Many *joms* now passed away. I grew more and more melancholy and desperate. I thought sometimes of fighting my way out. My firearms were now my chief consolation; for I had fully made up my mind not to die quietly like a slaughtered calf, but to strike a blow for life, and meet my death amid slain enemies. In this prospect I found some satisfaction, and death was robbed of some of its terrors.

===Chinese===
许多个约姆就这样过去了。我愈来愈忧郁，愈来愈绝望。有时我想着杀出一条血路。我的火器如今成了我主要的慰藉；因为我已拿定主意，绝不像一头待宰的牛犊那样安安静静地死，而要为性命奋力一搏，在杀倒的敌人中间迎接我的死亡。在这前景里我寻得几分快意，死亡也因此被夺去了几分恐怖。

## XXX The Day of Sacrifice / 三十、献祭之日

===Original===
At last the time came.

It was the end of the dark season. Then, as the sun rises for its permanent course around the heavens, when the long day of six months begins, all in the land of the Kosekin is sorrow, and the last of the loved darkness is mourned over amid the most solemn ceremonies, and celebrated with the most imposing sacrifices. Then the most honored in all the land are publicly presented with the blessing of death, and allowed to depart this hated life, and go to the realms of that eternal darkness which they love so well. It is the greatest of sacrifices, and is followed by the greatest of feasts. Thus the busy season﻿—the loved season of darkness﻿—ends, and the long, hateful season of light begins, when the Kosekin lurk in caverns, and live in this way in the presence of what may be called artificial darkness.

It was for us﻿—for me and for Almah﻿—the day of doom. Since the ceremony of separation I had not seen her; but my heart had been always with her. I did not even know whether she was alive or not, but believed that she must be; for I thought that if she had died I should have heard of it, as the Kosekin would have rejoiced greatly over such an event. For every death is to them an occasion of joy, and the death of one so distinguished and so beloved as Almah would have given rise to nothing less than a national festival.

Of time I had but a poor reckoning; but, from the way in which the paupers kept account of their *joms*, I judged that about three months had elapsed since the ceremony of separation.
===Chinese===
时辰终于到了。

那是黑暗季的尽头。每逢此时，太阳升起，开始它绕天长行的恒定航程，长达六个月的昼日由此开端，科塞金全境尽是哀伤，他们挚爱的黑暗将尽，人们在最肃穆的典礼中为它举哀，又以最盛大的献祭为它饯行。于是举国最受尊崇的人们当众受赐死亡的福分，获准辞别这可憎的生命，前往他们深爱之至的永恒黑暗之境。这是至大的献祭，献祭之后继以至大的筵席。繁忙的季节——那备受热爱的黑暗季——就此告终，漫长而可憎的光明季就此开端；光明季里，科塞金人蛰伏洞窟之中，就这样生活在一种不妨称作人造黑暗的东西跟前。

于我们——于我和阿尔玛——而言，这是大限之日。自分离仪式之后，我再没见过她；可我的心始终系在她身上。我甚至不知道她是死是活，只相信她必定还活着；因为我想，她若死了，我总该有所耳闻，科塞金人必会为这样一桩事大大欢庆。须知他们的每一桩死亡都是喜事，而像阿尔玛这样尊贵、这样受人爱戴的人若死，那简直要酿成一场举国大典了。

说到时日，我素来计算得糊里糊涂；不过从赤贫者们记他们约姆的方式来看，我推算自分离仪式以来，大约已经过去三个月了。

===Original===
The paupers were now all joyous with a hideous joy. The Chief Pauper was more abhorrent than ever. He had the blood-thirst strong upon him. He was on that *jom* to perform his horrible office of *Sar Tabakin*, and as he accosted me he smiled the smile of a demon, and congratulated me on my coming escape from life. To this I had no word of answer to make; but my hands held my rifle and pistol, and these I clutched with a firmer grasp as my last hour approached.

The time of departure at length arrived. Soldiers of the Kosekin came, following the paupers, who went first, while the guards came after me. Thus we all emerged into the open air. There the broad terrace already mentioned spread out before my eyes, filled with thousands upon thousands of human beings. It seemed as though the entire population of the city was there, and so densely packed was this great crowd that it was only with great difficulty that a way was laid open for our passage.

Above was the sky, where the stars were twinkling faintly. There was no longer the light of the Aurora Australis; the constellations glimmered but dimly, the moon was shining with but a feeble ray; for there far away over the icy crests of the lofty mountains I saw a long line of splendid effulgence, all golden and red﻿—the light of the new dawn﻿—the dawn of that long day which was now approaching. The sight of that dawning light gave me new life. It was like a sight of home﻿—the blessed dawn, the sunlight of a bright day, the glorious daybreak lost for so long a time, but now at last returning. I feasted my eyes on the spectacle, I burst into tears of joy, and I felt as though I could gaze at it forever. But the sun as it travelled was rapidly coming into view; soon the dazzling glory of its rim would appear above the mountain crest, and the season of darkness would end. There was no time to wait, and the guards hurried me on.
===Chinese===
这时赤贫者们个个满怀欢喜，那是一种狰狞的欢喜。首席赤贫者比以往任何时候都更可憎。他嗜血的渴念正炽。就在那个约姆，他要履行萨尔·塔巴金的可怕职司；他上前同我搭话时，脸上堆着恶魔的狞笑，庆贺我行将脱出人生。对此我无一语可答；只是我双手握着步枪和手枪，随着末限步步临近，把它们攥得愈来愈紧。

动身的时刻终于到了。科塞金的士兵来了，跟在赤贫者后面，赤贫者先行，卫兵则押在我身后。我们就这样一齐来到露天之下。先前说起过的那片开阔梯台在眼前铺展，上面挤满了千千万万的人。仿佛全城的人丁都在那里了，人潮稠密之至，费了好大的劲，才为我们开出一条通路。

头顶是天穹，星辰淡淡闪烁。南极光已不复见；诸星座只余微芒，月亮也只洒下一线弱光；因为远处，在高山冰封的峰脊之上，我望见一带灿烂的光华，金红交辉——那是新曙光的光——那正日益临近的长昼的曙光。望见那道渐显的天光，我顿时有了新的生命。那宛如望见了家园——有福的黎明，晴日的阳光，那久已失落的壮丽破晓，如今终于归来。我把这景象饱看不足，喜极而迸出泪水，只觉得自己可以永远这样望下去。可是太阳一路运行，正迅速进入视野；不多时，它那轮炫目的光弧便要跃上峰脊，黑暗季便要终了。没有等待的余暇，卫兵催我前行。

===Original===
There in the midst of the square rose the pyramid. It was fully a hundred feet in height, with a broad flat top. At the base I saw a great crowd of paupers. Through these we passed, and as we did so a horrible death-chant arose. We now went up the steps and reached the top. It was about sixty feet square, and upon it there was a quadrangle of stones set about three feet apart, about sixty in number, while in the midst was a larger stone. All of these were evidently intended for sacrificial purposes.

Scarcely had I reached the top when I saw a procession ascend from the other side. First came some paupers, then some hags, and then, followed by other hags, I saw Almah. I was transfixed at the sight. A thrill passed through every nerve, and a wild impulse came to me to burst through the crowd, join her, and battle with them all for her life. But the crowd was too dense. I could only stand and look at her, and mark the paleness of her face and her mute despair. She saw me, waved her hand sadly, and gave a mournful smile. There we stood separated by the crowd, with our eyes fastened on each other, and all our hearts filled with one deep, intense yearning to fly to one another’s side.

And now there came up from below, louder and deeper, the awful death-chant. Time was pressing. The preparations were made. The Chief Pauper took his station by the central stone, and in his right hand he held a long, keen knife. Toward this stone I was led. The Chief Pauper then looked with his blear and blinking eyes to where the dawn was glowing over the mountain crest, and every moment increasing in brightness; and then, after a brief survey, he turned and whetted his knife on the sacrificial stone. After this he turned to me with his evil face, with the glare of a horrid death-hunger in his ravenous eyes, and pointed to the stone.
===Chinese===
广场正中矗立着那座献祭金字塔。它足有一百英尺高，顶上是宽阔的平坛。塔基下我望见一大群赤贫者。我们从他们中间穿过，穿行之际，一阵可怖的死亡诵唱腾起。我们随即拾级而上，到达坛顶。顶面约六十英尺见方，上面有一圈四四方方排列的石块，彼此相距约三英尺，总数约六十块，正中则是一块更大的石头。这一切显然都是供献祭之用。

我刚到坛顶，便望见一队人从另一侧登上来。先是几名赤贫者，随后是几个老妪，再后，在另外几个老妪的跟随下，我看见了阿尔玛。这光景把我惊得呆立当场。一阵战栗窜过每一根神经，一股狂野的冲动攫住我，要冲开人群，奔到她身边，为保她的性命同他们全体拼一死战。可人太稠密了。我只能立在那里望着她，端详她脸上的苍白、她无言的绝望。她看见了我，哀哀地挥了挥手，凄然一笑。我们就这样被人群隔开，伫立相望，四目相锁，两颗心同怀着一种深沉炽烈的渴望——恨不得飞到彼此身边。

这时，那可怖的死亡诵唱又从下面升起，愈响愈沉。时间紧迫。诸事已然齐备。首席赤贫者在正中那块石头旁站定，右手握一柄修长的利刃。我被引向那块石头。首席赤贫者随即眯起那双眵昏眨动的眼睛，望向峰脊上方愈燃愈亮的曙光；匆匆一瞥之后，他转过身，在献祭石上磨刀。磨罢，他向我转过那张凶恶的脸，饕餮的双眼里闪着可怖的死亡饥渴的光，朝那块石头指了指。
===Original===
I stood without motion.

He repeated the gesture and said, “Lie down here.”

“I will not,” said I.

“But it is on this stone,” said he, “that you are to get the blessing of death.”

“I’ll die first!” said I, fiercely, and I raised my rifle.
===Chinese===
我伫立不动。

他又比了一遍手势，说道：“躺到这里来。”

“我不躺。”我说。

“可正是在这块石头上，”他说，“你将领受死亡的福分。”

“先杀了我！”我声色俱厉地说，端起了步枪。

===Original===
The Chief Pauper was puzzled at this. The others looked on quietly, thinking it probably a debate about some punctilio. Suddenly he seemed struck with an idea.

“Yes, yes,” said he. “The woman first. It is better so.”

Saying this he walked toward Almah, and said something to the hags.

At this the chief of them﻿—namely, the nightmare hag﻿—led Almah to the nearest stone, and motioned to her to lie down. Almah prepared to obey, but paused a moment to throw at me one last glance and wave her hand as a last farewell. Then without a word she laid herself down upon the stone. At this a thrill of fury rushed through all my being, rousing me from my stupor, impelling me to action, filling my brain with madness. The nightmare hag had already raised her long keen knife in the air. Another moment and the blow would have fallen. But my rifle was at my shoulder; my aim was deadly. The report rang out like thunder. A wild, piercing yell followed, and when the smoke cleared away the nightmare hag lay dead at the foot of the altar. I was already there, having burst through the astonished crowd, and Almah was in my arms; and holding her thus for a moment, I put myself in front of her and stood at bay, with my only thought that of defending her to the last and selling my life as dearly as possible.
===Chinese===
首席赤贫者对此大惑不解。其余的人静观不动，只道这多半是在争执什么礼数细节。

“是了，是了，”他说，“先来女的。这样更好。”

说着他便向阿尔玛走去，对那些老妪吩咐了几句。

于是众妪之首——正是那个梦魇老妪——把阿尔玛领到最近的一块石头前，示意她躺下。阿尔玛正要俯首听命，却停了一停，朝我投来最后一瞥，挥手作最后的诀别。随后她一言不发，在石上躺下了。见此情景，一阵狂怒贯穿我的全人，把我从麻木中惊醒，驱策我行动，把我的头脑灌满疯狂。梦魇老妪已把她那柄修长的利刃高高举在半空。再过一瞬，那一刀便要落下。可我的步枪已抵在肩头；我瞄得致命。枪声如雷炸响。随之而起的是一声狂厉刺耳的尖叫，待硝烟散尽，梦魇老妪已经死在祭坛脚下。而我早已冲过惊愕的人群抢到那里，阿尔玛已在我怀中；我把她这样抱了片刻，随即掩在她身前，像被逼到尽头的困兽般挺立拒守，心中只有一个念头：护卫她到最后一刻，拼着自己一死，也要叫对方付出最昂贵的代价。

===Original===
The result was amazing.

After the report there was for some moments a deep silence, which was followed by a wild, abrupt outcry from half a million people﻿—the roar of indistinguishable words bursting forth from the lips of all that throng, whose accumulated volume arose in one vast thunderclap of sound, pealing forth, echoing along the terraced streets, and rolling on far away in endless reverberations. It was like the roar of mighty cataracts, like the sound of many waters; and at the voice of that vast multitude I shrank back for a moment. As I did so I looked down, and beheld a scene as appalling as the sound that had overawed me. In all that countless throng of human beings there was not one who was not in motion; and all were pressing forward toward the pyramid as to a common centre. On every side there was a multitudinous sea of upturned faces, extending as far as the eye could reach. All were in violent agitation, as though all were possessed by one common impulse which forced them toward me. At such a sight I thought of nothing else than that I was the object of their wrath, and that they were all with one common fury rushing toward me to wreak vengeance upon me and upon Almah for the slaughter of the nightmare hag.

All this was the work of but a few moments. And now as I stood there holding Almah﻿—appalled, despairing, yet resolute and calm﻿—I became aware of a more imminent danger. On the top of the pyramid, at the report of the rifle, all had fallen down flat on their faces, and it was over them that I had rushed to Almah’s side. But these now began to rise, and the hags took up the corpse of the dead, and the paupers swarmed around with cries of “*Mut! mut!*” (dead! dead!) and exclamations of wonder. Then they all turned their foul and bleary eyes toward me, and stood as if transfixed with astonishment. At length there burst forth from the crowd one who sought to get at me. It was the Chief Pauper. He still held in his hand the long knife of sacrifice. He said not a word, but rushed straight at me, and as he came I saw murder in his look. I did not wait for him, but raising my rifle, discharged the second barrel full in his face. He fell down a shattered, blackened heap, dead.
===Chinese===
其结果令人惊愕无比。

枪响之后，先是片刻深沉的寂静，随后五十万民众爆发出一阵狂乱而骤起的呼号——那整片人海从千万张口中迸出辨不清字句的咆哮，积聚起来，汇成一声浩大的霹雳，訇然震发，沿着一层层梯台街滚出回声，又远远滚荡开去，余音不绝。那像大瀑布的怒吼，像众水的轰鸣；那一大群人的声浪令我一时畏缩。畏缩间我朝下一望，目睹了一幅同那震慑我的声浪同样可怖的景象。在那数不清的人海之中，没有一个人不在动；所有人都朝金字塔挤来，仿佛朝着一个共同的中心。四面八方都是仰起的面孔汇成的茫茫人海，一直铺到目力尽头。人人都在剧烈的骚动里，仿佛全被同一个冲动附了体，这冲动驱赶他们向我涌来。眼见这幅光景，我只想到一件事：我便是他们怒火所集的目标，他们正怀着同一种狂怒向我扑来，要为梦魇老妪的被杀，向我、也向阿尔玛复仇泄恨。

这一切都不过是一瞬之间的事。此时我抱着阿尔玛立在那里——惊骇、绝望，却又决绝而镇定——又察觉到一重更为迫近的危险。步枪一响，坛顶上的人全都扑倒，脸贴了地，我正是从他们身上冲过去，到了阿尔玛身边。可这时他们渐渐爬起来了，老妪们抬起那具尸首，赤贫者们围拢过来，喊着“穆特！穆特！”（Mut！mut！——死了！死了！），连声惊叹。随后他们一齐把那双双污浊昏花的眼睛转向我，呆立在那里，仿佛惊得僵住了。终于，人群中猛冲出一个非要挨近我不可的人。正是首席赤贫者。他手里仍握着那柄修长的献祭之刀。他一言不发，径直朝我扑来；他来势之中，我看见他眼里的杀机。我没有等他，端起步枪，把第二筒的弹药整个轰在他脸上。他倒下去，成了一堆血肉狼藉、焦黑的东西，死了。
===Original===
As the second report thundered out it drowned all other sounds, and was again followed by an awful silence. I looked around. Those on the pyramid﻿—paupers and hags﻿—had again flung themselves on their faces. On the square below the whole multitude were on their knees, with their heads bowed down low. The silence was more oppressive than before; it was appalling﻿—it was tremendous! It seemed like the dread silence that precedes the more awful outburst of the hurricane when the storm is gathering up all its strength to burst with accumulated fury upon its doomed victim.

But there was no time to be lost in staring, and that interval was occupied by me in hastily reloading my rifle. It was my last resource now; and if it availed not for defence it might at least serve to be used against ourselves. With this thought I handed the pistol to Almah, and hurriedly whispered to her that if I were killed, she could use it against herself. She took it in silence, but I read in her face her invincible resolve.

The storm at last burst. The immense multitude rose to their feet, and with one common impulse came pressing on from every side toward the pyramid, apparently filled with the one universal desire of reaching me﻿—a desire which was now all the more intense and vehement from these interruptions which had taken place. Why they had fallen on their knees, why the paupers on the pyramid were still prostrate, I could not tell; but I saw now the swarming multitude, and I felt that they were rolling in on every side﻿—merciless, bloodthirsty, implacable﻿—to tear me to pieces. Yet time passed and they did not reach me, for an obstacle was interposed. The pyramid had smooth sides. The stairways that led up to the summit were narrow, and did not admit of more than two at a time; yet, had the Kosekin been like other people, the summit of the pyramid would soon have been swarming with them; but as they were Kosekin, none came up to the top; for at the base of the pyramid, at the bottom of the steps, I saw a strange and incredible struggle. It was not, as with us, who should go up first, but who should go up last; each tried to make his neighbor go before him. All were eager to go, but the Kosekin self-denial, self-sacrifice, and love for the good of others made each one intensely desirous to make others go up. This resulted in a furious struggle, in which, as fast as anyone would be pushed up the steps a little way, he would jump down again and turn his efforts toward putting up others; and thus all the energies of the people were worn out in useless and unavailing efforts﻿—in a struggle to which, from the very nature of the case, there could be no end.
===Chinese===
第二声枪响雷鸣般炸开，淹没了其余一切声响，随后又是一阵可怕的寂静。我环顾四周。坛上的人——赤贫者与老妪们——又扑倒脸贴了地。下方广场上，整个人群跪伏在地，头颅深深低垂。这寂静比先前更加压人；它骇人——它惊心动魄！它像是飓风更可怕的爆发之前那种令人胆寒的静默——风暴正蓄足全部力量，要以积聚的狂怒，向那命定的牺牲者猛扑下去。

可没有工夫呆望了，那段间歇我用来匆匆给步枪重新装弹。如今它是我最后的凭借了；倘若无济于卫护，至少还可以拿来对付我们自己。怀着这个念头，我把手枪递给阿尔玛，急急对她耳语：我若被杀，她可以拿它对自己用。她默默接过，但我从她脸上读出了她不可摧折的决意。

风暴终于爆发了。浩大的人群站起身来，怀着同一个冲动，从四面八方朝金字塔挤压过来，显然满怀着唯一一个遍及全体的欲望——要挨到我身边；这欲望经过方才的种种阻滞，此刻愈发炽烈暴烈。他们为何跪下，坛上的赤贫者为何仍扑伏在地，我说不上来；但我眼看那蜂拥的人海，感到他们正从四面滚滚压来——无情、嗜血、不共戴天——要把我撕成碎片。然而时间过去，他们却没能挨近我，因为一道障碍横在了中间。金字塔四壁光滑。通到坛顶的阶梯狭窄，每次容不下两人并行；然而，科塞金人若像别处的人一样，坛顶早该挤满他们了；可他们偏是科塞金人，便没有一个人上得顶来；因为在塔基之下、阶梯脚下，我目睹了一场奇特得令人难以置信的争斗。争的不是像我们那边那样谁先上去，而是谁最后上去；人人设法让邻人走在自己前头。个个都急切想上去，可科塞金人的克己、舍己和乐他人之福的心肠，又使每个人都热切地想让别人上去。于是酿成一场狂暴的争斗：谁只要被推上阶梯几级，便立刻跳下来，掉转身去使劲把别人往上送；就这样，众人的全部气力都耗在徒劳无功的奔忙里——耗在这场就其本性而言永无了局的争斗里。

===Original===
Now those on the pyramid began to rise, and soon all were on their feet. Cries burst forth from them. All were looking at us, but with nothing like hostility; it was rather like reverence and adoration, and these feelings were expressed unmistakably in their cries, among which I could plainly distinguish such words as these: “*Ap Ram!*” “*Mosel anan wacosek!*” “*Sopet Mut!*” (The Father of Thunder! Ruler of Cloud and Darkness! Judge of Death!) These cries passed to those below. The struggle ceased. All stood and joined in the cry, which was taken up by those nearest, and soon passed among all those myriads, to be repeated with thunder echoes far and wide.

At this it suddenly became plain to me that the danger of death had passed away; that these people no longer regarded me as a victim, but rather as some mighty being﻿—some superior, perhaps supernatural power, who was to be almost worshipped. Hence these prostrations, these words, these cries, these looks. All these told me that the bitterness of death had passed away. At this discovery there was, for a moment, a feeling of aversion and horror within me at filling such a position; that I, a weak mortal, should dare to receive adoration like this; and I recoiled at the thought: yet this feeling soon passed; for life was at stake﻿—not my own merely, but that of Almah; and I was ready now to go through anything if only I might save her: so, instead of shrinking from this new part, I eagerly seized upon it, and at once determined to take advantage of the popular superstition to the utmost.
===Chinese===
这时坛上的人渐渐起身，不多时全都站起来了。呼喊从他们中间迸发出来。人人都望着我们，却绝无半点敌意；那册宁像是敬畏与崇拜，这些情愫在呼喊中表露无遗；在那些呼喊里，我能清楚分辨出这样一些字眼：“阿普·拉姆！”（Ap Ram，雷霆之父！）“莫塞尔·阿南·瓦科塞克！”（Mosel anan wacosek，云与黑暗之主！）“索佩特·穆特！”（Sopet Mut，死亡之判官！）这些呼喊传给了下面的人。争斗停止了。所有人站着加入呼喊，最近的先接上，随即传遍那万千大众，带着雷鸣般的回响，远远近近反复震荡。

这一来我恍然大悟：死亡的危局已经过去了；这些人不再拿我当祭品，而是把我当作某种威严的存在——某种高超的、或许超自然的力量，几乎要加以崇拜了。因此才有这些匍匐，这些言辞，这些呼喊，这些目光。这一切都告诉我，死亡的苦味已经过去了。悟出这一点时，我心中一度生出反感与恐怖，恶心于身居这样一个位置——我，一个软弱的凡人，竟敢领受这样的崇拜；这念头令我畏缩。可这感觉随即消散；因为性命交关——不只是我自己的，更是阿尔玛的；如今只要能救她，无论什么我都甘愿做去：于是我非但不规避这个新角色，反而热切地一把抓住它，当即打定主意，要把民众的迷信利用到极处。
===Original===
Far away over the crests of the mountains I saw the golden edge of the sun’s disc, and the light flowed therefrom in broad effulgence, throwing out long rays of glory in a luminous flood over all the land. I pointed to the glorious orb, and cried to the paupers, and to all who were nearest, in a loud voice:

“I am Atam-or, the Man of Light! I come from the land of light! I am the Father of Thunder, of Cloud and Darkness; the Judge of Death!”

At this the paupers all fell prostrate, and cried out to me to give them the blessing of death.
===Chinese===
远处，在群山峰脊之上，我望见了太阳那轮金色的边沿，光明从那里沛然流泻，放出长长的荣耀光芒，如一道明亮的光涛漫过整个国土。我指着那轮辉煌的天体，向赤贫者们、向所有离得最近的人高声喊道：

“我是阿塔莫尔，光明之人！我来自光明之地！我是雷霆之父，云与黑暗之主；是死亡之判官！”

这话一出，赤贫者们尽数拜伏在地，喊叫着求我把死亡的福分赐给他们。

===Original===
I made no answer, but leading Almah to the edge of the pyramid, told her to fire the pistol. A million eyes were fixed on us. She held up the pistol and fired. Immediately after, I fired both barrels of the rifle; and as the reports rang out and the smoke cleared away, I heard a mighty murmur, and once more beheld all prostrate. Upon this I hurriedly loaded again, and waited for further revelations. All the time I could not help wondering at the effect produced by the rifle now, in comparison with the indifference with which it had been regarded at my first arrival in the country. I could not account for it, but supposed that the excitement of a great religious festival and the sudden death of the Chief Pauper and the Chief Hag had probably deeply impressed them. In the midst of these thoughts the whole multitude arose; and once more there came to my ears the universal uproar of innumerable cries, in the midst of which I could hear the words, “*Ap Ram!*” “*Mosel anan wacosek!*” “*Sopet Mut!*”
===Chinese===
我不作回答，只把阿尔玛领到金字塔的坛边，叫她开枪。百万双眼睛注视着我们。她举起手枪，开了火。紧接着，我把步枪两筒齐发；枪声鸣响、硝烟散尽之际，我听见一阵宏大的嗡喃，又见众人尽皆拜伏。于是我急忙再度装弹，静候进一步的启示。这中间我始终不免暗暗称奇：当初我初到此地之时，他们对这步枪何等漠然处之，如今它所生的效验却一至于此。我解释不来，只能猜想，盛大宗教节典的亢奋，加上首席赤贫者与首席老妪的暴死，多半给了他们极深的震动。正自思量，整个人群站了起来；又一次，无数呼喊汇成的鼎沸声浪传入我耳中，其中我能听见那些字眼：“阿普·拉姆！”“莫塞尔·阿南·瓦科塞克！”“索佩特·穆特！”

## XXXI Conclusion / 三十一、结语

===Original===
In the midst of this the paupers and the hags talked earnestly together. Some of those who had been nearest in rank to the late Chief Pauper and Chief Hag were conspicuous in the debate. All looked at me and at Almah, and pointed toward the sun, which was wheeling along behind the distant mountain crest, showing a golden disc. Then they pointed to the dead bodies; and the hags took the Chief Hag, and the paupers the Chief Pauper, and laid them side by side on the central altar. After this a hag and a pauper advanced toward us, each carrying the sacrificial knife which had belonged to the deceased.

The hag spoke first, addressing Almah, in accordance with the Kosekin custom, which requires women to take the precedence in many things.

“Take this,” she said, “O Almah, consort of Atam-or, and Co-ruler of Clouds and Darkness. Henceforth you shall be Judge of Death to the women of the Kosekin.”

===Chinese===
正当此时，赤贫者们与老妪们凑在一处热切商议。方才品级最接近已故首席赤贫者与首席老妪的几个人，在议论中格外惹眼。众人都望着我与阿尔玛，又指指太阳——太阳正沿着远处山脊的背后滚动，露出金盘一轮。接着他们又指指那两具尸身；于是老妪们抬起首席老妪，赤贫者们抬起首席赤贫者，把两人并排安放在中央的祭坛上。此后，一名老妪与一名赤贫者向我们走来，各捧一把献祭刀，都是死者生前之物。

那老妪先行开口，向阿尔玛致辞——这是循着科塞金的习俗；他们的规矩，许多事情上都须女子占先。

“这刀你收下，”她说，“哦，阿尔玛，阿塔莫尔之配、云与黑暗之共主。从今以后，你便是科塞金众女子的死亡之判官（Judge of Death）。”

===Original===
She then handed Almah the sacrificial knife of the Chief Hag, which Almah took in silence.

Then the pauper presented me with the sacrificial knife of the Chief Pauper, with the following words:

“Take this, O Atam-or, Father of Thunder and Ruler of Clouds and Darkness. Henceforth you shall be Judge of Death to the men of the Kosekin, and *Sar Tabakin* over the whole nation.”

I received the knife in silence, for I had nothing to say; but now Almah spoke, as was fitting for her to do, since with the Kosekin the women must take the precedence; and here it was expected that she should reply in behalf of both of us.

===Chinese===
说罢，她把首席老妪的献祭刀递到阿尔玛手中，阿尔玛默然接了。

随后，那赤贫者也把首席赤贫者的献祭刀呈给我，致辞如下：

“这刀你收下，哦，阿塔莫尔，雷霆之父，云与黑暗之主。从今以后，你将是科塞金众男子的死亡之判官，并任全族的萨尔·塔巴金。”

我默然接刀，因为我无话可说；但这时阿尔玛开口了——这也合乎她的身份，因为在科塞金人中间，女子凡事必须占先；此刻众人也正指望她代表我们两人作答。

===Original===
So Almah, holding the sacrificial knife, stood looking at them, full of dignity, and spoke as follows:

“We will take this, O Kosekin, and we will reward you all. We will begin our reign over the Kosekin with memorable acts of mercy. These two great victims shall be enough for the Mista Kosek of this season. The victims designed for this sacrifice shall have to deny themselves the blessing of death, yet they shall be rewarded in other ways; and all the land from the highest to the lowest shall have reason to rejoice in our rule.

“To all you hags and paupers we grant the splendid and unparalleled boon of exile to Magones. There you can have all the suffering which heart can wish, and inevitable death. To all classes and ranks in the whole nation we promise to grant a diminution in their wealth by one-quarter. In the abundance of our mercy we are willing ourselves to bear the burden of all the offerings that may be necessary in order to accomplish this. All in the land may at once give up one-quarter of their whole wealth to us.”

At this the hags and paupers gave a horrible yell of applause.

===Chinese===
于是阿尔玛手持献祭刀，仪态庄严地环视众人，开口说道：

“这刀我们收下了，哦，科塞金人，我们也要赏赐你们全体。我们要以种种难忘的仁慈之举，开启对科塞金人的统治。今季的米斯塔·科塞克，有这两位大祭牲便尽够了。原定为这场献祭备下的祭牲，将不得不自行舍却死亡的福分，但他们必以别的方式受赏；而全国上下，从至高到至低，都将有缘由为我们的统治而欢欣。

“至于你们众老妪与众赤贫者，我们赐你们流放马戈内斯之恩——一份辉煌无两、旷古未有的福赐。在那边，你们尽可以领受心所能愿的一切苦难，还有那必至无疑的死亡。至于全国各阶级、各品级，我们应许将众人的财富削减四分之一。我等仁慈浩荡，甘愿亲自承担为此所需的一切供献的重负。举国之人，即可将全部财富的四分之一献纳于我们。”

此言一出，老妪们与赤贫者们迸出一声骇人的欢呼。

===Original===
“As rulers of Light and Darkness, we will henceforth govern the nation in the light as well as in the dark. We will sacrifice ourselves so far to the public good as to live in the light, and in open palaces. We will consent to undergo the pains of light and splendor, to endure all the evils of luxury, magnificence, and boundless wealth, for the good of the Kosekin nation. We will consent to forego the right of separation, and agree to live together, even though we love one another. Above all, we will refuse death and consent to live. Can any rulers do more than this for the good of their people?”

Another outburst of applause followed.

“In three *joms*,” continued Almah, “all you hags and paupers shall be sent to exile and death on Magones. As for the rest of the Kosekin, hear our words. Tell them from us that the laborers shall all be elevated to the rank of paupers, the artisans shall be made laborers, the tradesmen artisans, the soldiers tradesmen, the Athons soldiers, the Kohens Athons, and the Meleks Kohens. There shall be no Meleks in all the land. We, in our love for the Kosekin, will henceforth be the only Meleks. Then all the misery of that low station will rest on us; and in our low estate as Meleks we shall govern this nation in love and self-denial. Tell them that we will forego the sacrifice and consent to live; that we will give up darkness and cavern gloom and live in light. Tell them to prepare for us the splendid palaces of the Meleks, for we will take the most sumptuous and magnificent of them all. Tell all the people to present their offerings. Tell them that we consent to have endless retinues of servants, soldiers, followers, and attendants. Tell them that with the advent of Almah and Atam-or a new era begins for the Kosekin, in which every man may be as poor as he likes, and riches shall be unknown in the land.”

===Chinese===
“身为光明与黑暗的统治者，从今以后，我们要在光明中治理这国家，一如在黑暗中。为了公众的福祉，我们不惜自我牺牲到这般地步：要住在光明里，住在敞亮的宫殿中。我们甘愿领受光明与辉煌的痛楚，甘愿忍受奢华、壮丽与无涯财富的诸般祸害，只为科塞金民族的好处。我们甘愿放弃分离的权利，应允同居共处——纵然我们彼此相爱。尤其是，我们要谢绝死亡，甘愿活下去。天下的统治者，还能为子民的福祉做得比这更多么？”

接着又是一阵欢呼迸发。

“三个约姆之后，”阿尔玛继续道，“你们众老妪与众赤贫者，都将被遣往马戈内斯，去就流放与死亡。至于其余的科塞金人，且听我们传谕。你们替我们告诉他们：劳作者将尽皆擢升为赤贫者；匠人为劳作者；店铺商人为匠人；兵卒为店铺商人；阿通为兵卒；科恩为阿通；美勒克为科恩。全国境内，将不复有美勒克。我们，出于对科塞金人的爱，从此将是唯一的美勒克。那卑下地位的万般凄惨，便都落在我们身上；我们既居美勒克的卑位，就要以爱与克己治理这个国家。告诉他们，我们愿舍却献祭、甘愿存活；我们要抛开黑暗与洞窟的幽冥，住在光明里。告诉他们，为我们备下美勒克的华丽宫殿，因为我们要拣最豪奢、最壮丽的一座。告诉全体民众，把他们的供献呈上来。告诉他们，我们甘愿拥有仆役、兵卒、侍从与随员的无尽扈从。告诉他们，随着阿尔玛与阿塔莫尔的降临，科塞金人的新纪元开始了——从此人人都可以随心所欲地贫穷，而财富将在这片土地上湮没无闻。”

===Original===
These extraordinary words seemed to fill the paupers with rapture. Exclamations of joy burst from them; they prostrated themselves in an irrepressible impulse of grateful admiration, as though such promises could only come from superior beings. Then most of them hurried down to communicate to the people below the glorious intelligence. Soon it spread from mouth to mouth, and all the people were filled with the wildest excitement.

For never before had such a thing been known, and never had such self-sacrifice been imagined or thought possible, as that the rulers of the Kosekin could consent to be rich when they might be paupers; to live together when they might be separate; to dwell in the light when they might lurk in the deepest cavern gloom; to remain in life when they might have the blessing of death. Selfishness, fear of death, love of riches, and love of luxury, these were all unintelligible to the Kosekin, as much as to us would be self-abnegation, contempt of death, voluntary poverty, and asceticism. But as with us self-denying rulers may make others rich and be popular for this, so here among the Kosekin a selfish ruler might be popular by making others poor. Hence the words of Almah, as they were made known, gave rise to the wildest excitement and enthusiasm, and the vast multitude poured forth their feelings in long shouts of rapturous applause.

Amid this the bodies of the dead were carried down from the pyramid, and were taken to the Mista Kosek in a long and solemn procession, accompanied by the singing of wild and dismal chants.

And now the sun, rolling along behind the icy mountain crest, rose higher and higher every moment, and the bright light of a long day began to illumine the world. There sparkled the sea, rising far away like a watery wall, with the horizon high up in the sky; there rose the circle of giant mountains, sweeping away till they were blended with the horizon; there rose the terraces of the *amir*, all glowing in the sunlight, with all its countless houses and cavern-openings and arching trees and pointing pyramids. Above was the canopy of heaven, no longer black, no longer studded with stars or glistening with the fitful shimmer of the aurora, but all radiant with the glorious sunlight, and disclosing all the splendors of the infinite blue. At that sight a thrill of joy passed through me. The long, long night at last was over; the darkness had passed away like some hideous dream; the day was here﻿—the long day that was to know no shadow and no decline﻿—when all this world should be illuminated by the ever-circling sun﻿—a sun that would never set until his long course of many months should be fully run. My heart swelled with rapture, my eyes filled with tears. “O Light!” I cried; “O gleaming, golden Sunlight! O Light of Heaven!﻿—light that brings life and hope to man!” And I could have fallen on my knees and worshipped that rising sun.

===Chinese===
这些匪夷所思的话，似乎叫赤贫者们心醉神迷。欢呼声从他们中间迸发出来；他们伏身下拜，感恩戴德的冲动按捺不住，仿佛这般许诺只可能出自超凡的生灵。随后他们大多急急奔下金字塔，把这辉煌的喜讯传告下方的大众。消息口口相传，须臾之间，万民尽皆陷入最狂热的激奋。

因为这样的事亘古未曾有过，这样的自我牺牲也从不曾有人想象过、或认为真有可能：科塞金的统治者，明明可以做赤贫者，竟甘愿身为富人；明明可以分离，竟甘愿同居；明明可以潜藏于最深的洞窟幽冥之中，竟甘愿栖身于光明；明明可以领受死亡的福分，竟甘愿留在人间。自私、怕死、贪财、恋奢，这些在科塞金人全然不可索解，恰如克己忘我、轻生蔑死、自愿受穷、苦行禁欲，在我们也是全然不可索解。然而，正如在我们这里，忘我克己的统治者能叫旁人致富、并以此深得人心；在科塞金人那里，自私的统治者也能叫旁人致贫、并以此大得民心。于是阿尔玛的话一经传开，便激起最狂热的震荡与热情，芸芸万众将胸中的情感一泻而出，化作经久不息、如醉如狂的欢呼。

在这片声浪中，两具尸身被从金字塔上抬了下来，由一支绵长而肃穆的队伍送往米斯塔·科塞克，一路伴着粗野阴惨的诵唱。

这时，太阳沿着冰封山脊的背后滚动，一刻高过一刻，漫长白昼的明亮光辉开始照亮这个世界。远处海面波光潋滟，像一堵水墙般远远高涌，地平线悬在天际高处；环形巨峰拔地而起，绵延开去，直到与地平线融为一体；阿米尔的层层梯台尽沐阳光，灼灼生辉——连同那数不清的屋舍、通向洞窟的门户、交拱成荫的树木与尖耸入云的金字塔。头顶是天穹，不再漆黑，不再缀满星辰，不再闪着南极光明灭不定的微芒，而是通体辉耀着灿烂的阳光，展露出无穷碧色的万千壮丽。一见这景象，一阵欢喜的震颤流遍我的全身。漫漫的长夜终于熬到了头；黑暗像一场狰狞的噩梦般消散了；白昼已至——那漫长的、不知阴影、不知衰减的白昼——整个世界都将被这环行不辍的太阳照亮——一个不跑完数月长程决不沉落的太阳。我的心中涨满狂喜，眼中噙满泪水。“哦，光明！”我呼喊道，“哦，闪烁的金色阳光！哦，天国的光明！——给人类带来生命与希望的光啊！”我几乎要跪倒在地，去膜拜那初升的太阳。

===Original===
But the light which was so glorious to us was painful and distressing to the Kosekin. On the top of the pyramid the paupers crouched, shading their eyes. The crowd below began to disperse in all directions, so as to betake themselves to their coverts and to the caverns, where they might live in the dark. Soon nearly all were gone except the paupers at the foot of the pyramid, who were awaiting our commands, and a crowd of Meleks and Athons at a distance. At a gesture from me the few paupers near us descended and joined those below.

Almah and I were alone on the top of the pyramid.

I caught her in my arms in a rapture of joy. This revulsion from the lowest despair﻿—from darkness and from death back to hope and light and life﻿—was almost too much to endure. We both wept, but our tears were those of happiness.

“You will be all my own now,” said I, “and we can fly from this hateful land. We can be united﻿—we can be married﻿—here before we start﻿—and you will not be cruel enough to refuse. You will consent, will you not, to be my wife before we fly from the Kosekin?”

===Chinese===
然而，这于我们如此辉煌的光明，对科塞金人却是痛苦与煎熬。金字塔顶上，赤贫者们蜷缩起身子，遮护着眼睛。下方的人群开始四散而去，各自躲进荫蔽处与洞窟，好生活在黑暗里。不久，人们几乎走尽，只剩金字塔脚下候着我们差遣的赤贫者们，以及远处一小群美勒克与阿通。我做了个手势，身旁几名赤贫者便下塔去，与下面的人会合。

金字塔顶上，只剩我与阿尔玛两人。

我在狂喜中把她拥进怀里。这从绝望渊底的大逆转——从黑暗与死亡，折回希望、光明与生命——几乎叫人承受不住。我们两人都落了泪，但那是幸福的泪水。

“如今你完完全全是我的人了，”我说，“我们可以飞离这可憎的国度。我们可以结合——我们可以成婚——就在这儿，趁还没动身——你总不至于狠心拒绝。在我们飞离科塞金人之前，你肯做我的妻子，肯不肯？”

===Original===
At this Almah’s face became suffused with smiles and blushes. Her arms were about me, and she did not draw away, but looked up in sweet confusion and said,

“Why, as to that﻿—I﻿—I cannot be more your﻿—your wife than I am.”

“What do you mean?” I exclaimed, in wonder. “My wife!”

Her eyes dropped again, and she whispered:

===Chinese===
听了这话，阿尔玛脸上漾满笑意与红晕。她的手臂环着我，并不抽回，只带着甜美的羞乱仰起脸来，说道：

“哎呀，说到这个——我——我不能比现在更算是你的——你的妻子了。”

“你这是什么意思？”我惊呼道，“我的妻子！”

她的眼帘又垂了下去，低声道：

===Original===
“The ceremony of separation is with the Kosekin the most sacred form of marriage. It is the religious form; the other is merely the civil form.”

This was unintelligible, nor did I try to understand it. It was enough to hear this from her own sweet lips; but it was a strange feeling, and I think I am the only man since Adam that ever was married without knowing it.

“As to flight,” continued Almah, who had quite adopted the Kosekin fashion, which makes women take the lead﻿—“as to flight, we need not hurry. We are all-powerful now, and there is no more danger. We must wait until we send embassies to my people, and when they are ready to receive us, we will go. But now let us leave this, for our servants are waiting for us, and the light is distressing to them. Let us go to the nearest of our palaces and obtain rest and food.”

===Chinese===
“分离仪式，在科塞金人那里正是最神圣的婚姻形式。那是宗教的形式；另一种，不过是世俗的形式罢了。”

这话无从索解，我也不想去弄明白。单是从她自己那甜蜜的唇间听到这一句，于我已是足够；但这感觉委实奇妙，而且我想，自亚当以来，结了婚而不自知的男人，怕是唯我一个。

“至于出逃，”阿尔玛继续道——她已全然改从科塞金的风气，凡事由女子领头——“至于出逃，我们尽可从容。如今我们大权在握，再没有危险了。我们须先遣使团到我的族人那里去，等他们准备停当、迎候我们，我们就动身。不过眼下且撇开这个吧，我们的仆役还候着呢，这光亮也叫他们难受。我们且去最近的宫殿，歇息歇息，用些饮食。”

---

===Original===
Here Featherstone stopped, yawned, and laid down the manuscript.

“That’s enough for today,” said he; “I’m tired, and can’t read any more. It’s time for supper.”

===Chinese===
读到这里，费瑟斯通停住了，打个哈欠，放下了手稿。

“今天就读到这儿，”他说，“我累了，再也读不动了。该吃晚饭了。”
